**2026-09-13 — the maintainer reversed "never a gap": a picture can be shown WHOLE, with black bars, and mirrored** (*"option to flip media on x and/or y … possibility to have black bars, so we can see the whole media"*). `Framing` gained `flipX`, `flipY` and `fit: 'cover' | 'contain'` (**v18**, the block at the END of `migrateTripDoc`, every stored framing landing unmirrored on cover). The gap is now only ever ASKED for: under `contain` scale 1 is the scale that shows the whole rotated picture, the pan runs along the FRAME's axes and clamps the rotated picture's bounding box inside the frame (exact at any angle — the picture-axis clamp let a turned picture's corners slide out, caught by a test), and `drawFramed` paints the frame `#000` first, because the video pipeline reuses one canvas and an unpainted bar shows the previous frame. The mirror is stored in the picture's axes (applied innermost, before the rotation), and the UI never toggles it raw: `flipFraming` mirrors what the FRAME shows by also negating the rotation and the pan along that axis (M·R(θ) = R(−θ)·M), so "Horizontal" on a quarter-turned picture still flips left ↔ right on screen. **How to apply**: switching fit re-centres at scale 1 (a crop's zoom means nothing once bars are allowed); Reset keeps the chosen fit; the fit is per PICTURE like the rest of the framing, never a trip or piece default. Verified with a real H.264 hook clip in the Browser pane: bars black, mirror burned in.

**One transform, three renderers.** `drawFramed` is called by `renderBadge` (preview and PNG deck) and by `exportVariantVideo` (the burned-in hook clip), which gained an optional `framing` defaulting to the centred cover — so the Studio's own exports are untouched. `coverRect` was deleted: it had no caller left, and a second copy of this maths is exactly how a preview and an export start disagreeing. The framing does NOT cross the Studio bridge — a reel from a linked project is framed there, over that project's footage — and the Picture tab says so when a project is linked.

**Gesture (rev. 2026-09-22).** Drag the picture where no element sits (the badge keeps first claim on a press — a hook is composed far more often than it is reframed), the wheel, a trackpad pinch or two fingers to zoom it **about the point under the hand** — `zoomFramingAbout` writes `x`/`y` with `scale`, so what is aimed at stays aimed at; it used to touch `scale` alone and grow the picture about the frame's centre — a sideways wheel or a pinch's drift to pan while the framing has slack, a Fill/Whole switch, sliders, ⟲/⟳ 90° and the two flips in the Picture tab, "Reset" back to centred inside the chosen fit. Per CELL on a collage: the cell under the hand is the one reframed, at its own size. The reading of the hand is the suite's one machine (`shared/ui/use-zoom-gestures.ts`, `frontend.md` «Two uses, one hand») on the BOX the canvas fills, which is also what refuses WebKit's own pinch there — the stage had no pinch at all before, and no `blockNativeZoom`. The badge's own React pointer handlers keep the single pointer and stand down while two fingers are on (`pinching`). A wheel burst or a whole pinch is ONE undo step (the history's 700 ms coalescing), measured; and the stage keeps the framing it last wrote for 300 ms so a pinch's pan is not overwritten by its zoom while the document lags.

**Trap, measured.** `migrateTripDoc`'s blocks run in SOURCE order, not version order — the badge itself is only built by the v2 block and slides arrive with v5. A v12 block placed at the top wrote `{ framing }` over a badge that did not exist yet and left every other field undefined, on a v1 document. Anything filling a field goes at the END of that function, and there is a regression test for it.

**Trap, measured (2026-09-15): a renumber on merge does not renumber the documents a branch already STAMPED.** The Itinerary branch took v19 for its Route conversion while main took v19 for the car; the maintainer ran that branch locally (he tests PR branches on his own dev server), so his real trips were stamped v19 with no `car`, main's `version < 19` block then never ran on them, and ⚙ Trip → Car / House style threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'model' / 'color')`. The fix is a REPAIR migration (v21: `readVehicleSpec` again, which keeps a real car and defaults a missing one), never an edit to the old block — it would not re-run. **How to apply**: when a merge renumbers a branch's migration, assume the maintainer's IndexedDB AND his Winnow copies already carry the branch's number, and ask whether main's block at that number is idempotent enough to run again under the next version; if it fills a field, add that repair in the same merge.

## A picture moves in its frame: the motion IS the framing over time, placed at the needle (2026-09-23)

**Decision, the maintainer's** (*"go with D, edit at the needle"*, over the *Banc-titre* lab, https://claude.ai/artifact/9RbuzCoxcgfmkGuig2asKx). A pan-and-zoom (a Ken Burns) is NOT an opener: every real opener declares `owns: 'frame'` and paints AFTER the picture, with no access to a clip's frame and its pictures graded without the slide's develop — an opener could not move a clip, a carousel picture or a cell, and could not combine with the Itinerary. So the motion is a property of each PICTURE (`motion: FramingMotion | null` on `PostBadge`, `PostSlide` and `CollageCell`, **v28**), evaluated as a framing at `t` (`shared/media/framing-motion.ts`, pure).

**The model, and why each rule.** Keys are the frames BEFORE the rest (`{at, scale, x, y}`, `at` in [0,1) of the span); the rest is `framing` itself at 1 — never two copies, and every surface that draws a slide settled (PNG deck, rail, grid thumbnail) draws `framing` and knew nothing. A key holds pan and zoom only; rotation, mirror and fit are read from the rest (a flip mirrors every key through `flipMotion`, the `flipFraming` rule). Zoom is geometric and `pan / scale` travels linearly in `1 / scale`: that keeps a point both frames share STILL on screen under both fits and any rotation — pinned by a spec through `zoomFramingAbout`. Nothing clamps in the module: `framingTransform` clamps at draw, so an overshooting curve (Back, Spring) never shows an edge. A pause is two equal keys; explicit instants mean no curve inverse is needed, so all of `shared/motion/easing.ts` applies. `start: 'after-opener'` waits `ResolvedHook.seconds` (Défilé covers the frame while it sweeps).

**Editing at the needle — the rules that make it one gesture.** The stage is handed each picture's framing AS SHOWN at the needle (`stageFraming`, `stageCollage` in `PostEditor`) and draws what it is given; its gestures start from that frame and ONE writer (`placeFraming(i, next)`, also behind the Picture tab's rows) routes the write through `placeAtNeedle`: pan/zoom to the frame the needle is on (snap `KEY_SNAP_SECONDS` = 0.15 s), a new key where it is on none, rotation/flip/fit to the rest. Held, a key within snap is SHOWN as itself (`framingAtNeedle`), or the write would jump the picture on landing. **Paused on the slide's first moment the stage shows the COMPOSITION** — the rest, exactly as the badge settles there — so the hook thumbnail (taken only there) stays the rest with no change to its capture, and a gesture there edits the rest; the first frame is reached a hair in (`NEEDLE_NUDGE_SECONDS` 0.04). A gesture on a playing slide pauses it first. The band marks every placed frame (`slideMotionMarks`, lead + drawn cells). The section is **Pan & zoom** (the collage's *Motion* section already owns that word); it says what the next gesture writes.

**Exports.** `renderBadge` takes a `MotionClock` (motion + screen seconds + opener seconds); a collage's cells move only under `CollageRender.clock`; settled surfaces hand none. `exportVariantVideo` gained `framingAt`, asked every frame with the same delivered clock its painters read. The still delivery check reads `deepestFraming` — a slide that starts at ×2 over a proxy needs the pixels of its closest frame.

**Verified in the Browser pane** (dev server on its own port, a 3000×2000 PNG whose pixels encode their position): the composition shows the rest and First the ×1.15 starter on the same centre; a wheel at the first frame rewrote key 0 only with the point under the pointer held (0.435 vs 0.437); a drag mid-slide placed a key at `at` 0.508; three marks on the band; playback went 0.09 → 0.133 → rest; flip negated every key; Remove took the middle key; the plan said `.mp4`; the painted hook clip decoded 0.090 / 0.133 / rest. The CLIP path (`exportHookVideo` on an in-page H.264 of two black bars) framed all 60 frames at the expected zoom, worst 0.003.

**Quick moves (2026-09-23, his "build the gesture presets too").** `applyPreset` writes TWO frames — a start key and the rest — over the picture as composed, replacing every key and keeping the easing, steps and start; the needle refines them after. A PAN travels the REAL slack edge to edge at the composed zoom (`framingTransform`'s slack in the axes the pan is stored in, so a rotated picture pans along its horizon and a `contain` one between its bars), and is REFUSED under 2 % of the long edge rather than offered as a creep. A PUSH ends on the composition when it can start `PRESET_ZOOM` (1.6) wider; a composition at its widest is pushed IN on instead, the rest brought closer about the frame's middle. A PULL starts 1.6 closer and ends on the composition untouched. Names are the camera's (`pan-right` = the view travels right: a positive pan moves the PICTURE right, so it goes + → −). The room is measured against each picture's decoded SHAPE, which the stage now reports (`onPictureSizes`, lead then cells, keyed by the slide in `PostEditor` so a slide just opened never measures against the last one's); only shapes matter, so the stage's bounded pixels serve. **A disabled button shows no tooltip in Chrome**, so the reason is written UNDER the grid, one line per reason naming the buttons it holds back — the "never a silent refusal" rule. Verified in the Browser pane: Pan ↑ ↓ greyed with their reason on a 3:2 in 9:16, Pan → from source x 0 to 1, Push in at the widest → rest ×1.6, Pull out → start ×2.56.

**The tour (2026-09-23, his "build the tour mode too").** A tour is NOT stored: it is written as ordinary keys (`tourMotion`: the first stop at 0, a pause as two equal frames, the glides sharing what the pauses leave by how far each travels on screen, the last stop the REST) and READ BACK out of any motion (`tourOf` / `tourFrames`: one stop per run of equal frames, the zoom the rest's, the first pause found) — so the map opens on frames placed at the needle or by a quick move as readily as on a tour, and nothing can drift between a recipe and the keys. A stop is where the view really CENTRES at that zoom (`framingOn` → `panBy`, which clamps), so a tap the framing cannot centre on is drawn where it lands, never where it was tapped: a 3:2 picture at ×1 in a 9:16 frame fills the height, and every stop sits at y 0.5 until the zoom is raised. One stop is no motion at all, so a PAUSE has nowhere to live with one stop — the slider is disabled with that sentence, not left to be silently dropped (the first build lost it). «Previous / next» step through ARRIVALS (`arrivalMarks`), never the end of a pause, which shows the same frame again. The map (`TourMap`, in the Pan & zoom section) is the whole picture as an `<img>`/`<video>` with each stop's window drawn as four corners (`framingWindow`, turned with the picture), as wide as the section: at a field's control width (201 px) the dots were 5 px and a drag grabbed nothing but made a new stop — the grab reaches at least 12 px. `setPointerCapture` is wrapped: it throws for a pointer the browser no longer tracks, which killed the handler before the write. Verified in the Browser pane: three stops at ×2 with a 0.4 s pause wrote five keys at 0, 0.08, 0.247, 0.327, 0.92 of a 5 s slide; First showed stop 1 and the composition the last; › stepped 0.25 → 0.30 → 0.75.

**Not built, deliberately or awaiting him:** the collage's `inside` entrance still translates the picture OVER the framing (and by reading uncovers a band of the cell's ground while it fades) — re-expressing it on `framingAt` changes existing collages' look, his call (question 3 of the lab, unanswered); continuity between a reel's slides; the Studio's stills have no framing to move yet.

**Reported the next day as not intuitive, and the EDITING is redesigned — the storage is not (2026-09-24).** His words: *«cliquer sur Moves, zoomer, se déplacer sur la timeline, se redéplacer… pour que les keyframes soient pris en compte. Ce n'est pas toujours intuitif»*, and the ask: elegant, comfortable on a phone, a timeline one can see. The diagnosis is six rules that each have a reason and together make an interface one deduces (`docs/picture-motion-ui.md` §2): the stage shows the COMPOSITION at 0 (the arrival, before the departure); a gesture places a frame silently past 0.15 s from one, said only in an inspector row that a phone draws in a sheet OVER the stage; the band's marks are 6 px on a 150 px cell; nine rows for one idea; no playback in the section; the tab is a veiled sheet where Develop's rule asks for a drawer. **Three faces designed, one storage** (the lab, three working phones: https://claude.ai/artifact/CUaBnL4XxsHYsh8zHVCvG9): **A · Cards** — a frame is a thumbnail card in a row (*Départ*, stops, *Arrivée*), a tap selects it and the gesture writes THAT card, `+` adds a copy after it, time shared by distance with a pause per stop (`tourMotion`'s arithmetic at a zoom per stop); **B · Lane** — today's needle with a lane, named diamonds one drags to retime, and a chip on the stage saying what the next gesture writes, with a switch trying «a gesture between two frames places one / does nothing»; **C · Map** — `TourMap` as the main surface, a window per stop at its own zoom, pinch tightens the selected one, the output as a picture-in-picture. **Recommended: A as the face on every screen, the band keeping its marks (a draggable diamond later), C behind «Visite» in a sheet — with four rule changes**: the stage shows the SELECTED card and the thumbnail is captured on *Arrivée* explicitly (revises «paused on the first moment the stage shows the composition» above — the reason kept, the means changed); a gesture never places a frame in silence (revises the «at the needle» half of his D on the phone; the wide screen is his question 1); what a gesture writes is a CHIP on the stage, not an inspector row; the Image tab is a `drawer` on the compact shell. Nothing built; six commits and four questions are in the brief. **How to apply**: build from the brief's commit list, starting with the pure `motion-cards.ts`; do not re-propose a needle-first editor for the phone, and do not touch v28 — every face writes the same keys.

**Built as cards (2026-09-24, his «a»; §6.1–6.3 of the brief).** `shared/media/motion-cards.ts` (pure, tested) reads any motion as its cards (`tourFrames` + the first pause as the hold) and writes a row back with the tour's arithmetic at a zoom per card, a doubling of the zoom counting for a pan across 35 % of the frame (`ZOOM_TRAVEL`); `writeCard` reframes ONE card's run of keys at the instants they have, so a nudge never retimes the others; only `insertCard`, `removeCard` and the pause re-share the time. **Trap**: a card added as an exact COPY reads back as a pause (two equal frames are one card) and vanished from the row — so `+ Stop` is the copy pushed ×1.08 closer (`INSERT_ZOOM`, out when at ×8), and the chip says it. **The editor's rules** (`PostEditor`): `cardPick` is `'end'` when a slide or a cell opens — the composition, kept as a WORD so a card added before it does not shift it, and what keeps the hook thumbnail captured on open (`composedView` now also asks for `'end'`); a tap on a card sends the needle to the card's arrival (`arrivalMarks`); a scrub of the band or a pause of playback FOLLOWS the needle (`cardAtNeedle`, the old 0.15 s snap) onto a card or `'between'`; paused, the stage shows the picked card, or the instant while between; a gesture writes the picked card through `writeCard`, an unselected cell's composition, and while between REFUSES and says so for 1.8 s in the stage's new `caption` chip (`BadgeStage`, top-left, `on-media` ink, accent when it refuses); a quick move writes Start and End, picks Start and PLAYS. The row (`panels/MotionCards.tsx`) draws every card through `drawFramed` from ONE bitmap decoded within 0.5 MP (`loadBadgeSource` + `boundSource`, a clip through its seeked element), ungraded on purpose; a still picture is one card with a dashed Start ghost. **Trap, his first report (2026-09-24): that bitmap was released in the decode effect's CLEANUP, so when the piece moved on to its next slide — new cards and a new file in one commit — the paint effect of the same commit drew a closed `ImageBitmap` («the image source is detached») and the tool crashed. It is released one commit AFTER the state lets go of it (an effect keyed on the source), the stage's own rule (`studio.md`), and a closed bitmap (width 0) draws nothing rather than throwing.** Reproduced and re-checked headless on a two-slide carousel played through its pan. **Second trap, his next report («the images are black»): the picture's measured shape (`onPictureSizes`) was keyed by the SLIDE, and the stage reports only when a decode lands — so a slide showing the SAME picture as the one before it (a carousel's second slide, played into) never got a report: its cards drew black and its quick moves said «still being read» for good. The sizes are keyed by the FILES they were measured from now (same files, same shapes, whichever slide), and the row's thumbnail source needs only the frame's shape (`frameBox`), never the stage's report; a new file's cards stay dark until it decodes rather than drawing the last file's picture.** The section keeps the quick moves, the tour's map (unchanged, still one zoom for all stops) and the curve; the Placing / Needle / Frames rows and `NEEDLE_NUDGE_SECONDS` are gone; the band keeps its 6 px marks. **His four questions were answered by the brief's own recommendations for want of his**: the placing gesture is dropped everywhere; the cards live in the section and the band is unchanged; a zoom per stop waits for the tour step; the ×1.15 starter stays. **Verified headless** (Playwright over the dev server, a 2000 × 1500 position-encoded PNG dropped through a patched `HTMLInputElement.prototype.click`, a trip seeded with `putTrip`): the ghost Start zooms ×1.15 on the same point (left-edge R 0.212 → 0.251), `+ Stop` writes keys `[0, ×1.15], [0.265, ×1.242]`, a drag moves Stop 2 and only it (centre R 0.502 → 0.459), End shows the composition again, a pause at 0.8 s reads «Between Start and Stop 2 · 0.8 s» and a drag there changes nothing and says «tap a card». **And on the phone the Picture tab is a DRAWER (§6.4, the same day)**: `PanelHost compactAs` is `'drawer'` for that tab alone — what it sets is judged on the picture above it, so a sheet's wash over that picture would lie (Develop's rule, `frontend.md`) — while Content, Look and Export keep the sheet they are picked from; the stage column drops its `pb-3` while the drawer is up, since the drawer is then what sits above the bar. Driven by touch at 390 × 844 through CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent`: the stage stays uncovered, a one-finger drag on it moves Stop 2 (centre R 0.502 → 0.376) and the pause / refuse path reads the same as on the wide screen.

**And the map is on the cards too (§6.5, the same day)**: `TourMap` (kept in `panels/`) draws every card as the window it shows and a dot where it looks (`stopOf`, now exported), one selection with the row; a tap APPENDS a card looking there at the picked card's zoom, which becomes the End — the map's own rule, kept, since a tour visits its stops in order and rests on the last —, a dot dragged moves that card at its own zoom through `writeCard` (its instants kept, like a gesture on the stage), and a pinch (two pointers), a trackpad or the wheel zooms the PICKED card about the point it looks at, as does the Zoom slider under the map — a zoom per card, which `TourPlan.zoom` never had. The map's own Pause and Stop rows are gone (the section's Pause, Remove and Hold still are the same verbs); `tourOf` / `tourMotion` stay exported and tested but nothing in the editor reads them. **Traps, measured while driving it**: (1) IndexedDB lags the document (the trip autosaves on idle), so a headless check must read the UI — the caption, a slider's value, a stage pixel — never `listTrips()` a few hundred ms after a gesture, which returned the previous write and looked like a lost edit; (2) the panel above the map changes height with the pick (the «between» hint under Cards), so a map's box measured before a tap is stale after it — re-measure, and on a phone scroll it back into view, or a touch lands off-screen and does nothing. Verified wide and by touch on the phone: a tap makes a 4th card that is the End looking where tapped (clamped by the edge, x 0.70 for a 0.82 tap at ×1), a drag from Start's dot picks Start and moves it (the stage then looks at x 0.30), a wheel on the map takes Start from ×1.15 to ×1.90 on the slider.

## A slide holds SEVERAL pictures: the collage (2026-09-16)

**Decision (L1–L4 of the collage plan, the maintainer's "okay start this" over the *Collage in Motion* artifact).** `PostBadge.collage` / `PostSlide.collage` (**v24**, `shared/roadtrip/collage.ts`) name a `layout-templates.ts` id, a spacing and a background, and cells 2…n. **Cell 1 IS the slide** — its `media`/`framing`/`develop` stay where they were — so nothing that reads one picture per slide (the rail, the date read from the picture, the export plan, the Studio bridge) had to learn about it, and "One picture" hands the frame back to the lead untouched. The geometry is pure (`shared/media/media-layout.ts`: weighted tracks + CSS-grid `areas` for grid/stack/bento, insets, free prints; spacing in SHORT-SIDE fractions so 720 and 2160 cut the same cells; a non-rectangular area is refused), the paint is `shared/media/cell-paint.ts` (`drawLayout` through `drawFramed` per cell; `tile`/`roundedRect` lifted out of the Itinerary), and `renderBadge` takes `collage: { collage, items }` — decoded items lead first, each with a CALLER-owned grader, since a cell's cube is `lutFor({ ...slide, develop: cell.develop })` and sizes differ per cell. `loadCollageSources` (badge-render) is what the PNG deck and the rail use; the stage decodes cells itself, bounded by `boundSource`, keyed on the files' identities. **Rules**: a crop and a develop are per cell and never inherited; an EMPTY cell draws nothing in the export (the stage draws a slot on its chrome canvas only); a smaller template KEEPS the extra pictures (`collageKept` lists them) — no click on a tile ever drops one; `readCollage` turns an unknown template into no collage, so a newer file opens as its lead.

**The inspector, the Library and the develop sheet follow the SELECTED cell** (`PostEditor`: `selectedCell`, reset per slide; `patchCell`/`writeLead` write lead + collage in one `onChangePost`). The Library sync is given a shim slide keyed `<slideKey>#<cell>`, so stepping cells re-points the Library the way stepping slides does. **Trap, measured**: `useSlideLibrary` records the active picture onto a slide whose ref is EMPTY the moment it is opened (right for a new slide), which copied the lead into every empty cell the author stepped through. Guard: the active file at the moment a cell is selected is its `cellBaseline`, and an empty cell only takes a picture ticked AFTER that; the ticked one goes in through "Use the ticked picture". **Stage gestures** (`BadgeStage`, after elements and the opener): click selects a cell; drag pans it through `panBy` at the CELL's size, the delta turned into the cell's own axes (a print may be tilted); wheel zooms the cell under the pointer; a hold (420ms) or an Alt-drag onto another cell swaps pictures + framings + develops (never places, which belong to the slot); on a free layout a plain drag MOVES the print and Shift-drag reframes it. **Verified in the Browser pane** (dev server on its own port — 5173 is the maintainer's, with his real trips): 2×2 with B in cell 1 and C in cell 2 (zoomed ×1.49, panned −0.379), cells 3–4 empty and drawing background, all persisted at v24. Not driven: hold-to-swap, the free prints' drag. `computer` clicks there are in a 2× frame of the CSS viewport (the screenshot frame), so a coordinate read from `getBoundingClientRect` must be doubled.

**Every drawn cell is fetched back on opening, not only the selected one (2026-09-16).** The Library sync (`useSlideLibrary`) follows ONE ref — the selected cell's shim — so after a reload the other cells of a collage whose pictures live on a Winnow stayed empty slots on the stage, the rail and the exports until clicked. `use-collage-refetch.ts` asks for all of them when the slide opens (which refs: `collage-refetch.ts`, pure — connected instance, absent from the pool BY NAME, since that is what the stage's `resolve` draws with, each `assetId` once), three at a time, into the pool only: `addFiles` never moves the active asset, and nothing is written to the document. **Rules**: each picture at most once per opening (the pool is read through a ref, so one the author removes is not fetched back behind them); a new slide bumps a generation that drops late answers; a fetch is never cancelled because another cell changed. **Hand-off with `useSlideLibrary`** (its `fetchedElsewhere`): the collage hook claims synchronously in its effect, and the restore's own miss is only seen a microtask later, so no picture is asked twice; while `fetching` the restore stays UNCLAIMED (the pool changing re-runs it and activates the picture); once `failed` it CLAIMS, or the record-back gate stays shut and ticking another picture would not re-point the cell — measured: with cell 4 failed, ticking pic2 wrote it into cell 4. The report is the Picture tab's `recovery` for the selected cell, the Layout section's cell hint, and one status line for the rest. Verified in the Browser pane (port 5188, fetch stubbed, asset 4 answering 404): cells 1–3 filled with no click, cell 4 said «no longer has», refs untouched. Under `StrictMode` (dev) every picture is requested twice, as `useSlideLibrary`'s own restore is — the first answers are dropped by the generation.

**A picture is DRAGGED from the Library — a local row or an instance tile — onto the cell it belongs in (2026-09-16, rev. same day).** The maintainer's idea («ça m'évite de faire un clic…»), then his report that it «did not work»: he grabbed rows by their NAME (only the cover was draggable) and dragged from the Winnow tab (tiles were not draggable at all — his actual first ask). Now: the WHOLE row / tile / instance tile is the handle (`useAssetDragSource`, `shared/library/use-asset-drag.ts`), left behind as a dashed, faded outline one frame after the drag starts (the browser snapshots the element as `dragstart` returns). **Two channels** (`asset-drag.ts`, tested): the `DataTransfer` carries a key under our own MIME type (what lets a drop be allowed at all, and tells us from a desktop file); the DRAG ITEM lives in module memory for the drag — its label for the target to say, and `resolve()` giving the FILE: at once for a pool asset, after `picker.pick` for an instance tile (the sidebar stashes each fetch's files by asset id, since the pool regroups later). **Feedback** (`DropZones.tsx` over the canvas, copy + geometry pure in `drop-zones.ts`): armed the moment a drag starts anywhere (stage ring, hint pill, dashed cells with a quiet «+»); the hovered cell solid with «Place here · Cell 3» / «Replace · pic-D»; a SEAM between cells refuses the drop (no preventDefault → no-drop cursor); after the drop «Fetching… from <host>» with an indeterminate bar, then a `drop-settle` ring + «Placed», or a danger chip; a polite live region says each. **Traps, all measured in the Browser pane**: (1) a drop on an OCCUPIED, unselected cell did nothing — selecting the cell restarts the Library sync, which re-activated the OLD picture and wrote it back: `dropAsset` now WRITES first and selects after; (2) accept on `dragenter` as well as `dragover` — a flick released as it arrives never sees a dragover and fell back to the sidebar; (3) a drag's end is only reported to its SOURCE, and a tile disabled while its own drop is fetched lost its `onDragEnd`, leaving every target lit forever — `beginAssetDrag` also watches the page (a drop anywhere, deferred past the target's handler; the first `pointermove`), and the tile keeps its handlers while disabled; (4) `setDragImage` (ANY element, even one on screen) made the pane's synthesized drag never reach its target, so no custom ghost ships — the native snapshot of the row is the preview; (5) after an HMR edit the page can hold TWO copies of `asset-drag.ts` (the stage reads one, the sidebar writes the other): test drags after a real `location.reload()`, never a hash navigation. **Limits, deliberate**: a MOUSE gesture (on a phone the library is a sheet over the stage; the cell stepper + «Use the ticked picture» stay the touch path); Firefox will not start a drag from a `<button>` (the instance tile and the row's cover are buttons) — unmeasured, the maintainer uses Chrome.

**Motion, M1–M4 (2026-09-16).** ONE curve registry, `shared/motion/easing.ts`, is what `overlay/animation.ts`'s `easeAt` and `hooks/easing.ts` both read: the overlay's four quadratics kept their ids and values, the openers' five ids (`ease-out`…) stay as ALIASES of registry curves (`HOOK_EASING_CURVE`) with the openers' own words, so no stored option moved. New curves are additive string ids — `back` and `spring` OVERSHOOT (a scale or an offset follows them past rest, an opacity and a reveal clamp in `stepTransform`; they carry no inverse and so never reach an opener's list), `steps` moves in jumps and is the one curve that takes a number (`AnimStep.steps`, 2–12). `shared/overlay/stagger.ts` derives entrance DELAYS from boxes (order → dense ranks, ties within 2% of the short side share one, `random` seeded and the seed stored) — never written into an element. The badge's **cascade** (`PostBadge.cascade`, **v25**, `BadgeCascade = { step, stagger }`, a LOOK so `hookDefaultsFrom` carries it) replaces every piece's entrance with the one step delayed by rank; a piece's own EXIT stays; the boxes are the pieces' metrics in height units (x × aspect, width 0 — unknown without a canvas — so `columns` ties the stack and `size` ranks by height, the numeral first); `badgeSettleSeconds(styles, cascade)` answers an UPPER bound (`each × (pieces − 1) + duration`) because which pieces a badge has depends on its content, and a still taken a little late is still at rest. `hookAnimates(styles, cascade)` makes Auto deliver video. The Look tab's Cascade section reuses `StepRows` (exported from `PieceStylePanel`, `hideDelay`). Verified live: toggling the cascade flips the hook's medium to video and stores the v25 shape.

**M5 — a collage's cells arrive and leave (2026-09-16).** `SlideCollage.enter` (one step + a stagger, `inside` moving the picture behind its mask instead of the cell) and `SlideCollage.exit` (a step + `reverse`), read by `readCollage`, absent → null (no version bump: a v24/v25 document simply reads them as none). `collageCellMotions` (pure, tested) is the one clock: delays from `staggerDelays` over the RESOLVED cells, and an exit expressed as each cell's WINDOW ending earlier — `AnimStep.delay` is an entrance's alone in the engine, and `phasesFor`'s midpoint rule already covers the overlap; `reverse` is last-in-first-out. `paintCollage` hands `drawLayout` a `CellMotion` per cell and `renderBadge`'s `timeSeconds` is the clock, so the stage (deck transport), the rail (at `collageSettleSeconds`), the PNG deck (settled, no screen time → never leaves) and the painted video share one composition. `collageAnimates` joins `resolveSlideMedium` for the hook AND for a content slide, so Auto delivers a moving collage as video; a content slide now has a clock in `PostEditor` (`badgeTime` = settle at rest, `deck.local` playing). The Picture tab's Motion section (`CollageMotionSection`, over `StepRows`) offers Arrive / Leave / Mirror the entrance. **L5 — a collage slide leaves as a PAINTED video** through `exportHookStillVideo`'s `collage` option (`exportCollageStillVideo`): every cell graded ONCE into a bitmap (the single picture's own rule), then `renderBadge` per frame with the cells' clock; the output is the frame at the variant's resolution from a NOMINAL 4096 source of the frame's shape, since a collage has no one source to size from. Both `renderSlideVideo` (the piece export) and the hook-clip export take that path whenever the slide holds a collage — before the "is it a clip" branch, so a collage whose lead is a clip is still painted (clips in cells are not decoded per frame; the lead's frame is its still). **Not built**: a per-cell override (`LayoutCell.enter`, offered in the lab and not recommended), clips in cells.

## The Itinerary opener: the author's own map, and the media along it (2026-09-14)

**Decision, from the maintainer** (*"a map hook, where i can pick different place on the map, and display them on a map path … options to present the media along the map"*). The engine's third variant, `map` — **Itinerary** on screen, `map` everywhere it is written down, the Trips-vs-`roadtrip` rule. It is deliberately the inverse of the Route: the Route READS the legs and therefore refuses to pin a point (a place has no dates), while here every stop is the AUTHOR's assertion — picked, ordered and given its picture by hand — so marking a point and captioning a photograph with a place name claims nothing the document cannot support. That one sentence is what justifies everything the Route would not do — and it is what still separates the Itinerary from **Virée**, the suite's other map opener: Virée's stops come from the DOCUMENT (the legs, or a picture's own GPS) and it drives a car over them; the Itinerary's come from the author, hold one picture each and are drawn still. Two map openers are not a duplication while they answer different questions; the Route, which answered the same one as the Itinerary with less, is the one that went.

**A stop is a place and, optionally, ONE picture** (`MapStop {id, name, lat, lon, picture?}`), stored in the variant's own options, so it travels in `.roadtrip.json` through `portablePost`'s deep clone with no migration and no fifth site (the `PostBadge.hook` field is v15). `mapOptions` reads a stored list the way `routeOptions` reads a colour: a stop with no finite coordinates is DROPPED (a place typed by hand is a complete place, just not a point), a picture whose ref cannot name a file is dropped, and **the list has NO cap (2026-10-07)**. History, so nobody puts one back: 24 («past that the dots merge») → 99 on 2026-09-29 (his east-coast itinerary met 24 at Agnes Water; 99 because a dot held two digits) → none, his words *«moi j'en avais 120, ça a été coupé à 99 … pas de limite»* — a list cut on READ had silently dropped his last 21 places. What replaced the two-digit reason is `numeralScale` (`stops.ts`): from three digits the numeral shrinks so its width stays that of 2.3 digits at full size, in all three renderers (the opener's canvas, the panel's SVG field, the big map's markers) and the list discs; one and two digits draw exactly as before. Whether dots merge is his to judge on the stage; the pictures' decodes already share one budget (`picture-budget.ts`). Virée's `MAX_PICTURE_STOPS` (24) is a different thing, the thinning of picture stops derived from EXIF, and was left.

**The stops' lists reorder by DRAG (2026-10-07)**, his ask: *«dans la liste … du drag and drop … on peut garder les petites flèches»*. Both lists — the big map's and the panel's — carry a grip per row over ONE hook, `shared/ui/use-list-reorder.ts` (+ pure `list-reorder.ts`); the arrows stay as the one-step and keyboard twin. Rules the code does not say: the list is DRAWN in the held order while the grip travels, so the numbers already read what a drop writes, and the document is written ONCE on the drop (`moveStopTo`) — in the panel that is one undo step, not one per row crossed; the landing is counted on the rows AS DRAWN, the held one skipped, so it moves only past a NEIGHBOUR's middle and cannot flicker; a held row near the scroll box's top/bottom 44 px scrolls it (that is what makes 120 stops reorderable without letting go); the grip alone is `touch-action: none`, the rest of a row still scrolls the list by finger (driven: a swipe on a name scrolls 105 px and moves nothing); the moves are read on the WINDOW and never through pointer capture, because React MOVES the held row's DOM node when the order changes and a capture on a node leaving the tree can be released mid-drag. Driven headless at 1440 (mouse, 120 stops, stop 110 dragged to first through the edge scroll, Done stored it) and at 390 by CDP touch; not on his iPad or iPhone.

**The stops panel: every stop in a ten-row box, a POPOVER per stop, and a stop that KNOWS its place — BUILT 2026-10-07** (§1–§2 of `docs/map-openers-next.md`, his ask over the lab's «first five»: the picture is chosen there, so no stop may be out of reach). Rules: (1) `MapStop extends HookPlace` — the optional v30 facts (`state`, `area`, `stateCode`, `searchCode`, `country`, `countryCode`, `style`) read defensively by `readStops` (`placeFacts`: an empty string is never stored, a country code is upper-cased, an unknown writing dropped), no migration; a stop adopted from a trip place (`hookPlace` copies the facts into `HookStage.places`) or found by the search (`searchPlace`, `adoptSearch` — the `adoptSearchResult` rule: a typed state is never overwritten, the search's code always the latest) knows its state from the start, a dropped pin knows nothing until told. **2026-10-07, the TOWN INDEX fills the rest** (`hooks/stop-index.ts`, pure, §1's «where it can be read»): a tap on a town of the big map adds `cityPlace(town)` (a `Town` read from the index IS the city's record, so it carries `region` / `country`), a tap on a trip place adds the place WHOLE (it used to copy the name and position only), «Call it X» writes the town's facts with its name (`nameOffer` now returns the `city`), and **Fill from the town index** under both lists writes every NAMED stop lacking a state or country in ONE change — by name (the same folded name within `SAME_TOWN_KM` 15, a town over a suburb), else by position only without doubt (a town within `AT_TOWN_KM` 10 and every town within `AROUND_KM` 25 in one `regionKey`: Coolangatta / Tweed Heads stay unfilled), never overwriting a fact and taking nothing from a town whose country or state contradicts the stop's (`officialStateCode` lets «NSW» match New South Wales). Names are folded ONCE per fill (`townNames`): a fill of 120 stops costs ~115 ms on the real index, where folding per stop took seconds. The big map's Done compares stops with `sameStopPlace` (facts included), or a fill alone read as no change. The panel reads the index on the click (`loadGazetteer`); the note stands until the lacking count moves. Driven headless: 3 of 4 filled, Point Danger left, Brisbane's tap and «Call it Eagleby» stored with Queensland. (2) **ONE writing path**: `stopText(place, style, writing)` runs `writePlace` through the cascade stop → opener (`MapOptions.placeStyle` / `DriveOptions.placeStyle`, `'trip'` by default = the trip's BADGE writing, so nothing stored changes) → trip; the trip's `placeStyle` + `stateCodes` reach every opener as `HookContext.writing` (`hookContextFor`); the Itinerary writes its stops in `prepare()` (`writtenStops`, so labels, cards and the caption agree), Virée in `driveRoute`'s `customStops` and `placeStops` (the legs' places too). A `Written as` row sits under Names in both panels (`StopStyleRow`), hidden on Virée's picture stops (a day, not a place). (3) The list is `max-h-[21rem] overflow-y-auto overscroll-contain`, no `touch-action` (the grip alone claims a gesture), the selected row revealed in ITS box (`revealInScroller(row, { scroller })`), the `—`/`photo` column a `Icons.image` glyph with the file name on hover in both lists. (4) The popover is an `AnchoredPopover` (`role="dialog"`, `within={listRef}` so a press on another row re-anchors instead of closing) anchored to the row, in his order: a `MapField` (a click on empty ground MOVES this stop, a ring REPLACES its place whole — `replaceStopPlace` keeps id and picture —, nothing adds), `PlaceSearchField` bounded to the stops' and places' majority country, State + its own Written select (only once a state is known), lat/lon, the picture, Earlier/Later/Remove. The open stop IS the popover: the old `lastSelectedStop` memory across unmounts is gone — a popover reopening on its own, anchored to a row not yet measured, is the worse surprise. Driven headless at 1440 (31 stops in a 336 px box, Sydney adopted with its facts and written «Sydney, NSW» on the stage label and the badge's caption, Earlier, Escape); not on his phone.

**The map openers' next round is DECIDED, not built (2026-10-07) — `docs/map-openers-next.md`.** His answers to the «Labo Virée» lab (https://claude.ai/artifact/XkX5EwmpS2gf8g5Gyg2pa3), with «je n'ai pas demandé le développement tout de suite»: (1) the panel keeps EVERY stop — its picture is chosen there — in a box about ten rows tall that scrolls, a click opening a POPOVER (mini map, name + search, the STATE written like any place — «Sydney NSW», in full or the name alone —, lat/lon, picture, Earlier/Later); this reverses the lab's «show the first five». A stop must KNOW its place (state, country, codes, writing — the v30 fields, optional on `MapStop`), which it does not today. (2) The `—`/`photo` column becomes a picture glyph. (3) The camera exactly as proposed, presets included (`map-camera.ts`, a track baked with the plan). (4) Grouping nearby places with every option kept (`stop-clusters.ts`, render-time, off by default, consecutive by default). (5) The trip RECAP (lab https://claude.ai/artifact/WXTxg7iybW7Ez2Q65CWmSq), accepted on its recommendations: ONE clock — the trip's day — read by the car, the counter, the km and the background picture; the badge's counter declared as *Follows the drive* in its Counter section (the existing `content(t)` contract, never a silent override); a Calendar ↔ Road pace; dates of «your places» from the stages, else the picture, never invented. Order: the panel and the glyph, then the recap, then the camera. Open: whether the panel keeps PR #253's drag grip.

**The trip RECAP — the badge counts WITH the car — BUILT 2026-10-07** (§5 of `docs/map-openers-next.md`, his «tu peux commencer le développement … la récap de voyage … OK avec les recommandations»). Rules: (1) **Declared where the counter lives, never a silent override**: four `CounterMode`s (`drive-days` · `drive-km` · `drive-places` · `drive-days-km`, `driveCountOf()` in `day-badge.ts`; the last added later the same day, §5's «Days + km»: the DAY is the headline, odometer and all, and the distance so far rides in the counter beside the day's total in WHOLE units with the suite's space grouping — `of 90 · 1 479 km`, a decimal ticking there read as noise —, its base at rest the day alone since the unit is the opener's), listed with the others, usable only when the piece's `hook` holds the `drive` layer (else `unavailable` says so and the day of the trip stands); `counterPieces` hands TRUE readings at rest (the day of the trip; `0` km and `Stop 1` with no total — the road's total is the opener's to know) and Virée's `content(t)` overwrites `label` · `headline` · `counter` every frame through `driveCounterPieces` (`drive-plan.ts`), the contract Défilé's numeral and the caption already ride; past the end the final reading, so a still is the trip told whole. (2) **ONE clock, the stops' DAYS**: `DriveStop.days {arrive, leave}` on a continuous scale (day 1.0 = the morning of day 1, N + 1.0 = the end of day N; the counter reads `counterDay` = floor, capped at the trip's length), dated from the document only — `stagePlaceDays` shares a leg's span EVENLY among its located places (a leg is dated, its places are not; an even share invents no order the list lacks) with a place's own `arrived`/`left` winning (`HookPlace.arrived/left`, copied by `hookPlace`); a custom stop takes the trip place within `SAME_PLACE_KM` (1 km), else its picture's day, else nothing; a picture stop its pictures' earliest → the end of the latest. `stopClock` places an UNDATED stop between its dated neighbours by distance (on the road, moving no counter), holds at the ends, and is null when nothing is dated — the day mode then hands back `{}` and the badge's base stands; `DriveRoute.undated` is said in the panel. Every phase carries `day0/day1`, a run reading them through its EASING so the day and the km follow the same car; `DriveMoment.day`. (3) **The recap changes nothing of a plain drive**: `drivePlan(route, o, recap)` — `recap` = `driveCountOf(ctx.counterMode) !== null`, passed by `prepare`, the panel and `wantsBasemap` — and only then do the clock, the `stay` phases, the `summary` beat and the milestones exist; a document whose badge counts something else keeps its schedule to the second (pinned by a spec). (4) **Pace** (`DriveOptions.pace`, 0..1, default 0.65 — safe because it is read under a recap only): a run's share of `driveSeconds` is `(1 − p) · length share + p · travel days / day total`, a `stay` at a stop `p · dwell / day total` — the sum is still `driveSeconds`, and since 2026-10-07 truly so: `shareRoadTime` gives a run under `MIN_RUN_SECONDS` its floor TAKEN FROM the others (and, where the floors alone overrun the length, shares it evenly and gives the stays nothing), because the floor used to be added on top — free while runs were few, but a recap ends a run at every dated stop, so 160 stops made a 12 s drive last 68 s and an Auto slide capped at 60 s cut the end off (his report «ça rallongeait la durée … on ne pouvait plus voir l'animation en entier»); the panel says when the hops are under the floor (`schedule.roadFloor`) and a recap now costs only the summary card's 3 s; a piece whose length is SET (every piece made before rule D) is told of the cut beside the Counter's select, with a «Use N s» verb, not only in the Slide section; and **the car STOPS for a place's days only when asked** (`DriveOptions.waitStays`, off, «Wait at the place» in the Recap group, his «les mouvements du véhicule stoppent à chaque lieu alors qu'on n'a pas coché ce mode»), and then only for a stay of `STAY_MIN_SECONDS` (1 s) or more: every dated place used to end an eased run, so the car came to a halt at each one, a stay of 0.1 s included — now a run ends only at a halt, a kept stay or the last stop, and the places between are KNOTS on the run (`Phase.knots`: arc length, day, cost by pace), the car rolling through them slower where a place took days, the counter reading each place's days as it passes (a place rolled past is crossed mid-way through its days, the first left on its first day, the last reached on the trip's last); `at()`, the arrivals and `dayTurns` read the knots; a day picture pops on its day only inside a kept stay; and the CAMERA follows from it: Follow's pull-back is per HOP between the stops the car stops at, so a recap that stopped at every place had no hop long enough to pull back over and the zoom stayed tight (his «ça ne respecte plus les paramètres de zoom», measured 0.38 of a plain drive's widest view) — the knots give it back, a kept stay splitting the hops exactly as a picture's halt does (pinned by a spec); at 0 the days a place took pass when the car leaves it (the hint says so); the last stop's dwell runs over the `arrive` phase, so the counter ends on the trip's last day; a run now ends at a stop that halts OR stays, and a halt of 0 s is no longer pushed. (5) **Summary** (`SUMMARY_SECONDS` 3, `schedule.summaryAt`, `paintSummary`: days · distance · stops on a paper card in the map's box, the words the trip's `BadgeWords` — `stop` is a new optional field, `Stop`/`Étape`, in `WORD_FIELDS` — through `DriveScratch.words`) and **milestones** (`roadMilestones`, pure: every `MILESTONE_DAYS` 50 and `MILESTONE_DISTANCE` 1 000 in the piece's unit; a day reached during a stay sits ON its stop, one on a run interpolates; a tick across the road, its number once passed), both default on, both recap-only. (6) **The plate** (`DriveOptions.plate`, off): `backdrop` pictures or the `picture` ground get the map's box as a translucent paper plate, the road and names inked as on paper, the picture pushing in 6 % over 8 s (`fullFrame`) — usable on any drive, not only a recap. (7) `DRIVE_LIMITS.driveSeconds.max` 12 → 60 (a stored value is ≤ 12, nothing changes). **The ODOMETER digits are BUILT (the same day, later — «The counter's digits are an ODOMETER» below)**; **The picture of the DAY and a tick per day are BUILT (2026-10-07, later)**: `DriveOptions.dayPictures` (on) — `driveRoute` gives each `StopPicture` its trip day once (`date` from the picked picture, `day` through the calendar), and under the recap's clock a picture whose day falls strictly inside a stop's stay (`arrive < day < leave`) pops at that day's fraction of the stay phase (the last stop's over the arrive beat) and leaves with it; the arrival day and the leaving day keep the old pop on arrival, since the leave day is only reached as the run starts. `dayTicks` (on) adds a light tick (`0.32` gain, `1.35` rate) at each instant `dayTurns()` reads — the integer crossings of every phase's `day0 → day1`, a run's through its easing's INVERSE so it lands on the frame the numeral turns —, dropped within `DAY_TICK_GAP` (0.07 s) of the previous one or of a landing/shutter. Both act only under a recap; a plain drive is unchanged. **Défilé's RIBBON under the map is BUILT (2026-10-07, his «vas-y pour le ruban»)** — as `DriveOptions.ribbon` (off), NOT an opener stack: the ribbon reads Virée's day clock and Virée's map box, which no contract between two layers carries, so a stack would have needed a cross-layer channel AND the stack screen `hook-engine.md` §6 still lacks; the option borrows Défilé's painter instead (`scrub-paint.ts`'s `paintTape(g, reading, style, geometry)`, now exported and fed by Défilé itself the same way), so the two tapes are one drawing. `drive-ribbon.ts` (pure): the reading is null without the recap's clock or on a one-day trip; the head is the clock's day clamped to [1, N], so it stands over tick k exactly while the badge says day k (pinned against `counterDay`); the tape is as wide as the map's box and hangs under it past the scale bar and the distance (100 u, else 28 u, 40 u past a plate), ABOVE the box with its ticks rising where the band would leave the frame (a landscape frame, a map at the bottom); inked like the map on paper, Défilé's white on a dark band over a picture (backdrop or picture ground). The stack's storage is untouched and still has no UI. Driven headless (seeded trip, the stage at several times, the three modes' lines, the summary frame); not on his Mac or phone, not exported to H.264 here.

**The picking mini-maps draw the SHIPPED coastline (2026-10-09, his «la carte est complètement noire»)**: `MapField` — the field in the stops editor AND in a stop's popover — read nothing but a graticule, so it was a black box with dots. It now draws `public/geo/land.json` (the big map's and the overview's own file, from our own origin: no network exception, and `loadLand` caches the promise so every field shares one request) through `shared/map/land-path.ts` (pure: only the rings whose box meets the field, a point under 0.8 field units from the last skipped), projected once per framing, as an on-media fill at 13 % under the graticule. OSM tiles stay where they were: the big map's opt-in switch.

**No opener STACK screen (2026-10-09, his call)**: the layer list stays storage only. The badge is drawn over every opener anyway (the «Badge» opener is the one that draws NOTHING), sequencing two openers is two slides, two frame owners make no sense together, and the one cross-opener need so far (Défilé's ribbon on Virée's clock) was built as an option. Do not propose a stack UI again without a combination he asks for.

**Every map opener takes its stops from ONE source choice (2026-10-08, his «les mêmes options de sélection des points que Virée» for the Itinerary and the Recap card).** `stop-source.ts`: `stopsOn` = `places` (the legs' located places up to the piece's day) · `custom` (the author's own list, `stops`) · `pictures` (the picked photos' positions), with `picked` and `includePieces` — Virée's own keys, now also on `MapOptions` (default `custom`, so every stored itinerary is unchanged) and on the card's options (default `places`, what it drew). The legs and the photos are resolved by Virée's `driveRoute` and READ BACK as editor stops (`sourceStops` → `mapStopOf`: the bare name, the place's facts through the new `DriveStop.source`, the first picture), so the three openers draw the same list. `StopSourceRows` (`stop-source-rows.tsx`) is the one panel for all three: the Legs · Your map · Photos switch, the photo chooser on Photos (Virée's Pictures group no longer repeats it there), the told days' pictures on the legs, and the editor ALWAYS shown — on the legs or the photos it shows the derived list, and an edit makes it the author's own map (`ownStops`: `stopsOn: 'custom'`, fresh ids). A stop holding a picture is never given it again by the picked pictures (`customStops`), which is what makes taking over a photos list safe. The Itinerary's `wantsPictures`, `frameBox` and `drawnOptions` read the resolved list; its `needs` gained `coverage` (the days that date the legs and photos). The Recap card's Photo ground stays the slide's own picture; only its contact sheet asks for the road's pictures.

**The recap's SUMMARY CARD is configurable (2026-10-08, his «lance le développement» on the lab https://claude.ai/artifact/5rCRZCwpbsywuVaXfZrKGY).** (1) **The trip's LOOK reaches the openers**: `HookContext.theme` (= `TripDoc.theme`, null = neutral) and `tripName`, filled by `hookContextFor` — an opener that sets words of its own hands each string to the overlay engine as a text element under that theme (`look-text.ts`: `drawLookTexts`, `measureLookText`, `fitLookText`, `textElementSize` exported from `draw-overlays.ts`), so it takes the badge's face, weight, case, spacing, colour, glow and grain, never a second recipe. On the map's PAPER a string takes the map's ink and drops the glow and the legibility (`LookText.ink` pins `color`, `glow`, `legibility`): gold or red on cream reads badly, the face and the case still say the look. The engine sets the canvas alpha itself, so a string's opacity is handed to it as the progress of a linear fade (`originSeconds = t − alpha`) — an outer `globalAlpha` is overwritten. A badge whose pieces are all hidden loads none of the look's faces, so Virée loads them (`loadLook`) in `prepare` and awaits them in `ready`. The stamp of before (days · distance · stops in the map's box) now wears the look, on OPAQUE paper with a stacked-fill shadow (2026-10-09: at 0.94 alpha the map's names read through it, his «tampon translucide»). (2) **Six faces over ONE model** (`summary-card.ts`, pure: options, facts, cells, labels, look, ink, `cardScene`; `summary-paint.ts`, the painter; `summary-card-panel.tsx`, the rows): `cardFace` trace · ticket · passport · sheet · stamp · dash. A stored recap that says nothing reads `stamp` (`CARD_DEFAULTS`), a NEW Virée writes `trace` (`NEW_CARD` over `driveVariant.defaults`) — his «Trace par défaut» without moving any saved piece. The card options are FLAT keys on `DriveOptions` (`cardFace`, `cardLook`, `cardFacts`, `cardGround`, `cardTitle`, `cardSubtitle`, `cardLabels`, `cardLabelKm`, `cardLabelName`, `cardChosen`, `cardEntrance`, `cardFrom`, `cardTo`, `badgeWhen`), read by `readCardOptions` inside `driveOptions`; what names THIS trip (title, subtitle, chosen places, codes, the facts list — a list, so the house-style test demands it) is in `contentKeys`. (3) **Facts are MEASURED** off the plan (`cardFacts`): days from the recap's clock, km, places (a grouped halt counts its members), states (`DriveStop.states`, written by `stopState` like every list — the trip's code, else the state's name), photos, the longest stay; a fact the drive cannot measure is LEFT OUT of the card, never a zero; numbers count up eased (`cellAt`) over 1.4 s, the road drawing itself on the same clock, or `cut`. (4) **Names** (`cardLabels`): none · ends · groups · all · chosen; `groups` reuses `stop-clusters.ts` at the CARD's own radius (default 200 km, every visit of a place one group — the card sees the trip at once, his Q3) named by the biggest town (`biggestTownWithin`), the longest stay or the first place, written «Cairns +2»; the ends rank first and a name that would cover another, the title or the facts is SKIPPED at paint (`placeLabels`). `DriveStop.place` keeps the bare name (the writing may add the state). `wantsTowns` now also asks for the index when a drive's card groups by town. (5) **Look and ground** (`cardTheme`, `cardInk`): the trip's theme or a preset for the CARD ALONE (his Q4, the badge keeps the trip's); ground `photo` = the road's last picture veiled (falls back to `solid` where the road has none — the drive's pictures are the card's), `solid` = one per look (warm black + grain for Or ciné, phosphor + scan lines for Pixel CRT, a flat of the look's red with WHITE words for Rouge plein cadre, slate otherwise), `paper` = the map's paper with the map's ink (the look's colour kept for the road where it reads on cream — a white look falls back to the ink). (6) **The badge and the card are never on screen together** (`badgeWhen`, `auto` = `during`): `HookRender.badgeWindow` → `ResolvedHook.badgeWindow` (the last layer that says) → `windowedBadge` in `hookElementsAt`, `slideRender` and the editor's `hookElements`, which re-window the BADGE pieces only (`piece:` ids — the slide's free text keeps its life); an exit lands on the window's end, an open window keeps a piece's own end; `fitRender` scales it. `before` = the car waits `BADGE_BEFORE_SECONDS` (2.5 s) on top of its delay; `during` = [0, summaryAt]; `end` = NO card, the reveal runs and the badge comes at `revealAt`; `never` = [0, 0], the editor's ghost still picks a piece. Only a recap with its summary on gets a window; everything else lives as before. (7) **The card is the drive's END**: a schedule with a card has no reveal (`DriveSchedule.reveals`), so the still at rest — the hook's thumbnail — IS the card; once a full-frame card has risen the map under it is not painted (`cardCovers`). (7b) **The pickers are PICTURES** (2026-10-08, his «des mini-miniatures … des mini-tuiles»): `card-thumbs.tsx` — each face drawn small by `paintCard` itself over the piece's real road and numbers (a stamp sketch, `paintStamp`, on the paper for the one face Virée draws in its map's box), repainted once the look's fonts are in; the look a row of five dark tiles with «90» set in each (the trip's own first). The face's one-line hint sits under the grid. (7d) **The PASSPORT stamps the road, state else place** (2026-10-09, his report — one place with a state made every other place vanish — and the lab https://claude.ai/artifact/N6Shz9sFd3vdg6eK2UQDX7, «vas-y avec tes recommandations»): `passport-stamps.ts` (pure) walks the stops in order — `cardStampMode` `mixed` (default, for stored cards too: no migration, his Q1) · `states` · `places`; a missing state is read from the shipped town index AT RENDER (`cardStampFill: 'index'`, default; `placeFromIndex` by the place's name, else `regionAround` the point — only what the index says without doubt — written as the trip writes codes through `stopState`; never written into the places, his Q2; the editor keeps «Fill from the town index»); consecutive stops in one state are one stamp, a destination stamped `once` (default, Q3) or at each `visit`; a state as its code or in full, a place with none as three letters, in full or no stamp; the SHAPE says the level (round = state, rectangle = place — it used to alternate by index); past 9 stamps a «+N». The «states» fact counts the derived states too. The shell's `towns` ARE the gazetteer's records at runtime (`townsFromOrder`), so the index reader casts them and caches its folded names per array (`WeakMap`). `cardWantsTowns` now asks for the index for a passport that reads it. (7c) **No distance comparison** («plus que Paris → Le Caire», the recap lab's variant): REJECTED 2026-10-09 — anecdotal for him, and it would ship a table of famous distances; do not re-propose it. (8) **The card alone on a slide** — the `card` opener («Recap card», `card.tsx`): the same `cardScene` and painter over the trip's legs up to the piece's day (`stopsOn: 'places'`, no pictures of its own, no grouping, `drivePlan(…, recap = true)` for the clock, so its numbers are Virée's); a `layer`, so on a Photo ground the SLIDE's own picture is under it, veiled (`CardScene.below`); its badge window is always shut; `count` plays 2 s, `cut` plays nothing; the stamp is not offered (it needs the map). The PNG thumbnail is the deck's ordinary PNG export of that slide. Driven headless: the six faces in four looks, with and without pictures, 9:16 and 16:9, the panel picking a face and a ground, the Recap card picked in the opener list; not on his Mac or phone, not exported to H.264.

**The counter's digits are an ODOMETER — BUILT 2026-10-07** (the last line of §5 of `docs/map-openers-next.md`: «each in a fixed-width cell, so the badge never shakes when 199 turns 200»). The badge stays text drawn by `drawOverlays`; what changed is that a text element may carry the continuous VALUE behind its numeral (`OverlayElement.odometer`, engine-level — `studio.md`). Rules: (1) **The value travels with the content, the text stays the string** every reader knows: `BadgeContent.headlineValue` (optional), handed by Virée's `driveCounterPieces` with every rewrite (`HookContentPatch` = the pieces + `headlineValue`, merged by `contentAt` like a piece), and `badgeElements` puts it on the HEADLINE element alone — days on the continuous scale (`m.day` clamped to the trip), the distance in the piece's unit (and only from 10 up: under ten the numeral keeps a decimal and is no odometer's), the stops reached; past the end a WHOLE number, so the final reading rests. (2) **The cells** (`overlay/odometer.ts`, pure): every digit takes the widest digit's advance, measured on the element's font with its letter-spacing, so the box holds still as the numeral counts (a 1 sits centred in a 0's room); a separator — the thin space of «1 317» — keeps its own; the box's height is the ten digits' own, not the current text's. Measured headless: `111` and `100` take the same box as odometers, 55 px apart as plain text at this size. (3) **The roll is a car's**: the units rest for 70 % of their unit and roll over the last `ROLL_SHARE` (0.3), a higher digit along with them only while every digit under it is a 9 (`digitRolls`: 199 → 200 rolls three, 198 → 199 one) — rolling UP, the leaving digit out by the top and its successor in from below, each cut at the line's own height. (4) **Every pass draws through ONE routine** (`fillLayoutText`: the glow's three layers, the grain mask, the plain fill), and a rolling cell is cut in a scratch buffer (`'roll'`) that is then drawn with the pass's own shadow, blur and composite — cutting the glyph in place cut its SHADOW with it into a visible rectangle (seen headless on the first try); the grain mask's cache key carries the rolls quantised to 1/32 (`rollKey`), or a glow's grain would hold the mask of the digit before. A typewriter reveal counts cells. Driven headless at 600 px: the plain and glowed roll at 199.85 and 1 317.9, the resting 199; the recap's stage not re-driven (the contract is the one the recap already rides). Not seen on his Mac or phone.

**Virée's CAMERA is a baked track — BUILT 2026-10-07** (§3 of `docs/map-openers-next.md`, accepted as the lab proposed, presets included). `hooks/map-camera.ts` (pure): `cameraTrack(plan, o, box, margin)` samples the plan at 30 fps ONCE in `prepare` (`driveTrack` on the nominal 1080-wide frame of the piece's shape, so every size sees one camera — pinned by a spec) and `at(t)` interpolates; nothing accumulates while playing. Rules: (1) **View width is KILOMETRES across the map's box** (`viewKm`, 5–3000 on a log slider, `roundKm`), turned into plan units by `kmPerPlanUnit` = `KM_PER_DEGREE / geo.scale`; a piece stored with a SHARE (`followZoom`) and no width reads as that share (`followWidth`), so nothing stored changes — pinned. (2) **The defaults are the plain follow of before** (fixed zoom, no smoothing, north up, no wide shots) and choosing *Follow the car* in the panel writes the **Calm** preset over a piece that never chose (`viewKm === null` and the default share); a stored follow piece keeps its look. (3) Pull-back is van Wijk & Nuij's flyTo (`flyToWidth`, ρ = 1.42) per RUN phase, mixed by the strength in LOG space and smoothed in log space, so a 1 000 km hop opens up in its middle and a 10 km one barely moves. (4) Smoothing is a CENTRED Gaussian over the car's position read `lookAhead` later (the drive is known ahead: no lag); heading-up weighs the heading by DISPLACEMENT over the turn window (a halt turns nothing — pinned), unwraps, rate-limits to `maxTurn`, and sets `angle = −π/2 − heading` with the car `LEAD_ROOM` (15 % of the frame) down from the centre. (5) Open / End wide blend from `wholeFrame` to the follow frame over `WIDE_SECONDS` (1.8). **The car never leaves the frame**: the centre is kept within `KEEP_IN_FRAME` (0.7) of the box's half-height from the car — the lab's look-ahead read 0.7 s ahead on an 8 s drive over 2 100 km put the car 400 px below the frame (seen headless; pinned by a spec). (6) **`View` carries an `angle`** now (`applyView` rotates after scaling, `viewOf` builds one): the road, dots, cards, labels and milestones go through `at` and turn for free; what is drawn on the frame's axes — the tiles' rectangle, the graticule — is drawn INSIDE a canvas rotation about `(tx, ty)` against the unturned view (`turned()`), the graticule reaching the frame's diagonal; the car's heading and the milestone ticks are rotated by `turnedDir`; the compass rose turns with the map. (7) `driveBasemap` covers the WIDEST frame of the track (`widestFrame`) and the frame's diagonal under heading-up. Panel: a *Camera* group — mode, presets (`cameraPresetOf` lights the one the values ARE), view width, zoom + strength, orientation, wide shots, and the four fine rows behind a `Fine settings…` link (Hick). Measured headless at 540 × 960: prepare 5–14 ms per preset, paint 5 ms a frame (15 under heading-up, the turned graticule and tiles paid). The Itinerary's camera and the finer tiles along a following camera are built — the next two entries. Not seen on his Mac or phone.

**The Itinerary's CAMERA follows the pen — BUILT 2026-10-07** (the last of §3's «may reuse»). Rules: (1) **The camera follows a SUBJECT** (`CameraSubject` in `map-camera.ts`: `at(t)`, `runAt(t)` — the run's fraction and its km, for the pull-back —, `bounds`, `kmPerUnit`): `driveSubject(plan)` is Virée's car (`cameraTrack(plan, …)` unchanged, bit for bit: its spec), `mapSubject(o, timing, box)` the pen — its position on the same arcs the paint draws (`arcControl` + `quadAt` over `penAt`), a hop's `hopKms`, in the pixels of the map's BOX on the nominal frame; `subjectTrack` bakes either. (2) **North stays up, by decision**: a pen has no heading worth turning the map for, so `MapOptions` keeps only `camera · viewKm · zoom · pullBack · smoothing · lookAhead · openWide · endWide` (`MAP_CAMERA_KEYS`; `mapCamera(o)` fills the rest, `mapCameraPatch` drops a heading or a turn a shared row might write — pinned), and `cameraPresetOf(o, keys)` compares on the opener's own keys so Navigation without its heading is still Navigation there. (3) **The view is a transform over the paint's own projection** (`mapView` → `viewed(project, view)`): the track is baked on the CENTRED nominal box, read at the painted box through `u`, so a dragged or resized map sees the same geography (pinned) and the whole-camera path is untouched (`view = null`); the line, the dots, the names, the pins and the pen are CLIPPED to the box's rounded rect under a follow (an inset map cuts its world), the card, the strip, the compass and the distance stay in the frame. A name laid out against the frame's edge may be cut at the box's — accepted. (4) **The panel's rows are ONE component** (`camera-rows.tsx`, `CameraRows`: mode, presets, width, zoom, orientation only where `heading`, wide shots, the fine settings behind a link; `roundKm` and `presetValues` live there), Virée's group being the first consumer; the Itinerary's *Camera* group shows only while the journey is drawn with two stops or more, says *Whole map · Follow the pen*, and choosing Follow on a piece with no width writes Calm. (5) **The ground follows**: `mapBasemap(o, aspect, track, budget)` returns a `BasemapSet` (the wide raster grown to the widest frame under a follow, plus the pyramid — `basemap-strip.ts`, shared with Virée), and `wantsBasemap` now reads the stops as DRAWN (`drawnOptions`: written, grouped — the keys the paint looks up; before, a grouping that moved the bounds made the shell fetch one region while the paint looked up another). Measured headless on a four-stop fixture at 540 × 960: follow 60 km → 90 one-tile patches at z 9 over the wide raster's z 7, prepare 5–13 ms, paint 0.2–0.3 ms a frame; the frame centred on the pen's stop, clipped at the box. Not seen on his Mac or phone, not exported to H.264.

**Finer tiles along a following camera — a zoom PYRAMID, and an export that WAITS for it — 2026-10-07, rev. the same day** (his report: Virée, and he thought the Itinerary, «zooms without updating its scale», the tiles staying at their first density and BLURRY while the camera is close, and a recorded file must never miss a tile). **Measured first** (vitest over the 2 100 km Western Australia fixture, 8 s, 9:16, ground density at the frame's centre over the density its 1 920 delivery asks, per frame): the first version — a STRIP at ONE zoom, the deepest the whole road fitted in 256 tiles, faded to the wide raster past 2–3× the follow's width — drew 80–100 % of the frames under HALF their density (fixed 35 km: 0.29 everywhere; Calm median 0.42; Navigation median 0.11, its pull-back being most of the drive). Three causes, all confirmed: one zoom for every frame, so a pull-back's middle drew the 3 072 px wide raster enlarged ten times; a portrait frame's density read off its WIDTH as if it were the 1 920 long edge (one zoom too deep, four times the tiles, which the budget then took back from everywhere); and, under heading-up, the paint's cull computed the frame around its own centre while `turned()` rotates about `(tx, ty)` — Navigation dropped its patches and drew the wide raster three zooms short (seen headless, colour-coded stub tiles). Rules now: (1) **Each frame of the baked track takes its OWN zoom** (`planPyramid` in `shared/map/tile-strip.ts`, pure: sampled at `TRACK_FPS`, so a frame the export draws IS a sample; `zoomForDensity` rounds UP, so a tile is never enlarged; density from the delivery's LONG edge) and the tiles it touches at that zoom, counted once; levels at or under the wide raster's own zoom cost nothing. (2) **Over the budget every frame gives up the same number of levels**, and the room left is given back one level at a time to the STILLEST frames first (`StripSample.motion`: screen pixels a second of pan plus zoom — a halt, an opening, a slow stretch, where the eye has time to read the ground) — measured against lowering a ceiling, which starved exactly the tight frames he looks at. (3) **The paint draws the frame's OWN tiles** (`BasemapSet.tilesAt(t)`, from the planner's `frameTiles`) over the wide raster, and for one not decoded yet its nearest decoded ANCESTOR — never a hole. Under full strength the levels are composed in ONE frame-sized layer and laid down once (`paintGround`): two layers at 0.9 leave 1 % of paper where one leaves 10 %. (4) **The pyramid is STREAMED** (rev. the same day, his «build the tile streaming for long drives»; «Streamed tiles» below): the budget counts requests and blobs, not bitmaps. A tile that cannot be fetched is fetched from its PARENT, then its grandparent (`fetchPatch`) and cut out of it at decode, and counted (`HookPictureStatus.coarser`); each tile request times out at 20 s. (5) **An export AWAITS the ground**: `useHookPictures().ready(signal)` resolves once the LATEST ask (its pass key and Library, compared by what was asked, not a counter — an export clicked between a change's render and its effect waited for nothing in a first try) has landed or failed AND the editor has rendered with it; the piece export, the slides export and the hook clip await it before their first frame, then read the opener and the pictures as they are THEN (`ground` ref in `use-post-exports.ts`), and what the ground lost is said with the delivery (`groundNote`: «N map tiles could not be fetched — a coarser map stands in there; M came from a coarser zoom»). The panel says the pyramid in one line, and that an export waits. **Measured after** (same fixture, budget 512): Calm median 1.50 (0 % under 0.8), Documentary 1.45, Navigation 0.85, fixed 35 km 0.58; a one-day drive (Perth → Cervantes) every preset at ≥ 0.7 and most at full density. Headless with the stub (`page.route` answering PNG tiles coloured by zoom): Navigation, Calm, Documentary and the Itinerary's follow draw the planned level frame by frame (the Itinerary z8 → z12 → z8 through the drive), `ready` resolved after 4–5 s with all 512 in, with ~20 % of the deep tiles answering 500, 63 were drawn from a parent and 2 left to the wide raster, never a hole; paint in SwiftShader 175–220 ms a frame against 85 for the wide raster alone (software; the strip's 2.1 ms on a GPU is the reference). Not against the real tile server (unreachable here), not on his Mac or phone, not exported to H.264.

**Streamed tiles — the whole pyramid fetched ahead, decoded around the playhead — BUILT 2026-10-07** (his «build the tile streaming for long drives», the proposal recorded the same evening). Full detail over a long, tight drive is thousands of tiles (2 000–3 700 over the 2 100 km fixture at 35 km) and a decoded tile is 256 kB, a blob 20–40 kB. Rules: (1) **Two budgets, two resources** (`tile-strip.ts`): `STRIP_TILES` 3 072 on a computer, 768 on a phone is what the planner may FETCH — requests to OSM and compressed blobs kept for the piece —; `STREAM_DECODED` 512 / 128 is the bitmaps held at once (128 / 32 MB). The planner returns each sample's tiles (`PyramidPlan.frameTiles`), so `tilesAt(t0, t1)` is a window nearest first. (2) **A stream per pyramid** (`shared/map/tile-stream.ts`, `TileStream`, injected fetch/decode so its window rules are node-tested): `fetchAll` takes every blob, four at a time, coarse levels first; `ready(tiles)` PINS the nearest `cap` and decodes them, awaited; `request(tiles)` decodes in the background without touching the pins (and remembers a tile whose blob is not in yet, decoded the moment it lands); least recently used bitmaps are closed past the cap, never a pinned one; a queued decode the latest preview paint no longer asks for is DROPPED before it runs (a phone's 128-bitmap window otherwise thrashed: stale decodes evicting the tiles on screen), never one an export's `ready` awaits; `dispose` answers every queued decode so an awaiting export cannot hang; an export frame HOLDS its stream while it waits (`holdingStream`), so a camera changed under a frozen piece cannot release the ground the rest of the export draws; a module registry (`openStream` / `closeStream` / `streamFor`, refcounted, released 4 s late) is how the shell fills it and the painters read it, the variant never fetching. (3) **The shell opens it**: `wantsBasemap` returns the wide raster and ONE pyramid want (`HookBasemapWant.pyramid`), `useHookPictures` opens the stream and its pass ends only once every blob is answered — so `ready()` (what the exports await at the click) means the network is done. (4) **An export waits per FRAME**: `HookRender.ready(t0, t1)` / `ResolvedHook.ready` (folded across layers, scaled by `fitRender`); `renderBadge({ ground: true })` awaits `ready(t, t + EXPORT_AHEAD)` (1 s ahead, so decoding overlaps encoding) — the PNG deck (`badgeToPng`), the painted clips and the rail thumbnails set it, the stage never does; the clip-over-video path, whose paint is synchronous inside the decoder's output, awaits `FrameProcessor.ahead(t)` in the FEED loop before each sample, the window `[t − 1, t]` covering the frames the decoder still holds back (`exportVariantVideo`'s `prepareUnderOverlays`). (5) **The preview never waits**: the paint draws what is decoded, asks for the next 0.6 s, and the stage repaints when tiles land (`onGroundLanded`, 80 ms batches, only the stage of a piece with an opener; progress readers have their own channel so 3 000 blobs landing do not repaint the stage). (6) **Decode trap, fixed**: a tile decoded from its blob must take its plan from its OWN column and row — a plan derived from the box rounded the west edge a hair under and started a column early, drawing a TRANSPARENT tile (the wide raster through it, seen headless); the decode re-lays out of Mercator in 32 bands, not 256 rows. **Measured headless** (stub tiles coloured by zoom, 9:16, 8 s over the fixture): Navigation fetches 3 072 of its 3 713 (one zoom short, against two before), Calm 911 of 911, a fixed 10 km view 3 070 of 9 435; the decoded window peaks at its 512; every exported frame's ground is the PLANNED level at sampled points (the few misses were the opener's own furniture — compass, end card); ~35–40 ms of decode waited per frame and ~100 ms of paint (SwiftShader); 7.5 s to fetch 3 072 stub tiles (the real server will take a minute or more — the panel counts them); a cold jump draws the wide raster, the planned level 0.4 s later. With 20 % of deep tiles answering 500: 523 from a parent, 18 left to coarser ground, said in the delivery. Not driven: the clip-over-video path end to end (no H.264 here), the real server, his Mac or phone. The cache surviving the tab is built: «Tiles kept on the device» below.

**Tiles kept on the device — BUILT 2026-10-07** (his «build the IndexedDB tile cache»). A following camera's pyramid is thousands of tiles and the tab's memory dies with it, so a second export or tomorrow's reopening asked OSM's volunteer-run server for every one again — what its tile policy asks a client not to do. Rules: (1) **Each tile's BLOB is kept** in its own IndexedDB database, `atelier-tiles` (`shared/map/tile-cache.ts`, keyed `z/x/y`, a `used` index), by `fetchTile` in `osm-tiles.ts` — the one door every opener's tile goes through —, never a decoded bitmap. (2) **Fresh for 30 days** (`TILE_FRESH_MS`, pure policy in `tile-cache-policy.ts`): a fresh blob is drawn without a request; an older one asks the server and is drawn ANYWAY when the server fails (offline, 500, timeout) — never on an abort.
