(3) **Bounded by bytes**, 256 MB on a computer, 64 MB on a constrained device, least recently used out first down to 85 %, run 2 s after a burst of writes; `used` is rewritten at most once a day so reads stay reads. (4) **Forgotten with the consent**: `allowTiles(false)` clears the database AND the tab's blob map — what this device's yes fetched goes when the yes goes — and a tile that lands AFTER the no is drawn for whoever asked but never written (`tilesAllowed()` checked at the write; without it, 5 tiles in flight at the revoke came back into the cache, measured headless), and the background row says what is kept («N tiles kept on this device (size) for a month…») with a *Forget them* verb. (5) Every storage failure (private window, quota) degrades to the network, never to an error. **Measured headless** (stub server, Calm over the fixture): first session 985 requests and 985 kept; second session 0 requests, ground ready in 2.4 s; with the server aborting every request, 0 requests and no tile failed; after `allowTiles(false)`, 0 kept. Not against the real server, nor on his Mac or phone (iOS may erase script storage after 7 days unused — the cache simply refills).

**Nearby places are GROUPED at render time — BUILT 2026-10-07** (§4 of `docs/map-openers-next.md`, every option kept). `hooks/stop-clusters.ts` (pure): `groupStops(stops, km, visits)` — `consecutive` (default: only stops that follow each other merge, a later return is a second halt) or `all` (every visit into the first, the car never comes back) — the halt on the member nearest the centre; `groupName` by `town` (the biggest of the shipped index within the group's reach + 15 km), `first` or `central`, a group of one keeping its own name, `town` with no index reading as `first` — never a blank. Rules: (1) **the list is never edited**: Virée folds its BUILT route (`groupRoute` — the three sources alike, pictures concatenated then `crowd`ed, days spanned, the deeper tick kept, `DriveStop.members`), the Itinerary folds the written stops in `prepare` (`groupMapStops`, `MapStop.members` on the prepared copy — `readStops` never reads it, pinned); the `MERGE_KM` 150 m picture merge stays the floor underneath. (2) The options are the opener's (`groupKm` 0 = off, 0.5–80 on a log slider with the three shortcuts; `groupVisits`; `groupName`), read defensively, `mapFromRoute` starting them off; ONE `GroupRows` component in both panels says the real count («12 places make 3 halts»). (3) **The town index reaches `prepare` synchronously** through `HookContext.towns` = `townsIfLoaded()` (a sync cache beside the promise in `load-gazetteer.ts`): the EDITOR asks for the index when any deck layer wants a town name (`wantsTowns`) and rebuilds `baseHookCtx` once it lands (`townsTick`), so the stage, the rail and the exports name the same groups; until it lands the first place names the group, and the row says so. An export from a fresh session that never opened the piece would name by the first place — accepted, said here. (4) The big picking map marks what would merge (`HookStopsChoice.grouping` → `StopsMapSheet`): a member's disc hollow and dashed, the halt's wearing `×N`; both canvases draw `×N` at the dot's shoulder (`paintCount`, shared). Driven headless: 12 places → 3 halts («Melbourne ×10» once the index landed, «Melbourne ×10» by the first place before), the frame, the rows, the map's marks. Not on his Mac or phone. **2026-10-07, his report that grouping made the whole editor stutter**: `groupName('town')` scanned all 130 000 towns per group — 685 ms for 40 groups on the real index — and every surface asks for the names again (the panel on each render, so each FRAME while the piece plays; `prepare`, `wantsPictures`, `wantsBasemap`): playback fell to one frame every 1.7 s, measured headless. Cure: `biggestTownWithin` reads only the one-degree CELLS around the group from a grid built ONCE per index array (a `WeakMap` beside it, ~45 ms the first time), the same answer to the town as the scan (ties by list order, the antimeridian wrapped — held by a spec against a brute scan; the box in degrees is `geo.ts`'s `degreeWindow`, exact on `haversineKm`'s sphere — the old `km / 111.32` and `km / (111.32 · cos lat)` box was 0.1 % short and missed the circle's bulge, so a town 49.99 km away fell outside a 50 km search and `regionAround` missed a border town at 24.99 km; the gazetteer's `nearestCity` and `stop-index.ts` read the same box): a naming pass is 0.3 ms and playback with grouping is the baseline's (33 ms median in SwiftShader either way). Rule: anything an opener's panel or `prepare` computes runs PER FRAME during playback — a scan of the shipped index there is a bug, whatever it costs once. The index's first read is a task now (*Reading the town index* in the pill), so turning grouping on says it is working.

**The clock is PHASES, not one curve.** Hold, then per hop: travel, then dwell. Travel time is shared out by planar hop LENGTH (an even split makes a long hop look teleported) and the easing shapes EACH hop, so «Even» + no dwell is a constant-speed pen and «Settle» + a dwell is a car stopping at each town. One curve over the whole line cannot express a wait, and the wait is what makes a picture at a stop legible rather than a flicker. With the drawing off, `mapTiming` returns every arrival at zero rather than an empty list — the whole path drawn, every pin up, the last stop showing — so no reader downstream branches on "is this one moving".

**The picking map is the export's own projection, run backwards.** `fitProjection` gained an `unproject`, so a click on the field is a coordinate pair and a stop can be dropped where there is nothing to click on; what you point at is what goes out, at the same scale and with the same bow. **No tiles and no basemap, deliberately** — on THIS field (rev. 2026-09-28: finding places moved to the big map below, which offers the existing tiles opt-in; this field stays the export's own projection and fetches nothing): the suite has two network exceptions (the Flight Map's opt-in OSM layer, the opt-in place lookup) and a third hidden inside an options panel is how a local-first promise stops being checkable. The backdrop is a graticule, the trip's own located places are the landmarks (a click adopts one), and a place you cannot see is found by NAME through the same `PlaceSearchField` the legs use. Every gesture has its keyboard twin in the panel beside it: chips for the trip's places, the search field, two number fields for the selected stop's position, buttons for the order.

**Four ways to present the media, and what each is for**: **pin** (a tile beside the stop's own dot, several on screen at once — the mode that makes it an itinerary rather than a slideshow), **card** (one tile under the map, cross-fading, captioned with the stop's NAME — which is why a paper mount is the default: the mount is where a name can go), **backdrop** (the picture fills the frame, dimmed so the line survives; the only mode that replaces the piece's own picture), **strip** (every stop's picture in a contact row, the ones ahead held back behind a veil — the only mode that shows what is still to come). A stop with no picture, or one that could not be decoded, draws **nothing**: no stand-in, no previous picture carried forward — a card captioned "Exmouth" showing Coral Bay is the exact fabrication this tool refuses. Because that means an opener can END on an empty frame, the panel says so when the last stop has no picture; it never quietly fills it.

**The line ahead is the path's BED, not what is left over (2026-09-15).** The maintainer's report: *"when the current trace path is being drawn it removes the faded line underneath (dashed, faint…)"*. The paint drew each hop once, up to the pen — so the moment the pen entered a hop, that hop's dashed remainder vanished and the shape of the journey stopped being readable exactly where the eye was following it. It is now TWO layers over every hop: the undrawn part in the ahead style (`quadTail`, the other half of the de Casteljau split `quadSplit` already gave), then the travelled part solid on top. The rule generalises: **a progress drawing shows the whole path and fills it in; it never grows into nothing.** `hidden` still means hidden — it is the one style that draws no bed.

**Names can ACCUMULATE behind the pen (2026-09-15).** `labels` gained `passed` — every stop the pen has reached keeps its name — beside `current`, which names only where the pen is. Both readings are wanted (a list being written vs. one name following the pen), which is why this is a list of modes and not a switch; `all` was the only way to see more than one name, and it says every name from the first frame, which is a different picture again.

**The opener is CONTENT on the stage: click it, drag it (2026-09-15).** The maintainer: *"we should be able to drag the opener anywhere, like any other content: we click on it and it opens its inspector and we can drag it too"*. `HookVariant` gained two optional, **editor-only** members — `frameBox(options, ctx, frame)` (where the drawing sits, asked for at the stage's own pixel size, so a rect measured against another size cannot hit-test a place the drawing is not) and `moveBy(options, dx, dy)` (pure, incremental, fractions of the frame; options in, options out, so the stage writes them back through the one path the panel uses). Deliberately NOT on `HookRender`: `prepare()`'s closure is what every renderer and both exports share, and none of them has business knowing where a pointer is. A variant that offers neither is simply not grabbable, which is right for the badge. **Défilé offers both (2026-09-15, same request, reversing "its full-frame tape is not grabbable")**: the flashes fill the frame and the numeral is a badge piece, so the thing grabbed and moved is the TAPE — `tapeBox` is the band behind it (drawn or not), the drag writes `offsetX/offsetY` beside Runs / From edge. Its clamps are split on purpose: `moveTape` limits frame-free (sideways until an end meets the edge, the baseline inside the frame), and `tapeGeometry` keeps a MOVED band inside on the frame's own aspect, because the band's depth is in 1080-frame units; an unmoved tape is placed pixel-identical to before. The band's sizes moved from `paintTape` into the pure geometry so the outline and the paint cannot disagree. The "Put it back" row is now one `MovedRow` in `panel-ui.tsx`, shared by both variants. On the stage the **badge keeps first claim** — an opener is placed once, a badge is composed constantly — so the opener is tested only where no element sits, and the picture only where neither does. Selecting it switches the inspector to Look (its panel IS what was selected; there is no one field to focus) and it wears the same dashed outline an element does. The Itinerary stores the drag as `offsetX/offsetY` beside the coarse anchor, clamped to ±45 % so a map can never be dragged out of the frame, and the panel grows a **"Put it back"** row once there is something to undo — the one-way-door rule the cover panel had to be taught.

**A BIG map to FIND places: tap one, two, three (2026-09-28).** His ask: *«une map pour sélectionner les points sur la carte … appuyer un, deux, trois, les tracer sur la carte … un complément»*. The inline field is the export's projection and shows only a graticule — right for nudging a stop, useless for finding one. `StopsMapSheet` (tools/roadtrip, drawn by `HookPicker` through the new host verb `HookPanelHost.editStopsOnMap`, the chooser's pattern: a panel never fetches) is MapLibre in a modal, opened from the shared `StopsEditor`'s «Pick them on a map…» / «Open the map…», so both map openers have it. **Rules**: a DRAFT with Done/Cancel/Escape, the garage's rule (Escape must leave the opener as it was — and since 2026-10-07 the draft is its OWN `HistoryState` (`shared/history/history.ts`): every discrete edit one step (a unique label each, so two quick taps never merge), a name typed one step per stop (`name:<id>`, the 700 ms window), ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z taken in the CAPTURE phase and `preventDefault`ed so the page's undo behind stands down, `UndoRedo` at the foot, and an edit that changes no stop (renumbered onto its own place) records NO step — an undo must always undo something; a held marker re-lights the map and re-says its line only when its landing CHANGES, never per pointer move, since each `say` re-rendered the whole sheet; the panel's «Fill from the town index» writes over the list as it stands AFTER the index's first read, never the one captured at the click; the old «Undo last» REMOVED THE LAST STOP, one he may never have added — his «les undo, redo, ce sont des choses qui doivent être réelles»); **2026-10-07, his verdict on the stops lab (https://claude.ai/artifact/LePzM5kArWVGXAkgqdFKGq)**: a DROP is decided by its landing, never a mode (`hooks/stop-drop.ts`, pure: on another stop within 18 px → SWAP numbers, on a line within 10 px → INSERT between its ends — the dragged stop's own two hops excluded, a stop beating the line it ends —, else MOVE), said live in a chip and lit (the hop as a dark line `hop-lit`, the swap target ringed) while held, the held marker exempt from the reset that puts every other marker back at its draft place; a tap on EMPTY ground does NOTHING (only *Snap to towns: off* still drops exactly), a tap on a LINE inserts the town tapped, else `nameOffer`'s, else the bare point, between its two ends; the number of the stop already picked in the list becomes a field (Enter = `moveStopTo`, Escape and Enter `preventDefault`ed so the sheet's dialog keys stand down); the shipped coastline (`loadLand`, the trip map's colours) is the ground at every open, hidden under OSM tiles. The panel's `MapField` follows the same three drop rules (`onReorder`, its arcs sampled as paths), writing ONCE on the drop rather than per pointer move — one undo step a drag. Before that day: a tap within 16 px of one of the trip's located places (hollow rings, listed first so they win a tie) or of a town takes it WITH its name — «Snap to towns» turns that off to drop exactly where tapped; an unnamed stop is OFFERED the nearest town within 30 km (`nameOffer`), never given it — the stop is the author's point; markers drag; the line joins stops in order as they come; a place search adds and flies. **Offline by construction**: the towns of `public/geo/cities.json` ARE the basemap — handed to MapLibre a VIEW at a time (`townsInView`: the gazetteer sorted by population once, the 500 biggest in bounds per move, so density follows the zoom; 107 000 features in a GeoJSON source would cost a phone's map worker tens of MB), and the ~26 names that fit written as HTML (`labelTowns`, avoiding each stop's marker and name) because MapLibre's text needs a glyph server, i.e. a request. The OSM background is the suite's tiles opt-in on a third surface — off at every first open, `TILES_TOGGLE` wording, kept for the TAB only, credit written in the corner (`OSM_CREDIT`), measured: zero tile requests until the toggle. Driven headless 2026-10-07: swap, ⌘Z, ⇧⌘Z, insert, move, an empty tap that changes nothing, a line tap, three undos back to the start, a typed number, Done stored. Verified headless at 1400 and by touch at 390 × 844: three taps make three stops, a tap on Perth's ring takes «Perth», a marker drag moves stop 1 only, Done writes, Escape writes nothing, Virée's «Your map» gets the same sheet. **Traps**: a MapLibre `Marker` is positioned through its element's own `style.transform`, so painting it with `style.cssText` threw it to the corner — the disc and name are CHILDREN; the map's `click` also fires for a press on a marker, so the handler returns when the target is inside `[data-stop-marker]`; `blockNativeZoom` goes on the map element (the pinch rule). Not driven: a real iPhone, a real tile response (this container cannot reach the tile server).

**Tiles INSIDE the openers, in the preview and the file (2026-09-28).** His ask, the same evening as the big map: *«add option to have the map texture loaded in the animation … the case we want the maps background in the export (and preview as well)»*. The Itinerary gains `basemap` + `basemapOpacity` (tiles clipped to the map's own box, an inset map; the white line's hint suggests a darker colour on OSM's light palette), Virée a third ground `tiles` («OSM map», over the paper at `basemapOpacity`, 0.9 by default, so the paper shows through; no graticule over tiles). **Rules, each for a reason**: (1) the variant never fetches — `wantsBasemap` declares a REGION and a raster size, keyed `basemapKey`, and `useHookPictures` loads it into `ctx.pictures`, which is why no renderer needed a line (stage, rail, PNG deck, both videos); (2) the openers do not draw in Mercator, so the mosaic is re-laid ROW BY ROW once into a latitude-linear raster (`tile-math.ts` `rowSource` — both projections are cylindrical, only rows differ) that lands by placing its two corners through the opener's own projection (`basemap-paint.ts`) — measured with fake tiles carrying red discs at three towns: under 1 px from the stops on a 1080 frame; drawn straight it would sit ~15 % off over Western Australia; (3) the Itinerary's region is the box's extent at a nominal 1080 frame plus a 5 % overscan, so dragging, resizing or re-anchoring the map never fetches again — only the stops and the frame's shape do; Virée's is the frame as the whole-route camera shows it, or the route with half a frame round it when the camera follows (NOT wider than the whole view — a follow camera shows less ground, in more detail, which a first test got wrong) — plus, since 2026-10-07, a zoom PYRAMID of finer tiles along the road, each frame at its own zoom («Finer tiles along a following camera»); (4) consent is the DEVICE's (`local-first.md`), asked by the same gesture that turns the background on; (5) the credit is painted in every frame the tiles show. Not driven: a real tile response (this container cannot reach the server — stubbed), a real H.264 export with tiles, a phone.

**A pinned picture's STEM is drawn under the dot (2026-09-15).** The maintainer, looking at a real piece: *"the picture stem should be displayed un[der] the place dot"*. It was painted with its tile, after the dots, so the hairline crossed the numbered disc and read as a line THROUGH the place rather than as a picture belonging to it. The pins are now LAID OUT before the stops are drawn (`layoutPins`) and painted in two halves on either side of them — stem under, tile over. The rule for anything that points at a marker: the marker is the last thing drawn where they meet.

**Traps, all three measured in the browser:**

- **A pin covers what is under it.** Two stops a degree apart put the second's dot (and the hop reaching it) under the first's photograph — Exmouth vanished from the first render. A pin spot is now rejected if it covers ANOTHER stop's dot, on top of the frame and other-pin tests; the placement order is above, right, left, below, then dropped. The names use the same rule and take the placed pins as `reserved` boxes, so a name never lands on a photograph. That placer moved to `label-place.ts` (route-plan re-exports it) — the `easing.ts` / `tick-kits.ts` pattern, on its second consumer.
- **An inline `<svg>` with no height attribute defaults to `height: 100%`**, which in an auto-height column collapses it and leaves `aspect-ratio` nothing to govern: the picking map drew as a 25px band. `height: auto` is load-bearing on it.
- **A numbered dot has to be big enough to read a numeral in**: at the un-numbered dot's radius × 1.55 the glyph came out at 12px on a 1080-wide frame.

**The chooser takes files from the computer too (2026-09-15, from the maintainer: *"in Pictures for the sweep modal: lets be able to also pick local images as well"*).** It offered what was SHOT in the span — the Library's photographs, dated from their EXIF, and the instance's — so a picture it could not date into the span meant closing the sheet, expanding the Library, dropping the file and reopening. "Add from this computer…" opens the ordinary picker, puts the photographs in the Library (the one place every surface resolves a picture from) and remembers them for the life of the sheet. **The rule that makes it work: a file the author picked by hand is CHOSEN, not found, so the span does not filter it** — it stays in the grid whatever day it was shot on, ticked on arrival even when a held list does not name it, and grouped under its own date (outside the trip it simply has no "Day N"). Clips and RAWs are refused rather than added invisibly: this grid is photographs only. Défilé still leaves a picture dated outside the trip off the tape and says so — that is its own reading of a day, not the chooser's.

**The chooser's dates reach the WHOLE trip, and a picture the opener will not use is marked, not hidden (2026-09-15, reverses "nothing shot after the piece is ever offered").** The maintainer: *"we are reducing in term of media choice which feels legit but there are case where I want to fetch beyond the leg date range"*. So a **Whole trip** pill leads the quick spans (widest first) and both date fields are bounded by `tripSpan`, not `reachSpan`; `reachSpan` now only decides what the chooser OPENS on. Défilé and Virée still drop a picture shot after the piece's day (`partitionPicked` — a sweep or a drive cannot tell a day not yet lived); rather than refuse to show it, the chooser labels its day and its tile "left off" and counts the ticked ones, unless the variant says `HookPictureChoice.keepsLater` (the Itinerary, whose stops are the author's). **Trap it closed**: `defaultSpan` bounds a HELD list by the trip too — bounded by reach, a held picture from past the piece reopened outside the span and was silently dropped on confirm, because confirm keeps only what the grid shows. The two date fields are the shared `DateRangeField`: the hand-rolled `1fr auto 1fr` grid left each inline field on the left of its column and the arrow floating in the gap.

**The chooser had to learn one thing** (`HookPanelHost.choosePictures` gained an optional `HookPictureChoice`, and `defaultSpan` an `includeThisDay`): a sweep is a RUN-UP to the piece, so it opens on the days BEFORE it; an itinerary's stops are as often the day being told, and a chooser that hides today's photographs reads as "there are none". A pick of several is generous rather than clever: the first lands on the stop it was asked from, the rest fill the stops AFTER it that have none — never one that already holds a picture, never one before it — and what had nowhere to go is COUNTED in the panel.

**The PNG deck and the rail's thumbnails had to be taught the opener's pictures.** `slide-render.ts` passed none, on the reasoning that a still is drawn SETTLED, past the sweep, where the scrub shows the piece's own picture. That holds for Défilé and fails for this one: an itinerary still shows its stops' photographs at rest, so both surfaces were drawing a map the stage did not show. `slideRender` now takes an optional decoded set, `renderDeck` and `useRailThumbs` pass it, and the rail's signature carries a WeakMap token of the picture MAP (a `ResolvedHook` is closures, and `JSON.stringify` of a decoded bitmap is nothing — the cell would keep the empty map it was first drawn with). Only the hook's cell is signed with it, or one picture landing would redraw a whole carousel. The video paths already worked: they take the `ResolvedHook` the stage prepared.

## The picture gets treated, not the typography (2026-08-24)

**Decision.** `BadgeBackdrop` (vignette + scrim) is painted in `badge-render.ts` between the picture and the badge, never as overlay elements: it is full-frame, the engine has no such concept, and a scrim is a property of THIS picture rather than of the composition. The scrim runs either across the whole frame from the badge's own edge or confined to the hook zone (`gradientBand`, pure and tested), and the confined band is derived from `badgeBlockExtent` — the same numbers the layout uses — because a gradient that does not line up with the text it exists to lift is worse than none. **Why darken the picture rather than panel every line**: a bright sky exactly where the hook sits is the normal case, and doing it optically keeps the typography clean.

## A stage is a LEG, and its places are an ordered list (2026-09-03)

**Decision, taken with the maintainer.** A stage carries `places: TripPlace[]` — a name, a region, optional `{lat, lon}` — **in the order they were lived**. He asked for "un lieu de début et un lieu de fin" and answered his own question in the asking: *"toujours une collection, mais on prendra le début et la fin en fonction du besoin"*. So `stageStart`/`stageEnd` are `places[0]` and the last (`trip-places.ts`), never two stored fields. **Why**: the same rule that keeps a trip's 310 days derived from two dates — a second copy of a fact is a second thing to migrate and a second thing to get wrong. With one place, start and end are the same place, which is the truth: you did not go anywhere.

**A place carries NO dates.** The stage is the dated unit; a place is a point inside it. "Uluru on the 12th" inside a nine-day leg means splitting the leg. A dated place would be a stage under another name, and `stageAt` would then have two things to listen to.

**`stage.name` empty = computed, never blank** — the `textOverrides` rule again. Empty derives "Perth → Cairns" from the two ends; a typed name always wins; clearing it gives the derived label back. `stageRegionLabel` derives only the region the places **agree** on: Perth and Cairns have no common region, and printing either would be a quiet lie about the other. The whole derivation is `trip-places.ts`, pure and tested; `day-badge.ts` reaches it in two lines and still refuses to invent a place when a stage names none.

**The separator is `→` (U+2192), not an emoji** — the same measured rule as the `◆` marker below: this string is drawn on a canvas through a font stack we do not control, and `📍` drew *nothing at all* where no colour-emoji font existed. A test pins the glyph.

**A trip's route is never entered anywhere but its legs** (2026-09-22). The New trip modal asked for it twice over: first as a free-text "Destination" ("Australia — Perth to Cairns") that nothing could read, then as a `From` / `To` pair that seeded one stage over the whole trip. Both are retired — see «Creation asks for the DATES and nothing else». `PlacesEditor` on a leg is the one place a place is typed.

## The legs are a RULER under the grid, one open at a time (2026-09-07)

**Decision, with the maintainer, from a three-direction design pass** (a timeline ruler · coloured bands over the grid plus a card rail · a split list-and-inspector; he chose the ruler). The stages accordion — every leg with every field, the widest thing on the page — is gone. `StageRuler` lays the legs along one horizontal track scaled to the trip's days, DaVinci-style; `StagesPanel` opens ONE leg beneath it; the geometry is pure (`shared/roadtrip/stage-ruler.ts`: bars in day offsets, greedy lanes for overlaps, the uncovered gaps, month rules) and the edits are pure (`stage-edit.ts`: resize an edge, slide a leg, start one on a day, cover a gap, the day's menu), so the component only turns pixels into whole days and hands over. **What a drag writes is the stage's two dates** — the same fields the date inputs edit — so the two can never disagree, and a leg cannot be dragged outside the trip because the trip IS the track.

**The grid and the ruler are one calendar seen twice, and the seam is four things.** Each day's cell wears its leg's tint as an inset stripe along its foot (`boxShadow: inset 0 -3px`, so the rung that says what was told is untouched); the open day is a playhead on the ruler; a click on a day a leg covers opens that leg, while a click on an uncovered day leaves the editor as it was rather than closing it; and a click on a LEG opens it and goes to the day it began. Selection lives in `TripOverview`, not the panel, because the calendar is the other way in. **Opening a leg says WHICH leg** (`onOpenStage(stage)`, never "select the day then derive"): on a travel day two legs overlap and `stageAt` answers with the later one, so deriving would open a different leg from the one clicked.

**A drag moves a leg and nothing else; a day is picked with a CLICK (2026-09-09, replacing the scrub).** The playhead used to be *scrubbed*: press the 16px head strip or the empty track, travel, and commit on release (a live `navigate` would have buried the day you came from under thirty history entries). The maintainer rejected the gesture on both counts, and the second is the load-bearing one — to receive those drags the scrub surface had to cover the whole track and set `touch-none`, so **a phone could not scroll a track that is wider than the screen by construction**, and every swipe meant to read the rest of the trip moved the day he was working on instead. Now: the surface is a plain `onClick` (`pickDay`) claiming no `touch-action`, so one finger scrolls the track exactly as it scrolls the calendar above; the playhead is a marker with arrow keys (a week with Shift) and no pointer drag; the release-deferred commit went with the gesture that needed it, since one click is one `navigate`. The general rule this fixed, and the `touch-pan-y` a leg's bar and edges now carry so a vertical swipe still scrolls the page, are in `frontend.md`, «A drag surface inside a scroll box claims only the axis it writes» — read it before adding any gesture here.

**A drag says the date it would land on** (`DatePin`): a chip following the pointer with the edge's new date and what the leg would then be ("7 Sep 2025 · 15 days"), both dates for a slide. It is `position: fixed` and clamped to the viewport for `DayCard`'s reason — the track scrolls sideways inside its own box, so a pin positioned inside it would be clipped by that box on the very drag that reaches its edge.

**"Start a stage here" inside a leg is a CUT**, the way a cut on a video timeline ends one clip where the next begins: the covering leg ends the day before and the new one takes its remaining days (`startStageAt`, tested). A leg that itself begins on that very day is left alone and the new one is a single day beside it. The menu (`dayStageActions`) names the real leg it would touch — "End “Perth → Kalbarri” here", or "End stage 2 here" for an unlabelled one, the same number the editor prints — never "this stage", which could be any of them; it does not offer to end a leg on the day it already ends. It is a right-click only: touch gets the ruler's `+` and the header's, and the hint under the ramp hides below 600px because it names a gesture a phone does not have.

**Four muted tints, never the accent** (`STAGE_TINTS`, oklch, same lightness and chroma, hue only): the accent means *selected* everywhere in the suite, and a leg must not look selected for being third. This is the one place the "one accent" identity admits a categorical palette, and it stays at 22% mixes and 3px stripes.

**Drag is back for places, and it is not drag-only.** `PlacesEditor` is a row of chips joined by the badge's `→`, reordered by HTML5 drag — the slide rail's own gesture — and, because drag is unreachable by keyboard, a focused chip moves with the arrow keys; so do a focused bar (slides) and edge (a day, a week with Shift). The earlier "buttons, not drag" call for places stands corrected by the maintainer's explicit ask for drag-and-drop; what survives of it is the rule that a drag surface always has a keyboard twin. A chip is dragged by its body; the ruler's bars swallow the click that ends a drag (`swallowClick`) so sliding a leg does not also toggle it. The ruler measures its own width with a `ResizeObserver` and lets a day shrink to 6px before the track scrolls inside its box — verified: 0px document overflow at 390px.

## The day's pictures come from the instance, keyed by the piece's own day (rev. 2026-09-20)

**Decision, from the maintainer's own report of the friction**: *"i have to pick the day that matches the current roadtrip element data manually"*, picked media *"pollutes the list"* once the piece moves on, and *"picked media from winnow are not persisted so every time i rerun the web app i have to reimport everything"*. Three complaints, and only the third was answered by the re-fetch (see «The picture follows the Library selection»). The other two are answered by **a post being keyed by the day it tells, so the day IS the query**: the surface asks the connected instance for `date_from = date_to = post.date`, no date is picked by hand, and it re-points itself when another piece is opened.

**What it deliberately is not**: a second pool, a bulk import, or a second browser. One picture crosses per click, at proxy fidelity; it lands in the ordinary Library through `addFiles` and is then made the active asset, so the existing Library ↔ slide machinery records it onto the slide and no second path to a piece's picture exists. The Winnow picker (*browse all*, `winnow-picker.md`) stays the way to bring a whole day in; a click on a day's tile is the way to dress ONE piece, and that is what stops the pool filling with a day the author has moved past.

**That surface is the LIBRARY'S Winnow tab, and only it (2026-09-20).** The strip that first carried it (`DayFromWinnow`, in the Picture tab) was **retired**: `PostEditor` publishes the piece's span through `usePublishMediaScope`, so the sidebar's tab asks `use-scope-rows` for exactly the same day, through the same `use-connection`, and fetches through the same `materialize` — one instance read drawn twice. The tab is a strict superset: it activates a row already in the pool instead of fetching it twice (`use-pick.ts`, which the strip never did), a tile DRAGS onto a collage cell, a click can open the lightbox, and it carries the Incoming/Gallery filter, the name filter and the neighbour-day stepper. What the strip alone had was discovery — it unfolded by itself when the slide had no picture — and that survives as a sentence in the Picture tab's ⓘ. **How to apply**: a Road Trip surface that wants an instance's media publishes its span and points at the Library; it does not draw tiles of its own. An "open the Library on its instance tab" seam was offered with the retirement and **declined** by the maintainer — do not add one unasked.

**The two traps that strip paid for are the suite's, not its own** — `aspect-square` on a `1fr` auto-fill track (2026-09-07) and `auto` grid rows dividing a definite height into 6px slices (2026-09-12). Both are recorded in `frontend.md` and both still bind `WinnowScopeGrid` and anything else drawing a tile grid.

**Road Trip is what makes the sidebar's tab follow a day at all**: `PostEditor` publishes the piece's `date`→`endDate ?? date` and `TripOverview` the selected day through `usePublishMediaScope` (`shared/sources/media-scope.tsx`), taken back on unmount. **How to apply**: a Road Trip screen that shows a day or a span publishes it; a stage/leg would publish `stage.startDate`→`stage.endDate` the same way — the sidebar needs no new code for that, only a publisher.

## The timeline is ASLEEP, and Atelier does not lean on it (2026-09-07)

**Decision, by the maintainer, and it settles a confusion worth naming.** Winnow's timeline is a young feature of another project; Atelier is mature. A mature tool must not depend on an immature one, so **`TIMELINE_SYNC_ENABLED` in `shared/sources/winnow/features.ts` is `false`** and every entry point disappears: the leg tab in the media browser, the "seed from" row in the New-trip modal, the "↓ From <host>" button over the stages, and the two link routes, which are consumed and land on the ordinary screen. `hasTimeline` reads the switch first, so one line closes them all and no immature route is ever called. The work is not deleted — `timeline-import.ts` and its specs, `TimelineImportPanel` and the wire reading stay tested and dormant, and flipping the switch restores the previous behaviour exactly, capability degradation included.

**The confusion the switch ends.** Four recent features were being read as one because a spec note mixed them: the timeline sync (this one, asleep), the Road Trip editor (done), **the Library sidebar synchronised with an instance** (the maintainer's actual request, and the least advanced), and the trip's persistence on an instance. The instruction that produced all this asked for **media of the day being edited**, never for chapters — and a day, or a stage, is a DATE RANGE, which Winnow has filtered on since long before its timeline. **How to apply**: anything about reaching a Winnow's media goes through `date_from` / `date_to` (the Winnow picker's day scope, `DayFromWinnow`), never through a chapter; a Road Trip stage carries its own two dates and needs nothing remote to say what it spans. Never put a media-reaching feature behind this switch.

## The itinerary is DEDUCED from one position per day, not from the media (2026-09-20)

**Decision, with the maintainer**, answering the friction that was keeping him from using the tool: building a trip's legs by hand costs **8–10 interactions each** (create, name, two dates, add a place, pass the Nominatim consent, search, pick) — ~300 gestures for a three-and-a-half-month Australia trip, preceded by archaeology to remember where he was on a given day.

**The premise, and it is what makes the feature small: a journey's shape is not in its fifty thousand media, it is in ONE POSITION PER DAY.** ~100 days → ~30 legs → ~30 decisions. The volume never enters the arithmetic, no media byte is fetched, and the input for a whole trip is a few kilobytes. A view that listed *unprocessed media* as a backlog was considered and refused by the maintainer himself: most media will never be published, so it would turn an editorial choice into a debt and ask for fifty thousand refusals. The unit stays the day and the leg — the grid's holes.

**This is NOT the timeline and must never go behind `TIMELINE_SYNC_ENABLED`** (the rule above, applied): the deduction reads positions over a DATE RANGE, like the Winnow picker's day scope and `DayFromWinnow`. The dormant panel is not woken; a new one is written beside it.

**It needs no new model and no migration.** `timeline-import.ts` already turns `TimelineChapter[]` into stages, `diffTimeline` pairs id → span → first place (→ contained since 2026-09-29), `applyTimelineDiff` writes only what was ticked — and `TimelineChapter` is *Atelier's own shape, not the wire's*. So the whole feature is **a second PRODUCER** of that shape: `day-track.ts` → `segment-track.ts` → `track-chapters.ts` → the existing arithmetic, untouched. `StageOrigin` has existed since v11; the document stays at v25.

**A leg is a run of consecutive days whose position stays within a radius of the run so far.** No DBSCAN and no clustering library: the literature's stop detection is built for 1 Hz traces with noise, while this is fed one point per day already reduced to a median. At that sampling a threshold on a run is exact, pure, testable and readable — a dependency would buy a black box. The radius is a setting, never a constant (the maintainer's call).

**Blind days do NOT cut a run, and that is the load-bearing decision.** Winnow measured it (2026-09-20, 641 days of the library): 77 % of days carry a position today, 83 % after the batch geotagging, and **17 % stay blind for good** — one day in six, scattered. A run that closed on a positionless day would break a ten-day stay at Broome into three legs and make the proposal list useless. So two treatments, and the difference is moral, not technical: **bridging** (default ON) COVERS a blind day whose neighbours belong to the same run — nothing is invented, because a leg is a SPAN and "the 3rd to the 12th at Broome" claims nothing about where day 7 was shot; **interpolating a move** (default OFF) mints positions across a hole between two *different* places, which is a fabrication, so it is opt-in and everything it makes is marked. One predicate decides both questions — a hole is bridged exactly when the day after it would have joined the run anyway — or a hole ends up belonging to neither side. Every leg reports `bridged`, so a span held up by two fixes a month apart says so and the author judges it.

**A short halt is LISTED and marked, never hidden** (the maintainer: *"liste mais on laisse le choix non?"*). The threshold is therefore a **classification, not a filter**: a leg under it is proposed as a leg of its own, marked "étape de route", and unticked by default; a `shortLegs: 'merge'` setting folds them into the halt they were on the way to instead. The choice exists twice — globally and row by row. Merge only ever folds into an **adjacent** leg: merging across a blind gap would make a halt claim days nothing supports, which is exactly what bridging is careful not to do.

**The walk uses a running mean, the NAME uses a median.** Two numbers for two jobs, on purpose: the walk needs an online estimate, while the place a leg is named after must not move because one travel day slipped into the run. Same reason Winnow computes a day's own position as a median of its fixes.

**Provenance is read, never recomputed, and never votes on geometry.** Winnow's `gps_source` has three values (NULL measured · `manual` authored · `inferred` batch-guessed) and its per-day aggregate says `inferred` for a day resting only on guesses — computed from the measured frames alone when a day has any, so eight hundred batch-placed Sony frames cannot drown five real iPhone fixes. **How to apply**: treat an inferred day as a day to bridge OVER, not as evidence of where a leg is — it may have been guessed from a neighbouring folder, and a neighbour can be a day of driving. A leg whose every day is inferred is marked and unticked, the same treatment as a short one.

**Null Island is refused** (`readDayPoint`), like `parsePosition` does for a drone cue: `0, 0` is what a camera writes with no fix, and one such day drags a leg into the Gulf of Guinea. A date that is not a calendar day is refused too, never sliced out of an instant.

**Do not write a third `haversineKm`.** Two already exist (`hooks/geo.ts:118`, and a copy in `hooks/map-plan.ts:521`); `hooks/geo.ts` is the one to import — it has no imports at all, is pure and DOM-free, and already declares `GeoPoint`.

**A leg is NAMED from an index that ships with the app, never from a lookup going out** (`gazetteer.ts` pure + `load-gazetteer.ts` fetching, the `saved-grade.ts` / `restore-grade.ts` split, for the same reason: `import.meta.env` drags a test run into the bundler's world). The suite's one place lookup is an opt-in exception carrying **text the author typed**; reverse-geocoding a deduced leg would send **the coordinates of their photographs** instead — a larger claim on someone's data, for a name. So `scripts/gen-gazetteer.mjs` builds `public/geo/cities.json` from GeoNames `cities1000` (135 233 rows, 6.5 MB, 2.6 MB gzipped since the 2026-09-29 regeneration — `deployment.md`; fetched only when a leg needs naming — never at boot), and the README's two network exceptions are unchanged — the callout now SAYS so (2026-09-20), since a reader who has just read "a leg is named from its coordinates" will ask. `cities1000` and **not** `cities15000`: Kalbarri is 2 602 people and Broome 5 314, and a 15 000 floor loses both. **GeoNames is CC BY 4.0** and the attribution rides inside the generated file — do not strip it.

**Nearest is not the right answer, and two real cases prove it — both measured against the built index, not reasoned about.** At Perth's centre the nearest row is **Northbridge** (a suburb, 1 434 people) with Perth itself slightly further, so distance alone names the suburb. At Broome the nearest row IS the town, but GeoNames gives its suburb **Cable Beach 8 529** against Broome's **5 314**, so population alone names the suburb too. What gets both right is GeoNames' `featureCode`: `PPLX` is "section of populated place" and only 4 817 rows of 135 233 carry it. So the contest, within a 5 km tie window, is **a place beats a SECTION of a place → bigger population → nearer → name**, and the last rung makes the answer total. A section is still named when nothing else is near.

**The tie-break runs in TWO passes on purpose.** A one-pass version reads shorter and quietly returns two different names for two orderings of the same index — the nearest has to set the bar before anything can compete against it. The generated file is sorted by name for the same family of reason: a regenerated index must diff as the rows that changed, not as a reshuffle.

**A bounding box in degrees goes before any trigonometry.** 135 000 cities against thirty legs is four million great-circle solves for thirty names; the box makes it 1 ms per lookup (measured). It only has to be a superset — `haversineKm` settles what is really inside. Near a pole and for a window spanning the globe the longitude bound stops meaning anything, so it is dropped rather than computed wrong, and the longitude gap is taken the short way round so a town at 179.97°E is not lost from a point at 179.98°W.

**The name a deduction offers goes into the PLACES, and into `stage.name` only on request** (`track-chapters.ts`, the seam to `timeline-import.ts`; revised 2026-09-29 with the chapters). Each halt is named FIRST (its city says its region), then grouped, and a chapter's places are its halts' cities in lived order, **once each at the first visit** — compared on the lower-cased name, the key `applyTimelineDiff` merges on, so a loop back to Perth does not add a second Perth. `TimelineChapter.title` is null unless the panel's `nameByRegion` is ticked: an empty stage name means COMPUTED and the label derives («Perth → Broome»); «Western Australia» as a name is a decision, offered and never written by default. A halt nobody can name gives **no place at all**, not an empty one. The country and the region KEY stay OUT of the document (machine tokens); what a place carries is its region's NAME in `TripPlace.region`, which is what makes `stageRegionLabel` answer for a multi-place chapter. A chapter is keyed `track:<its first halt's startDate>` (the prefix so it can never collide with an instance's timeline ids, ISO instants) and its `revision` is span + volume + halt count. **A chapter is held back (unticked) only when EVERY halt in it is short or inferred** — one stop on the way inside six real halts is a place, not a reason to untick the region.

**Halts are grouped into CHAPTERS by region, and the chapter becomes the stage (2026-09-29, the maintainer's call, reversing «one halt = one leg»).** Thirty halts made a ruler of thirty bars in four repeating tints and thirty rows to accept; he tells the trip in chapters and wants the badge on a Kalbarri day to say «Western Australia». So `group-legs.ts` (pure) groups finished halts by `GazetteerCity.regionKey` (GeoNames admin1, `AU.08`), and the halts become the chapter's PLACES. **A wider radius was not the answer**: `segmentTrack` compares against the RUNNING MEAN, so a region-sized radius lets a run drift across a continent. Rules: a chapter is a CONSECUTIVE run (WA → NT → WA is three chapters, a span cannot hold two stays with another between); **an unknown region is not a new region** — a halt with no city, or a city with no region, joins the chapter it sits in, and a chapter that starts on such halts adopts the first key that follows; the KEY groups, never the name (202 keys have no name, and two Perths exist). **Known cost, accepted knowingly**: Western Australia is ~2 500 km tall, so Perth and Broome share a chapter; `maxHopKm` (off by default) is the seam to split on a hop later — do not re-propose a distance criterion as the default. **This depends on the badge saying the chapter**: a `TripPlace` has no dates and `stageAt` returns a stage, never a place, so if the badge must ever say the HALT again, chapters stop being viable.

**A reconcile only ADDS to a stage it met, and a chapter inside a longer stage COMPLETES it (2026-09-29, `timeline-import.ts`, both producers).** The maintainer wants a deduced region to fill a leg he already drew rather than double it, so `diffTimeline` gained a fourth pass after id → span → first place: `contained`, the incoming span inside an unmatched stage, and of several the TIGHTEST (shortest, then earliest, then id — never first-found, which is what the other passes' `find` over a `Set` would give). Every match now writes a MERGE, carried on `DiffEntry.outcome` so the list describes what will really be written: the stage's places first in the author's order, then the incoming ones it does not name (lower-cased name); the span is the UNION, so it never shrinks; an empty incoming name keeps the author's. The plan asked for the union on contained matches only; it is universal because a contained match gets its origin stamped, and the NEXT run then meets it by id — a replace-on-id would propose shrinking the author's leg back to the chapter, ticked by default. Replacing places also used to erase every place added by hand. **How to apply**: taking days or places away from a leg is the ruler's job or an explicit `dropped` row's, never a `changed` one's; a derived label still reports `name` when a new last place moves it. Known limit: two chapters inside ONE long stage — the first in lived order takes it, the second is an `add` that overlaps it.

**The wire is `GET /api/assets/geo?date_from&date_to&by=day` → `{ days: [{date, lat, lon, count, measured, source}] }`, READ against Winnow's own `src/app/api/assets/geo/route.ts` (2026-09-20) and not assumed** — the route's header calls it a cross-repo contract, to be changed additively or not at all. Reading it corrected three things a guess had wrong, and the third changed the design: the answer is an ENVELOPE (`{ days }`, not a bare array); provenance arrives as `source: 'measured' | 'inferred' | null`, a STRING, not a boolean; and **a day holding media with no position at all is still sent**, with null coordinates — Winnow calls it a *declared gap*. That last one is why **one request answers the whole question** and the `calendar` call the plan carried is gone: "no data for this day" and "no media that day" are different answers, and only the first appears in the response at all. Reading both from one response is also what stops the two counts ever disagreeing.

**Never send `has_gps` on that route.** It is a legal, cumulative filter and it would drop exactly the gap rows — leaving a trip that looks fully placed while a sixth of its days were never known. A client test pins the ENTIRE query string, the rule `geocode.ts` already follows, so a later "just narrow it a bit" cannot slip past review.

**Winnow's own rules the client relies on, so do not re-derive them here**: `date` is `capture_date` verbatim (never recomputed from the instant — both repos hold that rule for the same reason); `lat`/`lon` are the MEDIAN of the day's trustworthy rows alone whenever it has any, so five real fixes decide a day that also holds eight hundred bulk-accepted suggestions; `count` includes RAW+JPEG companions (the branch does not collapse groups); `measured` counts the non-inferred positions. `WinnowGeoDay`'s field names deliberately MATCH what `day-track.ts` reads and **neither module imports the other** — the arrangement `WinnowChapter` already has with `TimelineChapter`, so the generic client never learns what a trip is and the reader stays pure.

**The panel is its OWN (`DeduceStagesPanel`), never `TimelineImportPanel` woken up.** That one is coupled to `hasTimeline`, which `TIMELINE_SYNC_ENABLED` forces false; entangling a live feature with a dormant one would blur what the switch means. What the two shared until 2026-10-02 was `StageDiffList` (it draws `DiffEntry` and nothing else; `noteFor` and `holdBack` are how a producer speaks on a row) — the timeline panel's still; Deduce now proposes through `deduce-draft.ts` (verbs, not ticks — the entry below) and no longer reaches `diffTimeline`. **The entry is a `Deduce` button in the TRIP'S BAR (since 2026-10-02: the stages header it first lived in is not drawn beside the map, so the map view had no way in — his report; the phone keeps it in the legs sheet, whose bottom-bar cell exists in both views), offered for EVERY connected instance** — answering for a day's position is a date filter, not a capability, so nothing is gated on `hasTimeline` and nothing is asked until the button is pressed.

**Moving a slider must never re-ask the instance.** The one request is made on open and every setting recomputes from the days already read — measured in the browser, where widening the radius from 25 to 120 km and switching the short-halt mode left the request count at two (two, not one, because React's StrictMode double-invokes the effect in dev; the cleanup cancels the first). A change of setting also DROPS the author's ticks back to the defaults: the old keys name rows that may no longer exist, and carrying them would silently accept a leg nobody looked at.

**Two rows are held back by default and marked**: a short halt, and a leg every day of which rests only on guessed positions. They are the two the arithmetic is least sure of, and both are still listed — the maintainer's own rule that a short halt is *listed, with the choice*, never hidden. **The settings are `localStorage` (`atelier.roadtrip.deduce`), never on the document** — a `.roadtrip.json` does not carry how one machine was being driven.

**Verified in headless Chromium against a stubbed instance** (the `testing.md` recipe): over eight days holding Perth ×3 + a declared gap + Perth, a one-day stop and Kalbarri ×3 (the last `inferred`), the panel says «8 days · 7 placed · 1 with media and no position», proposes **Perth 2→5 Nov** (the blind day BRIDGED, not cutting the halt), an unticked one-day «Jurien Bay» carrying its note, and **Kalbarri 7→9 Nov**; «Fold it in» gives two legs; 0 px document overflow at 390 px; no console error. **Never driven against a real instance** — this container cannot authenticate to one.

**The panel proposes CHAPTERS and says both grains (2026-09-29).** Its coverage line ends «N chapters, N halts»; the radius slider still sets the HALT, which became the internal grain; a checkbox «Name each chapter after its region» (in `atelier.roadtrip.deduce` with the other settings, off by default) is the one way a region's name reaches `stage.name`. Driven in headless Chromium against the stub over twelve days in two regions (Perth · a stop · Kalbarri, then Darwin · Katherine): two rows, «Perth → Kalbarri» and «Darwin → Katherine East», becoming «Western Australia» and «Northern Territory» with the box ticked, no request added by the box or a slider, 0 px overflow at 390 px. The contained match and the place merge were NOT driven in a browser (making a hand-drawn stage through the UI first) — the specs hold them.

**`download.geonames.org` is refused by the agent container's network policy** (403 at the CONNECT, not a TLS fault). npm is reachable, and `cities-with-1000` ships GeoNames' `cities1000.txt` verbatim, so the generator reads either a dump path given on argv or that mirror — same output both ways, nothing installed, nothing added to `package.json`. The tarball is unpacked by a ~30-line tar reader rather than a dependency, the way this repo already prefers (hand-rolled IndexedDB over Dexie).

**The Deduce MODAL is THREE WINDOWS over ONE DRAFT — le grain, le calque, le paquet — BUILT 2026-10-02 (`src/tools/roadtrip/deduce/`), from the maintainer's asks of 2026-10-01/02.** Auto-adding stages *«n'est pas pratique, ça pollue l'interface»*; he wanted the three modals of the first lab (https://claude.ai/artifact/CqJxtGoshFUDmWjEcFnZkF) exactly, as tabs, one at a time, and asked how one passes from one to the other (*«je les veux toutes»*). Two readings were REJECTED on the way: the merged «ladder» (https://claude.ai/artifact/1XsmHQ1MU6kiBuXeskJZdb) and «Simple · Step by step · Advanced» (https://claude.ai/artifact/1T7Kxvu52Zffzjkwsw5YsD). The lab it was built from: https://claude.ai/artifact/VgXgFD92rBxAjFpeATcWWJ. **What the code does not say.** (1) The three are TABS of the modal (`Segmented`, keys A B C) and three VIEWS of one draft (`deduce-draft.ts`): a verb per proposal, the edits, the names given to halts — keyed on the proposal's SPAN (`start..end`) and not on the chapter id, which is its start date alone and would carry an answer onto a block that now ends elsewhere; so a grain change keeps every answer the new cut still covers. A hand-off carries the chapter under the hand — a grain card's *Deck ›* opens the paquet ON it, *Against yours ›* (only where it overlaps) the calque on its row (the intent widened to *Show everything* if it would hide it), a paquet card's *In the list ›* finds and focuses its card, outlined for 1.6 s; the keys do the same with the hovered or open chapter. The draft pill under the title counts what is set and clears it. (2) The GRAIN is four cuts over the same halts (`deduce-grain.ts`: regions · long drives (default, his call) · big halts · halts), `trackChapters` taking a `grain` and behaving as before without one. (3) The SAFE verb never writes over a stage the author drew: identical/inside → *into* its missing places else *already in the trip*; partial → *only the free days*; free → *add*; *Add over* by hand only, flagged red, named in Review. The calque's three intents are FILTERS over that one draft (fill = free/partial, enrich = has a target lacking places — the default, his call; all = everything), a tick being the verb the grain's card shows. (4) NOTHING is written before the Review pane; `Write` is the only writing verb and goes through `onWrite` (the shell's `handleChange` + span note), the modal STAYING open on the Written pane with Undo (the tool's own history, `history.undo`), Run again and Close; a chapter skipped on purpose stays skipped across a write (`keepSkips`). A written stage's mark is its `origin.chapterId` starting with `track:` — no schema change — which is what *Remove the N stages Deduce added* (the ⋯ menu, undoable) and a re-run read (`already in the trip · written by Deduce`); a stage completed keeps its id, name, span and NO origin: it is the author's. (5) The library's flaws are SAID — a strip under the title, a Data pane behind it: days without a position, a position far from its neighbours (`deduce-outliers.ts`, > 1 500 km from both neighbours that are < 150 km apart — a drone that kept the GPS of home —, IGNORED by default and covered as a blind day, keepable, and kept it cuts the stage in three), halts the index cannot name (named for this trip only, written on the place with the halt's measured centroid, never to the instance). The two names/one place case of the lab does not arise: names come from the gazetteer. (6) The grain's map is an SVG (`DeduceMap.tsx`, the coastline of `load-land.ts`), STICKY beside the cards and on top on a phone — a moving surface since 2026-10-06, next entry. (7) The thresholds stay in `localStorage` under the old key with the new fields beside the old (`settings.ts`); the lab proposed keeping them ON THE TRIP, still his to decide, as are: the window at opening (the last used per device, the grain first), *Into*'s order (after his places; the picture-order option of the lab was dropped — his places carry no dates to sort by), whether a halt named here may also be written to Winnow, whether *Add over* is offered at all. **Verified** headless in Chromium at 1280 and 390 (touch, dark) against a stubbed instance (the scratchpad's `drive-deduce.mjs`: a seeded trip with one hand-drawn stage, a blind day, an outlier, a nameless halt): every hand-off, the keys, the sticky map, the edit, the Data pane's keep/ignore and rename, Review → Write → store, Undo, Run again, Remove — 0 px overflow, no console error. Not on his phone. **Design pass, 2026-10-02 (his «très encombrée … beaucoup de texte», measured against the 20 UX laws he shared — `docs/deduce-ux-laws.md`)**: the tabs are NAMED by what they do, in the words the hand-offs already used — **All stages · Against mine · One by one** (the ids stay `grain`/`calque`/`paquet`, and so do the memory's names); the head is the title and ONE row (tabs · ⓘ with the keys · a chip for what was read, opening the Data pane · the draft chip, drawn only once there is a draft); no standing subtitle, key line or legend sentence (they are tooltips and the ⓘ); a card says its verb ONCE, the select wearing the pill's tone, warn only when over a stage of his; bulk verbs are one *All…* menu; an empty Review section is not drawn; *Written* opens on one line. **How to apply**: a new control or sentence in this window must replace one, not add one — the measure is what stands between the title and the first stage (ten before the pass, seven after) and whether a phone shows a stage on its first screen.

**Starting the legs over lives in Trip settings (2026-10-09, his ask after a Deduce run on wrongly-dated days: «wipe les stages et/ou lieux placés … repartir de zéro … dans trip settings»).** Three widths in `stage-reset.ts` (pure): `deduced` (= Deduce's own *Remove what Deduce added*, the `track:` mark), `places` (every leg keeps id, dates, name, region, origin; places emptied), `stages` (none). **Where**: the overview's *Trip settings* button opens `TripDetailsModal` (titled «Trip dates»), not `TripSettingsModal` — that one is only reachable from a piece's ⚙ and never shows the legs — so the row is drawn there, under the cover, editing only. **Rules**: a verb is drawn only when it changes something (`canReset`), and `deduced` is hidden when Deduce wrote every leg (it would say the same as *Remove every leg*); each asks through a `danger` `ConfirmDialog` with the trip's counts and a backup link; it writes the STORED trip, closing the sheet and dropping its draft dates; one `onChange`, so the trip's undo takes it back in one step (driven headless: clear → ⌘Z → places back). Never touched: pieces (none points at a leg), the span, `stateCodes`, the place-writing settings. **Trap**: `useDialogKeys` binds on `window`, so a confirm over a sheet makes Escape close BOTH — the sheet hands its keys down (`onCancel` undefined, `onConfirm` null) while the question is up.

**A place says its COUNTRY on its own line, a stage's places are a TABLE, and the Deduce map MOVES — BUILT 2026-10-06 (his asks; lab https://claude.ai/artifact/R4UyXo9JRF5cNwNiG9o4C5, v1 three faces, v2 his pick B).** His report: towns put on the wrong continent by the search (Exmouth → Devon, Albany → New York) showed only as far points on a map. **Decisions.** (1) **ONE formatter for every list of places**: `placeLine` (`place-style.ts`: the cascade's text, `stateText` — the code, or the state in full when the writing says so —, the country code, its name by `Intl.DisplayNames`, so no country table ships either) and, for a Deduce halt, `haltText` / `routeLabel(halts, draft, trip)`; the windows, panes and map labels of Deduce go through them (`pieces.tsx`'s private «name, state» formatter is gone). The calendar's bars and the ruler keep `stageLabel` (no room). (2) **Variant B, chosen over chips (A) and by-country groups (C)**: `PlacesTable.tsx`, shared by `ProposalEditor` (Deduce) and `PlacesEditor` (stage card — the chips and their drag are retired, ↑ ↓ replace them): #, place (+ dates chip), state, country (pill + name), distance, verbs; state and distance fold under the name in a `@max-[30rem]` container. **The verbs are bare glyph buttons with no outline (`op` in the file), never `Button`** — he asked the build to look exactly like the lab, where a borderless Fix was the thing he pointed at. (3) **A place is SUSPECT** (`place-oddity.ts`) when its country is not the trip's (`tripCountry`, the majority code) AND it is > `ODD_KM` (500) from its reference — a halt's pictures in Deduce, the rest of its stage on a card (`stageReference`, preferring same-country neighbours so one wrong town does not drag the centre and make its neighbours look far, the bug the first headless run showed). A stopover abroad photographed there is not suspect; distance alone never suspects a town at home. It only SAYS (orange line, ⚠); nothing is moved. (4) **Fix** (`PlaceFixPanel.tsx`): Same name — `homonyms()` over the SHIPPED index, nearest the reference (never the place's own coords, which are the wrong town's), offline — · Search · By hand. On a card a pick REPLACES the town whole (`replacePlace`: name, state, area, codes, country, coords; keeps id, dates, writing, an own code for the same state); in Deduce it is the draft's `places[haltKey]` (`HaltPick`, `choosePlace`, read first by `haltName`/`haltPlace`; `renames` stays the Data pane's), written on Write, never to the instance. (5) **The search is bounded to the trip's country** (`nominatimUrl(…, countryCodes)` → `countrycodes=au`, the URL test pins it; `PlaceSearchField` `country` prop, «Search everywhere» beside the answers) — recommended in the lab and taken for want of an answer, his to overrule. (6) **The Deduce map is a SURFACE**: `map-view.ts` (pure: Mercator unit square, fit, zoom-about, pan, van Wijk's flight = MapLibre's `flyTo` curve) + `useZoomGestures` (`wheel: 'any'`, `touch-none`, ± and frame buttons at every width); the coastline is one path in world units under a transform. It flies when what it FRAMES changes (Edit on a grain card — the grain now passes the edited proposal as `focus` —, the paquet's card, a place chosen) and never otherwise, so his pan survives hovering. **It frames REALITY — his rule, reversing the lab's proposal to frame only what agrees**: every place of the stage, a pick abroad included, even if that is the world; such a pick is drawn in orange where it claims to be, dashed to its halt's pictures. **But the reality of what will be WRITTEN (rev. 2026-10-07, his «une carte finale»)**: `deduce-map.ts` — a proposal HE answered Skip, a halt he left out (×) and the outlier days are neither drawn nor framed (a SAFE skip stays: those days are already his stages), so skipping the foreign blocks brings the frame back to the trip; the proposal looked at (`focus`, or `hot` for drawing only) is kept whatever its answer. A route stroke joining two different proposals is the TRAVEL between them, never the next stage's own first line — faint and dotted on the whole route, dropped when one stage is framed (his report: a China day before «Albany → Kalamunda» drew a green line out of the frame, read as the stage's). **The window is wide on a computer** (`calc(100vw - 4rem)`, ≤ 100rem — still a modal, the backdrop showing): from an 880 px body the grain splits 50/50, everything to read on the left, the map on the right `sticky` at `h-[100cqh]` (the body is a `container-type: size`) and drawn with `fill`, its viewBox taking the box's aspect (a resize re-places the frame unless the hand moved it); the paquet's map is `fill` at `clamp(15rem, 40cqh, 28rem)`. **`PlacesTable` never scrolls sideways** (his «léger scroll», reproduced at 300 and 500–590 px with his twelve places): no gutter before # or after the verbs, the country's name only from 38rem, its code joining the line under the name below 24rem, glyph verbs 24 px under a mouse — `table-sweep` in the scratchpad mounts it at 280–700 px. **How to apply**: a new surface that lists places reads `placeLine` and draws `PlacesTable`; never frame a map on «what agrees» — show what is and say why it is wrong; never frame what the author has thrown out. **Not built**: the overview map (`TripMapView`) still centres an opened stage only when it is off screen (the 2026-09-29 rule), and the Deduce map shows no ghost of a candidate under the hand (the lab did). Driven headless on a harness at 900 and 390 (table, Fix, picks on both surfaces, fly whole → edit → fixed, wheel + drag with the page still) — not inside the real modal against an instance, not on his Mac or phone.

**A PLACE keeps what it knows, may carry DATES, and says its STATE — BUILT 2026-10-02, v30 (his asks of 2026-10-01; lab https://claude.ai/artifact/An3BHLcQsRfxUz8p4kHTeF).** `TripPlace` was `name` · `region` (one free string the search filled with «Western Australia, Australia») · `coords`; it is now `name` · `state` · `coords` plus optional `area`, `country`, `countryCode`, three codes (`stateCode` the author's own, `searchCode` the search's, `codeFrom` a pin), `arrived` / `left` / `dateFrom`, `source`, `style`. The v30 migration splits the old string on its last comma into a state and a country (one part stays the state — nothing says whether «Japan» was a country) and deletes `region`; `TripStage.placeStyle?` and `TripDoc.placeStyle {badge, lists}` + `TripDoc.stateCodes` are additive and portable. **Rules**: (1) the WRITING cascades like the look — `placeStyleFor`: place → stage → trip, two trip defaults, `badge: 'name'` so no stored badge changes and `lists: 'code'`; `writePlace` degrades to what the place has (no code → the state, no state → the name), never a dangling comma; `stageRoute` says a shared state once («Kalbarri → Exmouth, WA») and is `stageLabel` on the badge by default — it is what `counterPieces`, the legs sheet, the day panel and the map's labels read, while the calendar's bars and the ruler keep `stageLabel`. (2) **The trip's own table first, then a SMALL shipped one** — his first call was none («j'ai peur que ça fasse lourd»), reversed for three countries on 2026-10-07 («corrige aussi QLD», Brisbane reading QUE on the map): `state-codes.ts` holds the official AU/US/CA abbreviations (≈ 70 lines, matched by the state's NAME within the place's country, else the first country listing it — the name is what tells Western Australia from Washington); every other country still derives, and a new country goes in only on a real trip's need. `stateCodes` is the TRIP's own correspondence, keyed by the state's name (looked up case- and accent-insensitively), filled from a place («Keep QLD for Queensland on this trip», offered whenever the resolved code is not the table's) or in Trip settings → Places, where every state the places name is a row with the code it reads today and its source. Resolution `stateCodeFor`: a pin (`codeFrom`) wins when its rung has a value, else own → table → search → OFFICIAL (`officialStateCode`) → DERIVED (`deriveStateCode`: initials minus small words, three letters for one word — right for NSW/PACA, wrong for Île-de-France→IF, which is why it is said out loud as the last rung and why the trip's table exists). (3) **Dates are a bonus**: both optional, `dateFrom: 'photos'` written by `haltPlace` from the halt's two days (a dashed chip), `'hand'` from the editor; a date outside the stage is kept and shown in orange (`datesOutsideStage`), never corrected; `+ date` only appears while there is none. This REVERSES «a place has no dates of its own» — the stage stays the dated thing `stageAt` and the badge count inside; a place's dates are facts, not a second span. (4) The search asks `addressdetails=1` (`geocode.ts`, `placeDetails`: `county|state_district|municipality` → area, `state|province|region` → state, `ISO3166-2-lvl4`'s second half → the code, a code with no dash dropped) and `adoptSearchResult` (`place-search.ts`) never overwrites a typed field, fills what was empty, writes nothing where the answer had nothing, and always takes the latest `searchCode`. (5) «AU» lands in `countryCode` — the field made for a code — and never in `country`; `haltPlace` and «Situer» write `source: 'deduced'`. **Traps**: `place-style.ts` must import only TYPES from `trip-types.ts` (which imports its `DEFAULT_PLACE_STYLE`) — a value both ways left `day-badge.ts` half-loaded in four suites; a test helper that lists `TripDoc`'s fields by hand needs the two new ones. **Not built, deliberately**: the house style does not carry `placeStyle` yet; the Studio and the openers do not read a place's dates (his example use, a map opener writing each stop's date and the days between, waits); a place's style select lives under «More…» only. Driven headless at 1280 and 390 (migration on open, chips, the code row and «Keep», a date outside the stage, the settings table) — not seen on his Mac or phone.

**A Polarsteps export is a THIRD producer of the day track and the FIRST source of names — BUILT 2026-10-08 (`polarsteps.ts` pure, `deduce/PolarstepsChip.tsx`, his ask after measuring his year in Australia).** Measured on his real export and Winnow day track (counts only; the files never enter the repo): positions were right, NAMES wrong in kind — the index's nearest town of ≥ 1 000 people named an island after the town across the water (35–56 km), a gorge after a suburb 79–113 km off, a rock after the town 125 km away, a monument after the town 102 km up the road, and desert days nothing at all; Polarsteps held those very places, named by him, each with its IANA zone; and ~12 % of his track's fixes have a UTC day that is not their local day (Winnow files a capture on its UTC day — fixed on Winnow's side in parallel), which walks mornings onto the day before. Both sources agree within 25 km on ~80 % of shared days; the > 100 km gaps are travel days only. **Rules.** (1) **The day is the LOCAL day of the step in force** — the zone of the last step begun at or before the fix, the first step's before any; then the trip's `timezone_id`; then (no `trip.json` at all) the SOLAR zone of the fix's longitude, said on the chip — read with `Intl.DateTimeFormat({timeZone})`, formatters cached per zone, no dependency; never UTC. (2) **Merge per day: track > instance MEASURED > step's own position > instance INFERRED** (`mergeDays`); an instance blind day placed by the export stops being blind; a day keeps the INSTANCE's media `count` whoever places it (an export day alone counts 0 — a fix is not a picture, the first drive showed «103 pictures» for 100); `DayPoint.from` (optional, `'instance' | 'track' | 'step'`) says who placed it, counted in the chip's tooltip and the Data pane. The track wins because it is the densest record on the day's own clock; a guessed instance day loses to a step because the step is authored. (3) **A step beats the index for the NAME, never for the grouping**: `trackChapters({ steps })` sets `NamedLeg.step` = the step begun on one of the halt's days nearest its centroid within `STEP_KM` (= the index's 90 km), earliest of equals (`stepFor`); `haltName` reads Fix pick → step → city → rename; `haltPlace` writes the step's position, name, `area`, `country`, `countryCode`, `source: 'polarsteps'` (an ADDITIVE `PlaceSource` value — no document version moved, `PlacesEditor` says *from Polarsteps*), and the state the export gave else the index city's region. The CITY stays on the halt because its `regionKey` is what groups halts into chapters — a step's state is a name, not a key, and keying on it would cut chapters differently from a run without the export. «Unnamed» now means `!city && !step`. (4) `full_detail` is split by `splitDetail`: the part before the country is the STATE only if `state-codes.ts` knows it (AU/US/CA), else an AREA with the state unknown (a locality before the country is common in a real export, and 75 of his 181 steps carry only «Australia»); a country with no table takes it as the state. (5) **Reading**: a file is told by its CONTENT (`all_steps` / `locations`), a zip is refused with «unzip it», a folder drop reads only `trip.json`/`locations.json` when present (an export also holds `user.json` and photos), the two halves may arrive in two drops, and of a multi-trip export the file of each kind covering the most of THIS trip's days is kept (`addPolarstepsFiles(…, span)`). Deleted steps, no time, no position and Null Island are skipped and counted. (6) **Local-first**: one-shot input in the panel's state, never persisted, never on the document, no request; a drop anywhere on the window or the chip's pick (`pickFilesOf`). The chip says ONE number (`Polarsteps · N days`, steps and fixes in its tooltip) so it shares a row with the instance's chip at 390 px — with both counts it wrapped the head onto a third row. With the instance FAILING, the export alone still deduces (a warn line says so); with no connection at all the Deduce button is not offered — **not built: Deduce from Polarsteps without any Winnow** (the modal and `StageOrigin.sourceId` are keyed on a connection). **Real export, parsed** (2026-10-08, scratchpad): 181 steps, 7 602 fixes, nothing skipped, 314 local days placed (293 by the track, 21 more by steps), 12.0 % of fixes on a different UTC day; of 134 halts at the default settings 84 named by a step, 43 by the index, 7 unnamed (15 without the export); the island, the gorge, the rock and the monument all took his step names. Parse 31 ms. **Driven** headless at 1280 and 390 (touch) against a stubbed instance on synthetic files: a zip refused on the chip, `trip.json` + `locations.json` + a `user.json` dropped together, the step's name on the card and written with `source: 'polarsteps'`, a 07:30-local fix filed on its local day, the Data pane's counts, the instance answering 500 → the export alone deduces; 0 px overflow, no console error. Not against his instance or his files in a browser, not on his Mac or phone. **Not done**: a halt holding SEVERAL steps contributes only the nearest one's name (the others are not extra places), and a place's dates from a Polarsteps halt still say «from your pictures» (`dateFrom: 'photos'`).

## «Situer cette photo» — one picture, one proposal, the same rules (2026-09-20)

**The second gesture of the deduction, and the one a container could build**: the deduction answers a whole trip from an instance, this answers the question a single photograph settles — *where was I that day?* — with no instance at all. `shared/roadtrip/locate-picture.ts` (pure) + `LocatePicturePanel` + a fourth `MediaAction` published by `TripOverview`, so the verb is offered wherever the shell shows a picture large: the Library's own lightbox and an instance's.

**It is not a second geometry.** Same index, same contest (`nearestCity`), same refusals: no position · Null Island · no date · a day outside the trip (CALLED OUT, never clamped onto an edge) · nothing near enough to name · the leg already names it. The name goes into the PLACE and never into `stage.name`, the country reaches the document only as the CODE it is (`countryCode: 'AU'`, since v30 — never a name a reader would print), and the place carries the CITY's coordinates, exactly as `track-chapters.ts` writes them.

**One proposal, never a menu, and it writes through the editors that already exist.** A leg covering the day that names nothing is NAMED; one that already has a route gains a place, appended (only the author knows where in a lived order it belongs, so the sheet says "appended · reorder it in the leg itself"); a day no leg covers gets one from `startStageAt` — the calendar's own "Start a stage here", to the day before the next leg — with the place written onto it. The sheet states the span it would create BEFORE it is accepted, and `apply(trip)` recomputes from the trip it is handed rather than a copy taken at read time.

**The sheet shows its working**: the day and where the day came from (`readCapture`'s three rungs — the camera's EXIF, the instance that vouched for it, the file's own date said to be a weak guess), the coordinates, the city with its country and how far away it is. A name with no working shown is a name nobody can weigh, and the country is a hint here precisely because it is not fit to store.
