**Rules the reducer encodes, so a driver never re-decides them**: the idle clock counts from the LAST edit; an edit during an in-flight push leaves the record dirty after `pushOk` (`pushStartedAt` is what tells); `offline` and `unauthenticated` retry on every trigger (only trying can tell the network or the session is back); `forbidden`, `conflict` and `gone` HOLD until a person acts and an edit does not move them; a `protocol` failure keeps the edit dirty with the message. `pillText` is the honesty rule again — every status yields the sentence the pill prints, with the conflict's time in the reader's own clock. The client side is `WinnowClient.listDocs/getDoc/putDoc/deleteDoc` (`architecture.md`, «Remote sources»): 304 is an answer, 404 is `notfound` (never forbidden — a foreign row's existence is not revealed), 412 is `conflict` carrying the server's etag, and the body cap is checked BEFORE a PUT so an oversize trip is refused with a sentence rather than a 413.

## Local now, remote on idle — and every state is a sentence (2026-09-06)

**Decision (D1–D4 of `docs/roadtrip-persistence.md`, taken by the maintainer, not to be re-opened).** The 800 ms IndexedDB write is untouched; a remote trip additionally pushes its mirror after 5 s of quiet, on `visibilitychange: hidden`, in the unmount flush, and on "Save now" (since 2026-09-15 the machine is the shared `shared/sources/use-document-sync.tsx`, its resume rule the pure `afterPull` — `architecture.md`). The remote copy is the authority and the mirror is its cache; opening a remote trip opens the mirror at once and `GET`s with `If-None-Match` — 304 nothing, 200 + clean mirror → replaced silently, 200 + dirty mirror → `conflict` and the pill offers **Keep mine** (re-PUT over the server's etag) / **Take theirs** (two-step, drops local edits); unreachable → the mirror opens and the pill says offline. That IS the resume workflow. A 404 on push is `gone` with **Keep here as local** / **Delete here**. The bucket is generic (`kind: trip | project`), trips first; thumbnails never cross (re-baked from the preview); writes are own-docs-any-role on Winnow, foreign rows 404.

**Rules learnt wiring it**: the tool reduces `pushStarted` / the outcome around `pushOnce` ITSELF and reduces the LIVE record after the request (an edit can land while the PUT is out, and only the live record's `dirtyAt` knows — `pushTrip` is for callers with no live state: create, import, move); the record is written by one function (`setRecord`: ref + state + store) so timers, unmount and the pill never disagree; a remote creation or import pushes FIRST and keeps nothing here if the instance refused (one gesture, one request, the result said); a remote delete carries `If-Match` and is refused while unreachable — there are no tombstones; a move writes the target and waits for the acknowledgement before deleting at the origin, keeps the id so `tripRef` links survive, and goes through the portable file so `projectId`s are nulled (projects do not cross). What leaves is the document minus `sourceId`; what comes back is stamped with the id and source of the REQUEST and migrated like a stored trip. The pill is one component (`SyncPill`) rendered by the shell into a `headerExtra` slot of both the overview and the piece editor, so the two screens read the same record.

**SHIPPED and confirmed working by the maintainer on the deployed pair (2026-09-07).** Before that, verified end to end against a REAL Winnow, its migration 0041 applied by the stack's own runner and its routes answering: create-on-remote (preflight 204 → PUT 201, the row in Postgres with `sourceId` absent from the stored document, as the portable split requires), re-open (GET `If-None-Match` → 304), an edit made elsewhere (push → 412, pill «refused: changed on another device at HH:MM»), **Keep mine** (re-PUT over the server's etag, checked in the table), the row deleted elsewhere (push → 404, pill «deleted on <host>»), and **Keep here as local** (the trip falls back to `local` losing nothing). Ownership holds: another account's row answers **404** on GET and on PUT, never 412, and the list shows only your own. The refusals answer 415 / 400 / 413 / 412 as designed. **The deployed pair was never needed** — two localhosts are same-site, so the cookie travels; the recipe is in `testing.md`. **Still unverified**: offline and 401 mid-edit. «Take theirs» was driven on 2026-09-15 against a stub bucket (the two-step, the mirror replaced, no pending local write flushed over it).

**The trap that hides a shipped capability: the capabilities sheet is a SNAPSHOT taken at connect time and nothing refreshes it** (`client.capabilities()` has exactly one caller, `ConnectScreen`). A browser that connected before the instance grew the document bucket goes on believing it has none, so the «Keep on» picker, «Move to» and the remote list all stay hidden and Atelier tries nothing — a shipped feature that looks unshipped, on every device that connected early. The cure is the `reconnect` link in the media browser's header, which re-runs `#/connect` and replaces the stored sheet. **How to apply**: any feature gated on a capability needs that sentence in its release note, and a capability that turns on later is a reason to re-read the sheet, not to assume the stored one is current.

## Import target folded into a modal, not a standing header select (2026-09-07)

**Decision.** `TripGallery`'s "import to" `<select>` used to sit in the header at all times, useful only during the rare import gesture. It is now `ImportTripModal`, opened by the "Import a trip file" button — same "Keep on" picker as `NewTripModal`, shown only when `documentSources.length > 1`. With one source the button skips the modal and picks the file directly, so the common case stays one click. **How to apply**: a control that is only relevant during one gesture belongs behind that gesture's modal, not permanently in the header — the gallery header's job is the trip list, not import plumbing.

## The tool is called Trips; the code keeps `roadtrip` (2026-09-10)

**From the maintainer**: *"roadtrip to be renamed Trips"*. What moved is the NAME on screen — `Tool.label` in the registry (so the masthead, the switcher and the home card follow), the publisher the Library sidebar prints beside a day (`MediaScope.publisher`), the hook scene's default name in the Studio, and the README. What deliberately did NOT move, and must not: the route `#/roadtrip/…`, the tool id, the IndexedDB database name, the `.roadtrip.json` extension, every module path under `roadtrip/`, and the `roadtrip:` prefix the Studio bridge marks its elements with. Renaming any of those breaks every link ever made, every trip file already exported and every project already briefed — for a word. **How to apply**: `roadtrip` is the slug, `Trips` is the name; a new user-visible string says Trips and nothing else moves. A project briefed before this keeps `Road Trip hook` on its stored scene until the next send, which is the ordinary cost of a default.

## The home draws no title of its own (2026-09-10)

**From the maintainer**, looking at the gallery on a phone: it opened on a serif `Trips` and a two-line paragraph, directly under the masthead's own `Atelier / Trips` — the same word twice, and a ninth of an 844px screen spent before the first card. **Decision**: the page draws no title. The heading stays as `sr-only`, so the document keeps an outline and a screen reader its landmark and only the ink is given back; and at `compact` the header ROW goes with it, since its two verbs are already in the shell's bottom bar and an empty row above the cards is the same waste in a thinner form. **How to apply**: a screen whose name the masthead already says (`Tool.label`, and the switcher beside it) must not say it again. The Studio's gallery still draws its own `Projects` — left as it is on purpose, since only this one was reported; take it the same way if he asks.

## Open, and decided but not built (2026-08-23)

- **The export-reminder banner is still unbuilt.** The `.roadtrip.json` file itself now exists (see below); what is missing is the nudge the maintainer asked for — a **discreet banner** ("last export 12 days ago"), never a blocking prompt, so a cleared IndexedDB cannot cost him a year of tracking.
- **The three visual directions drawn in the design pass were never formally chosen.** They now exist as the four title-style presets in the Style picker (the studio's own `StylePanel`, moved to `shared/overlay/` when Road Trip became its second consumer), so the choice is one click rather than a code change — but he has not said which one is the signature.
- **Time Machine** (an animated counter winding back to the media's day) and an API into his own geolocated media-triage system are wanted but explicitly last: both were discussed as "after the rest works".
- Element positioning is **not** fixed to a corner — he rejected a pinned top/side badge; placement is free, which is what the shared overlay engine already gives.

## The overview's counts are ONE ROW on a phone (2026-09-10)

**A figure block laid out to breathe does not shrink on a phone, it WRAPS** — and wrapping is what costs the fold. The five counts sat in a `gap-x-10` flex row whose labels are whole phrases ("days on the road", "days told · N left"); at 390px that is three rows plus `p-5`, and the calendar the screen exists for started below it. Shortening each label to one word and forcing a `grid-cols-3` still left two rows and 102px, and the maintainer's answer to that was "still too big — you can even make it scrollable horizontally".

**So: one row, `overflow-x-auto`, one `flex-none` figure per count — 47px, measured.** It costs one line whatever a trip's numbers turn out to be, where a grid's height is decided by how many figures there happen to be. All six fit at 360px and it scrolls at 320; when it does, the figure cut off at the right edge is what says there are more, which no wrapped grid can say. Read along a row, "days told · N left" is two figures pretending to be one, so the compact list is a DIFFERENT list and not the same one abbreviated: days · told · left · pieces · published · silence. Nothing changes above 820px, where the phrases are the better label and there is room for them.

**Two traps, both worth the measurement.** A flex child whose `overflow` is other than `visible` has an automatic minimum size of **zero**, so the moment this row could scroll sideways the column above crushed it to 18px with the numbers sheared off at the waist — it needs `flex-none`. And it must declare **no `touch-action` at all**: the rule elsewhere (`frontend.md`) is about a surface that WRITES a drag, while this is an ordinary scroll box where the browser already does both axes, and `pan-x` would not add horizontal scrolling — it would remove the vertical swipe that scrolls the page under a 47px strip. The end padding of a flex scroll container IS honoured (13px of paper after the last figure, scrolled fully right), so no trailing spacer is needed.

## The overview's two zones zoom — and they are the ONLY ones (2026-09-08, rev. 2026-09-10)

**Superseded 2026-09-13**: both pills are gone — the heatmap fits its box and the loupe windows the ruler (the L1 entry above). What survives of this entry is the rule that a media PREVIEW never carries a view zoom, and the traps `useStageZoom` still carries for the lightbox. **The ruler's zoom came back on 2026-09-28** — as the LENGTH of its window, not as a layout multiplier: «The stage ruler zooms again» below.

**Decision.** The day grid and the stage ruler each carry a `StageZoomControl` (`shared/ui/`, see `frontend.md` for the primitive and its traps), because both are day-sized zones that are either too dense to aim at or too wide to see. **They are what the pill was ever for**: the same control briefly sat over the piece editor's badge stage and the Studio's canvas, and was taken back out on 2026-09-10 — over a media preview it fought the picture's own framing gestures. Never add one to a preview. What the scale multiplies is the zone's own unit, not a transform: the heatmap rounds `CELL`/`GAP` to whole pixels at every scale so the cells stay on one lattice, and the ruler multiplies the fitted `width / total` day width while keeping the 6px `MIN_DAY` floor — a bar thinner than that cannot be dragged. Both already scrolled sideways in their own box, so nothing else changed; drags keep working because the ruler measures the pointer through the track's rect and the day width it just drew with.

**Each zone declares its geometry as a pure function of the scale** — `rulerDayWidth`/`rulerTrackWidth` in `stage-ruler.ts`, `heatmapColumn`/`heatmapWidth` in the new `day-grid.ts` — because the zoom needs to ask "how wide would you be at this scale?" without a layout, twice: to know how far out it may go, and how much the content really grew. The ruler reads its box from `zoom.viewport` rather than measuring it a second time; two measurements of one box is how the drawn day width and the zoom's floor start disagreeing.

**`MIN_DAY` is the ruler's BASE, not a clamp after the zoom** (2026-09-08). Clamping after froze the whole track: a 616-day trip in a 460px box drew the same 3696px picture from 25% to 800%, so zooming in did nothing but yank the scroll. The day width is now `max(6px, box/total) × scale`, strictly proportional, and the panel floors the zoom at **100%** — a day is therefore never drawn under 6px, a leg can always be grabbed by an edge, and on a trip too long for the box 100% simply scrolls. `−` greys at 100% on the ruler, which is the honest face of it.

**The ruler's scale steps with the day width it actually draws** (2026-09-12). Month labels alone never said how wide a day IS, so the track carries day strokes under its axis rule: every day while a day is at least `MIN_TICK_GAP` (9px) wide, real Mondays alone below that, nothing at all if even a week would crowd — `rulerTicks(trip, dayWidth)`, pure and tested. Mondays and firsts of the month are the taller stroke, so a dense comb still has a rhythm to read against. The step is chosen from the DRAWN day width, never from the zoom percentage: at 100% a long trip sits at the 6px floor and gets weeks, while a short trip fits and gets days at the same percentage. Weeks are real Mondays rather than every seventh day of the trip — a rhythm anchored on the trip's arbitrary first day means nothing.

**A bare wheel zooms them** (`wheel: 'any'`): the maintainer asked for it directly, and neither zone has another use for the wheel. Horizontal panning is what survives — shift-wheel and a sideways swipe still scroll the track.

**Where the control sits (rev. 2026-09-08).** Neither zone pays a row for it: the heatmap's rides the legend row it already had (`ml-auto`), and the ruler's sits in `StagesPanel`'s header beside "+ Stage" — which is why the zoom is created by the PANEL and handed to `StageRuler` as a prop. A row of its own under the track was the first shape and the maintainer rejected it. `StageRuler` uses the handed-down `zoom.viewportRef` AS its scroller ref rather than keeping a second one: one element, measured for the fitted day width and scrolled by the zoom. That header row's controls all stand `h-[2.125rem]` (34px, the zoom pill's own height), and "+ Stage" is the gallery's ink-filled primary at header size rather than another bordered pill — adding a leg is the panel's one creative act and must not read as a status chip.

## The stage ruler zooms again: the loupe is its window, and the zoom is the window's length (2026-09-28)

**Decision, the maintainer's ask** (*«quand on a beaucoup d'éléments ça devient compliqué de pouvoir les lire… zoomer sur le touchpad, le trackpad ou la souris en faisant un défilement… il faut bien prendre en compte le changement d'échelle sur la loupe, la zone va changer soit en se réduisant soit en grandissant… n'oublie pas des contrôles plus et moins»*). At the three months the C7 ruler details (~9px a day), a 1–3-day leg is 10–30px and its name is two letters. **The model** (`shared/roadtrip/loupe.ts`, rewritten, pure and tested): the track is the WHOLE trip in its horizontal scroll box and the box shows a WINDOW `{ from, days }` in FRACTIONAL day offsets — fractional so a pinch or a trackpad sweep moves it by less than a day and nothing snaps under the hand (the old loupe lived in whole weeks). **The zoom IS `days`**: a day is `width / days` wide, between `MIN_LOUPE_DAYS` (7, a week across) and `width / MIN_DAY` (the ruler's 6px floor — a leg's edge stays grabbable; ~135 days at 812px) or the trip. **100% is the three months around the month on screen** (`monthsAround`, unclamped, so the window is centred on them), the whole trip when that is shorter or on a short trip, and what fits at the floor on a narrow box (`baseDays`) — so the pill never opens on 111%. The panel stores a SCALE against that base, so the zoom is kept as the months go by (89–92 days each).

**What moves it.** The wheel / a trackpad pinch (⌘/ctrl-wheel) / two fingers zoom about the pointer through the suite's one reading of the hand (`useZoomGestures`, `wheel: 'any'` — the ruler had no use for a vertical wheel; `drag: null` leaves one pointer to the legs and the swipe); − / + in the header (`StageZoomControl`, `stepZoom`'s 1.25 steps, the % resets) zoom about the middle; a sideways sweep, a shift-wheel, a swipe with its throw and the scrollbar travel. **It follows the calendar**: when another MONTH comes on screen the window centres on its three months keeping its zoom — exactly the old «month on screen and its two neighbours» at 100%, measured identical to `main` (1 Aug → 31 Oct · 92 days) — and keeps the open day if it lies in that month; when the OPEN DAY changes it slides the shortest way to show it (`holdDay`, a day of room when there is some). A ruler gesture never scrolls the calendar: the calendar's multi-column rows make week ↔ scrollTop non-monotonic, and the two following each other would ping-pong.

**The map shows it, and not as a second frame.** `YearMap` draws the ruler's window as a 3px ACCENT bar in a lane under the weeks (`(lead + d) / 7` columns, the map's own Monday origin), which shrinks and grows with the zoom; the ink frame stays the CALENDAR's (R4). Two outlines that close fuse into one lumpy line (the 2026-09-14 border fix), and the two windows differ as soon as the ruler is zoomed. The header's legend is `Stages · N legs` again and the window's dates sit beside it with the same accent dash (`LoupeReadout`), hidden when the window is the whole trip. **Both readers subscribe to a store** (`createLoupeStore` + `use-loupe.ts`): the ruler writes on every scroll and zoom frame, and routing that through `TripOverview` state is the R8 trap.

**Rules the hand taught, measured headless** (a 344-day trip of 97 legs of 1–8 days, 1280 × 900): (1) the window the hand last ASKED for lives in a ref (`live`) ahead of the render — a wheel burst lands several notches before React draws — and every gesture reads it, never the props; (2) `onScroll` reads the box back into the window only when no zoom is pending (compare `live.days` with the scale last LAID OUT) and only past 1px, or our own write, rounded, overwrites the unrounded intent; (3) a pinch's centre pans the track, so the first finger's fling stands down while two fingers are on and for 250ms after, or it throws the track on the lift. The anchor holds to 0.17px over six notches from an INTEGER pointer — a probe from a fractional mouse position read 3px of false drift, since Chrome delivers `clientX` whole. Also measured: the ctrl-wheel pinch, a ×4 two-finger pinch by CDP touch at 1024px (100% → 400%), a swipe travelling 318px with its glide, the calendar scrolled to November carrying a 381% window to 3 → 28 Nov, a tap on the zoomed track opening its day, a mouse drag of a leg's end +2 days at 116px a day, a 25-day trip zooming from its whole to 332% with no map, and no console warning.

**Readable at the zoom, because that is what it is for**: past 22px a day the scale writes each date, past 44px its weekday in the calendar's two letters (`rulerDayLabels`), never on a 1st where the month's name stands; and a leg's NAME is `position: sticky` inside its bar, so zoomed into the middle of an 8-day leg (bar from −309 to 617px) the name still sits at 14px — clamped by the bar, so a short one still truncates.

**Not changed, found on the way**: the wide calendar opens one ROW late — the open day's month is jumped to before the blocks go multi-column, so a trip opened on 14 July shows September first, on `main` too; the ruler then follows September. And a headless check must open a trip on its SLUGGED route (tap a day first): a raw-id route is rewritten on the first navigation, and an edit made before that rewrite did not survive it in the check.

## The hook is a VARIANT now, and the contract is three rules (2026-09-13)

**Decision, with the maintainer.** A piece's opener is no longer only the six-piece badge: `shared/roadtrip/hooks/` holds a registry of hook variants, `PostBadge.hook` (v15) names which one draws, and `resolveHook` folds them into one reader the stage, the PNG deck and (later) the clip export all paint. The exemplar that drove it is the **scrub** — the trip's measuring tape sweeping to the day being told, flashing that day's pictures, ticking — chosen by the maintainer out of four explored directions. The whole design, the sound path and the seven phases are `docs/hook-engine.md`; what follows is only what the code cannot say.

**`prepare()` returns a CLOSURE, never a plan object.** The stop list, the projection, the timings are computed once and captured; `content`, `paint` and `score` read them by construction. Handing a plan around would let one of the three recompute it and drift — which a sound bed cannot survive — and it also removes every generic from the registry. **How to apply**: a variant that needs a value in two of the three computes it in `prepare` and closes over it, never in the callbacks.

**Three decisions taken before the first line, each cheap now and expensive later.** (1) A variant rewrites the TEXT of named pieces (`content(t): Partial<Record<BadgePiece, string|null>>`, merged over what `badgeContent` computed) and never builds `OverlayElement[]` — so `badgeElements` stays the single builder and the theme, ratios, deterministic ids and hit-testing keep working whatever draws; the headline may be rewritten but never hidden. (2) `owns: 'frame' | 'layer'` is declared from the start even with one layer: a scrub IS the picture while it sweeps, a route trace draws over it, and that is the only conflict a stack can ever produce. (3) The field is stored as a **list** with one entry, so the stack arrives as UI with no second migration on documents remote trips already carry. An unknown id is **skipped** and the badge stands in — a trip written by a newer build opens here and loses its opener, never fails to open.

**A field on `PostBadge` needs no fifth site**: `portablePost` deep-clones the whole post, so it travels in and out of `.roadtrip.json` by itself. The four-site rule (`ProjectPortable` and friends, `studio.md`) bites fields on the DOCUMENT, not on a post.

**What phase 1 deliberately did NOT wire: `content()`.** Both call sites (`slide-render.ts`, `PostEditor.tsx`) `useMemo` the elements on a stable `content`, and making that depend on the transport's clock would rebuild every element sixty times a second for a variant that rewrites nothing. The answer when the scrub needs it is to move the element build INTO the render — `renderBadge` taking `content` + `layout` rather than finished `elements` — not to add a time dependency to the React memo. The paint seam IS wired (`RenderBadgeOptions.hook`, painted between the picture and the shades), because it was already per-frame.

**The picker (2026-09-13) — two departures from the brief's first draft, both deliberate.** (1) A card's box holds the variant's `Sketch`, a drawing of what the variant DOES, and **not a render of this piece**: the stage beside the picker already shows the real thing, and a live render per card costs a decode and a WebGL context each to repeat it worse. (2) **No second picker for the trip-wide default**: ⚙ Trip → New pieces already saves the whole look through `hookDefaultsFrom`, which carries `hook` — two places to choose the same thing is the fault this tool keeps removing. The picker sits at the top of the Look tab, OUTSIDE the hook branch's other controls, because the opener decides what the hook is and the title style below it belongs to every slide. Its two writers replace the first layer only, so a stored stack survives a card click.

**A switch SETS AN OPENER ASIDE, never throws it away (2026-09-28).** His report: *«à chaque fois que je change d'opener ou que je change d'onglet les données sont perdues»*. Read in the code: `setHookVariant` started any newly chosen opener from its defaults, so trying Virée after an Itinerary erased its stops; a TAB switch lost nothing stored — only the panel's selected stop, component state unmounted with the tab. Now `PostBadge.hookShelf` (optional, no version bump — absent is an empty shelf) holds the options of the openers not drawing, by variant id, and `switchHookVariant` shelves the current one and takes the chosen one back (defaults only the first time); the active opener is NEVER on the shelf too, and the badge (no options) is never shelved. The shelf is the PIECE's: `hookDefaultsFrom` lists its fields and so never carries it, nor does the house style; the backup deep-clones it; an undo reverts the switch and the shelf together because both are the document. The selected stop survives a tab switch in module state (a stop id is a UUID, so it cannot select another piece's). **How to apply**: an opener's per-piece input needs nothing for this — it rides in its options; never reset an opener's options on a switch again.

**Défilé (2026-09-13) — the traps a moving opener exposed.** A hook can now move WITHOUT an animated badge piece, and three places assumed the opposite; each showed up as a visible bug on the first render, and each is now the rule. (1) `useBadgeClock` takes the opener's seconds: without them the transport never started and the stage sat forever on the sweep's first, dark frame. (2) The hook thumbnail is never captured while the transport plays — a scrub caught mid-sweep stores ANOTHER day's picture as this piece's hook, and that thumbnail is what the gallery cover draws. (3) `deckSlides` resolves `auto` through `hookMoves`, measured by preparing the hook: a scrub on day 1 plays nothing and stays an image. **How to apply**: anything that asks "does this hook move / when is it at rest?" reads `ResolvedHook.seconds` beside `badgeSettleSeconds`, never the piece styles alone.

**An untold day goes dark during the sweep.** Not a stand-in and not the hero shown early: an empty day looks empty. The piece being composed never counts as telling its own day. The numeral steps only under the `day` counter; under any other it would be a fabricated reading.

**Défilé flashes SOURCE pictures, and the author can pick them (2026-09-14, reverses "the flashes are the thumbs store").** The maintainer's report on the first build: selection rules the maintainer could not read (two "Day 1 / Day 21" selects, a Sampled/Chosen switch whose tiles toggled only in one mode) and, worse, flashes that were the thumbs store's 640px hook JPEGs with OTHER pieces' badges burned in — *"ce n'est pas du tout ce que j'avais en tête"*. **Rejected, so it is not re-proposed**: any derived picture (the thumbs store, a composed hook) as a flash source — "already graded, one read" was the whole argument and it produced the wrong picture. **Now**: `stopsOn: 'pieces' | 'picked'`; the pieces' days flash the ref each standing piece is composed over; picked pictures are refs + capture day stored in the options, chosen in `HookPicturesModal` the way the maintainer described it — a span, everything shot in it (Library + instance) ticked, untick the rest. Loader rules (`use-hook-pictures.ts`): Library by name/hash, else the instance's proxy fetched WITHOUT entering the pool, cropped to the frame at decode, one 32 MP budget shared by the set, graded with `composeWith(null)` so the flashes match the piece. Design and the rest of the rules: `docs/hook-engine.md` §9. **How to apply**: a variant that wants other pictures asks by REF through `wantsPictures`, never by day or post; a panel that needs a shell service gets it through `HookPanelHost`, never by importing from `tools/`. Verified in the Browser pane with ten generated Library photos (chooser at 1400 and 390 wide, untick/reopen/look, eight distinct pictures read back off the stage canvas in shot order); **not** exercised: the Winnow path, a piece's clip frame, an export carrying picked pictures, memory on a phone.

**`content()` reached the renderer as an optional `elementsAt(t)`**, not by moving the element build into `renderBadge` (the brief's first idea): `renderBadge`, `measureBadge`, `exportVariantVideo` and the still painter each fall back to `elements`, and callers build the function only when `ResolvedHook.rewrites`. The shared pipeline's `paintUnderOverlays` gained the clip-relative time as a fourth argument — additive, the Studio passes nothing.

**A `<style>` inside a React component leaks into the button's `textContent`**: the picker card's sketch put its keyframes inline and the card's text read "@keyframes scrub-sketch…". Keyframes belong in `src/index.css`, beside the others.

**Défilé ticks (2026-09-13).** The scrub's score is a detent per landing, a `leg` voice on a day a leg starts (the only sound carrying meaning, so the only different one) and a `seat` on today — read off the same stops as the head, so the ticks cannot leave their frames. The `sound` option defaults ON because the variant is chosen deliberately, and it reaches every video the hook makes: a still's and a clip recorded without sound take the ticks as their track, while a clip WITH sound keeps it bit-for-bit unless the author ticks "Mix them into a clip's own sound" (`mixWithClip`, off by default, hidden while the ticks are off) — `media-pipeline.md` for the plan. In the editor the ticks play through a live `AudioContext` behind a speaker toggle in the transport, OFF on every visit — a panel that ticks while its sliders are dragged is unusable. The toggle only shows when the opener scores anything.

**The route trace (2026-09-13) is RETIRED (2026-09-15), and what it proved is why the Itinerary may pin a point.** The Itinerary replaces it — the maintainer's call — so it is gone from the registry and from the tree; a stored `route` layer is CONVERTED rather than dropped back to the plain badge (`mapFromRoute` + the v20 migration: the trip's located places become the stops, every option that means the same thing comes across, the pictures start off and the line straight, which is what the Route drew). Keep these three, which outlived it: (1) it marked the LEG a day belongs to and **never a point** — a place has no dates and the badge's caption names the leg, so a "you are here" pin would be a claim the document cannot back; that refusal is exactly what makes the Itinerary's pin legitimate, because there the stop is the author's own assertion. (2) It needed no line changed in `HookVariant`, the resolver, the picker, the renderer or either export — only one optional `HookContext.stages` that `needs.places` had announced, which is the engine's contract test passing. (3) Its projection, its label placer and its distance format live in `geo.ts` — lifted out the day the DRIVE reached for them, not the Itinerary — and `currentLegIndex` moved to `hook-calendar.ts`, which is where Virée reads it. Retiring a variant costs nothing when its shared parts were lifted out the day a sibling wanted them; what would have cost is deleting the file Virée still imported.

**What the route's options taught (2026-09-14), which outlived the variant.** Three things a future agent should know that the code does not say: (1) what two variants both want lives beside the contract, not in either — `hooks/easing.ts`, `tick-kits.ts`, `panel-ui.tsx` and `colour.ts` were lifted out of Défilé the day the route reached for them, and Défilé re-exports the old names so its stored options and tests never moved; a variant importing its sibling's plan would be the "tool reaches into another tool" fault at a smaller scale. (2) The ticks are timed on lengths measured in the projection's OWN units (`planarLengths`), not on pixels — the projection is one uniform scale, so the ratios are frame-free and the score written at export lands where the preview's dots appear. (3) Every text the route draws is the place's own name, a distance the pen has actually covered, or the letter N — nothing derived, nothing guessed; the distance is great-circle and says "so far" in the panel because a road distance is a claim the document cannot back. All three carried straight into the Itinerary, which is what made it a file plus a registry line.

## An opener, a badge and masked text on ANY slide — evaluated 2026-09-16, built 2026-09-28

**Where it came from.** The maintainer put the founding "a piece has exactly one hook, and it is slide 1" back on the table: a day with three legs wants three itineraries, and two openers in one piece are two different animations and two reasons to keep swiping. His note also asks for text that MASKS the media, with blend modes. The whole evaluation, the model change and the commits are `docs/slide-capacities.md`; the interactive bench (six worked decks, real compositing) is https://claude.ai/artifact/Q8paemaSsDENij7Lck2ti4. Built on the brief's own answers to §9, which remain HIS to overturn.

**What the survey established, and what a future agent should not re-derive:**

- **The renderer is already slide-agnostic.** `renderBadge` paints background → picture → `hook.paint(t)` → shades → qr → `drawOverlays`, and never asks which slide it has. The wall is ONE pure function, `slide-render.ts`, branching on `DeckSlide.kind`; `renderSlideVideo` is already "one video for one slide" with `isHook ?` ternaries, and `hookContextFor` reads exactly three fields off `post.badge` (`durationSeconds`, `hookSeconds`, `mode`). The feature is a branch rewrite, not a pipeline.
- **`PostBadge` bundles four unrelated things** — the picture (which `PostSlide` already duplicates field for field), the badge, the opener and the shades. Only the last three are withheld from a content slide.
- **The shape proposed is the COLLAGE precedent**: `PostSlide` gains `hook`, `shades`, `badge` and `texts`, all optional and null, v29 changing nothing a stored trip draws — `PostBadge` stays what the first slide holds. The `PostBadge`/`PostSlide` merge is the right long-term model and is deliberately a SEPARATE commit: it moves stored fields, and a migration that moves data must not ride with a feature onto remote trips.
- **Several openers in one PIECE costs nothing once the opener is a slide's field** — three slides each holding one. The unsolved case stays the stack on ONE slide, which `HookLayer[]` was already shaped for and which still has no UI.
- **The picture budget would become the deck's, not the piece's.** `use-hook-pictures.ts` gathers wants from one layer list and splits one budget (`perPicturePixels(budgetCount(n))`); with openers on several slides the wants must be gathered across the WHOLE deck or three itineraries decode thirty-six pictures at a delivered frame's size.
- **A wash cannot go on the main context.** A wash (letters removed from a colour, so the photograph reads only inside the type) is built in its own buffer — the `drawGlowedText` rule, a shadow is dropped under a `destination-*` mode, and erasing on the frame would take the picture with the letters. A blend mode, by contrast, is three lines inside the per-element `save()/restore()` and reaches the stage, the PNG deck, the clip and the Studio bridge at once. (The survey said a PUNCH needs a buffer too; building it showed it does not — commit 6.)
- **Two places would stop being true**: `hook-scene.ts` says "one scene per project: a clip has one hook", so a piece with three openers must name which one crosses the bridge; and `HOOK_PICTURE = 'hook'` (shared by `post-grade.ts` and `develop-apply.ts`) is a STORED key, so renaming the role is cheap in the UI and expensive in the document.

**Built so far (2026-09-28), on the brief's own recommendations as working answers** — he asked to start without answering §9. **Commit 1, the model (v29)**: `PostSlide.hook | shades | badge | texts`, every stored slide landing on none; the readers and the chapter mark live in `shared/roadtrip/slide-capacities.ts`. `SlideBadge` has no `scale` — `BadgeLayout.sizeFrac` already is the numeral's size — and no camera credit, which is measured from the HOOK's picture. **Trap corrected from the brief**: a field inside a POST needs no line in `trip-file.ts` (posts travel whole through `portablePost`); the four-places rule is for fields on the `TripDoc` itself, where `tripDocFromFile` spreads `createTripDoc` and a forgotten line drops silently.

**Commit 2, rendering by capacity**: `slide-render.ts` composes EVERY photographed slide on one path — the first reads its badge, opener and shades from `post.badge`, another from its own record — and the stage (`PostEditor.openRender`), the rail, the PNG deck and the per-slide video all go through it for any slide but the first. Rules it fixed: an opener is told its SLIDE's time (`slideHookTiming`: screen time = the slide's, and no counter where the slide draws no badge, so a sweep never steps a numeral that is not there); a slide's still is taken at `slideSettleSeconds` (its opener, its badge's entrance — the piece's look so the piece's timing —, its text's `elementSettleSeconds`, its cells), the first slide's rest staying the stage's clock; every opener of the deck asks for its pictures in ONE `useHookPictures` pass, so they share one budget. Known limit: another slide's opener flashes pictures in the HOOK's grade (`flashLut`), as the first always did. Repaired on the way: a content slide's video drew its caption with NO title style (`theme: isHook ? trip.theme : null`) while its PNG, thumbnail and stage used the trip's.

**Commit 3, the editor**: everything is written through ONE notion, "the open slide's" (`openLayers`, `openShades`, `openBadgeLayout`, `openCtx`, `openContent` in `PostEditor`) — the first slide's read and written on `post.badge`, another's on its own record — so no panel asks which. Decisions it fixed: on another slide the opener picker's badge card reads **None** and clears the opener (the badge variant draws nothing extra, and there the badge is a capacity of its own); the badge's LOOK (piece styles, cascade) is edited on the first slide only, since every badge of the piece wears it, while a slide badge's words, counter and temporal line are its own and the marker, the "read on" day and the camera credit stay the piece's; a caption line and a badge piece now focus DIFFERENT fields (`captionFieldRef`), since one slide may hold both; `SlideDelivery`'s "what each choice would do" reads the deck's own resolved `reason` (`animated`/`settled`), not the hook-only `hookAnimates`, so it is right wherever something moves. Known limit, for him: a chapter mark draws EVERY piece the badge has (trip name, word, numeral, out of, place) — there is no "hide this piece", since an emptied override means computed; a mark that is only "NOON 12" needs that, and it would be a piece-level visibility the model does not have yet.

**Commit 4, free text on any slide** — the first included (`PostBadge.texts`, read `?? []`: v29 existed on the branch before the field joined it). Rules it fixed: a line is id'd `text:…` (`createSlideText`, `isSlideTextId`) so the stage and the inspector tell it from a badge piece (`piece:`) or a caption line (`caption:`); it wears the trip's title style like a caption and never the badge's glow or panel; it is dragged ALONE — and the block drag now starts only on a badge PIECE (`pieceFromElementId`): the stage used to treat any element as the badge, which commit 3 made wrong the moment a slide could hold a badge beside a caption (pressing the caption dragged the badge; measured, fixed); the stage's cursor now says grab only for what a drag moves. On the first slide the text travels with `hookElements` / `hookElementsAt` (stage, exports, Studio bridge alike) and every rest time reads `textsSettleSeconds`. **Trap**: this repo's ESLint has NO react-hooks rule — a prop read inside a `useCallback` must be added to its deps by hand, or the drag reads stale positions.

**Commit 5, blend modes** (`shared/overlay/blend.ts`, engine-level, so the Studio's element panel has the row too): a curated seven, never a `destination-*` mask — those ERASE, and are the knockout's business. It works by construction because every surface paints picture and overlays on ONE canvas (verified file by file before building, `slide-capacities.md` §11): if a surface ever moves its overlays to a separate transparent layer, every blend on it silently becomes a no-op — keep them on one canvas. An unknown blend reads as normal (`compositeFor`) and the slide reader drops it. A Trips line has its own ink (`withLineInk`, the badge pieces' two-move rule). How to prove a blend in a browser: a white line over the dark ground, max luminance of the glyph region — 255 plain, 15 multiply, 240 difference.

**Commit 6, masked text** (`shared/overlay/knockout.ts`, `OverlayElement.knockout`, one `KnockoutRows` for the Trips line and the Studio element alike): `wash` (the picture read through the letters) and `punch` (the letters cut out). **A punch needs no buffer**: every surface lays a FLAT ground before the picture (`renderBadge`'s `#100f0d`, an export's cleared frame), so erasing to the ground and painting the letters in the ground's colour are the same pixels — `glyphsOf` draws the bare letters (legibility, glow and ink pinned, blend dropped) in the author's ground colour. If a surface ever lays a non-flat ground (a blurred border, a gradient) a punch stops being honest there and would then need the brief's buffer. A **wash** is cut in the `knock` scratch at VIDEO coordinates, before the element's animation transform (the buffer takes it, so a zooming wash grows its letters, not its frame); past `MAX_SCRATCH` (4096) it is cut at a reduced scale and laid back full size — softer letter edges, never plain letters, so a full-size still does not disagree with its preview. No mask cache: the stage re-cuts per paint, one fill + the glyphs + one `drawImage`. A masked text does not blend (the mask is its look), a mask on an instrument is ignored (`canMask`: text and telemetry readouts only), and the panels hide Blend and Ink while a mask is on. **A slide's words are drawn UNDER its badge** now (`slide-render.ts`, and the editor's `hookElements` / `hookElementsAt`): measured in the browser, a first-slide wash covered the signature. Proof in a browser, on the bare ground: a white wash at 100% makes a far corner 255 while the letters read the ground's 15; a red punch makes the letters' red channel lead by 255; a red wash leaves the badge's white on top.

**Merged with the opener SHELF (#201, 2026-09-28)**: a slide keeps its own `PostSlide.hookShelf` (optional, additive — absent is an empty shelf, no migration), written by the same `switchHookVariant` through the open slide's writer (`setOpenLayers(layers, shelf?)`), and a slide's None card SHELVES what it leaves before clearing the opener — so Itinerary → Virée → None → Itinerary on slide 2 finds its stops again (driven in the browser). The OSM backgrounds needed nothing: they ride the one `useHookPictures` pass, which already gathers every slide's layers.

**Awaiting the maintainer** (§9 of the brief): whether a badge may appear on more than one slide at all — the decision on record is that its value is being ONE signature, and the proposal's answer is a smaller second rung, a chapter mark at ~0.36 scale, opt-in per slide and never inherited; whether the first slide keeps the name "hook"; two masked modes or five; whether the trip's new-piece defaults should carry a deck SHAPE rather than only a look; and which opener crosses the bridge.

## 2026-09-13 — The trip gallery has two views, and the card with its cover stays the default

**Built (Cards, first half):** the card re-skinned on the foundations — the whole card opens (keyboard too), its verbs in `OverflowMenu` (Open · Choose a cover… · Export · Keep on X… · Delete apart) with moves and deletes in `ConfirmDialog`, no "No destination set" line (an empty field is not a fact), the span and the derived route on one line, the silence count only once something was told, and a `last opened` tag from `localStorage` (`atelier.roadtrip.lastOpened`, written whenever a trip is open) when nothing is open. The header names itself (Trips · count) with Import and New trip as `Button`s. The card no longer clips (cover wrapper rounds itself) or the menu is cut.

**Built (Bands, second half):** `Segmented` Cards / Bands in the header (`atelier.roadtrip.gallery.view`), desktop only — a phone always gets cards. Bands = a `ResumeBand` for the trip you were on (open, else last opened: lead picture or a `DayStrip`, "N days told of M · K days of silence since <date>", a primary Open/Resume) over `TripRow`s (name · dates · a `DayStrip` on the grid's ramp — one cell per day up to 62, then `rhythmBuckets` — · the progress line). `TripActions` holds the ⋯ and the two confirms for card, row and band alike, so the three can never offer different verbs. **Trap**: the gallery is UNMOUNTED while a trip is open, so "last opened" is written on the way out (the `open` wrapper), never by watching `openTripId`.

**Decision (maintainer, while ruling on the UI audit).** A proposal replaced the cards with a "resume" band plus dense progress rows; he accepted the rows but **refused to lose the cards**, because the cover system (layouts, pins — entry above) is what the card is for. So the gallery header carries a segmented **Cards / Bands** toggle: Cards is today's card with its cover, re-skinned by the new foundations (whole card opens, actions in ⋯, no "No destination set" line for an empty field, a "last opened" tag); Bands is the last-opened trip as a band with "Resume" and a small cover, then one row per trip (name, dates, progress). **How to apply**: the chosen view is a reading preference kept in `localStorage`, never on `TripDoc` — it must not travel in a `.roadtrip.json` nor sync to a Winnow. Nothing about the cover changes. Note the rhythm band already caps at 49 buckets (`rhythmBuckets`), so "aggregate long trips by week" is not a fix to propose.

**The ticks' volume lives IN the score (2026-09-13, raised to 200% same day).** "Ticks volume" (0–200%) scales every event's gain inside `scrubScore`, never a gain node downstream: the score is the one thing live playback, a still's bed, a silent clip's track and a mix all read, so one number reaches all four with nothing else threaded. Two consequences to keep: the voices accept gains up to `MAX_VOICE_GAIN` (2) — at 200% the loudest scored gain is 1.7, still under that ceiling — so a turned-up score comes out louder instead of flattening against 1; and `renderBed` scales a whole bed down only if it would pass `BED_CEILING`. Measured on a worst-case dense sweep: 150% peaks at 0.82 (still linear), 200% is where the guard actually engages, holding the bed's peak at the 0.98 ceiling instead of climbing further — a net, not a limiter, and now proven to fire at the range's top end rather than sitting unused. At 0% the score is EMPTY, which writes no track rather than a silent one.

## 2026-09-13 — A weekend and a year get different overviews; the heatmap stays for a long trip

**Built (the lighter shell, every length):** the heading IS the summary — the name in the serif (click to rename), the route · dates · N days · N stages line (click opens the details sheet) and three `Figure`s at the right (told/total · published + drafted · days of silence at most, the last a link to the gap's first day). The five-count strip (`Stat`) and the "longest stretch" sentence are gone; "Trip settings" sits in the bar's trailing group. ONE frame level and NO rules: the heading, the calendar, the stages and the open leg are separated by spacing alone (the maintainer removed the horizontal lines on 2026-09-13), and only the open day keeps its card — inside which the piece rows and "Tell this day" carry no rules either; a row shows itself on hover. `StagesPanel`'s `pill`/`addButton` constants retired for `Button`/`IconButton`.

**Built (the short strip):** `isShortTrip` (`trip-days.ts`, ≤ `SHORT_TRIP_DAYS` = 31, tested) picks `ShortDayStrip` over `DayHeatmap` — one row of `flex-1` cells with weekday + day number above, the same ramp, foot stripe, hover card, right-click menu and accessible name (exported from `DayHeatmap`), no zoom — and passes `zoomable={false}` to `StagesPanel`, which hides the ruler's zoom pill and leaves the bare wheel to the page. The strip and the ruler line up because both are the trip's days over the same box at 100%; nothing is measured twice.

**Built (L1, first half — the fitted heatmap and its leg lane):** `DayHeatmap` no longer zooms: it FITS its box (`fittedColumn` in `day-grid.ts`, tested — a year in 1000px gives ~15px cells, a short span caps at 28px, past ~14 months the cell floors at 6px and the grid scrolls), measured by `shared/ui/use-element-width.ts`. Under the rows a LANE draws the legs (`HeatmapLeg`, from `rulerBars`) on the grid's own week axis, to the day within the column, stacked as the ruler stacks them; a click opens the leg. `RulerBar` gained `clipStart`/`clipEnd` (tested) for the loupe's windowed ruler. The heatmap's zoom pill is gone (its `useStageZoom` use retired); the ruler's goes with the loupe.

**Built (L1, second half — the loupe):** `shared/roadtrip/loupe.ts` (pure, tested): a window of whole days, 56 by default, opened around the open day, `moveLoupe` keeps its width and stops at the trip's edges, `resizeLoupe` floors it at 7 days, `loupeContaining` slides it the shortest way when a click lands outside. `LoupeBrush` draws it OVER the heatmap through `DayHeatmap`'s `overlay(geometry)` slot — the frame takes no pointer (cells stay clickable), only the 12px grip on top and the two edge handles do, every drag snapping through `columnWidth / 7`, arrows on the keyboard. `StageRuler` takes a `span` and FITS it to its box (`useElementWidth`, no zoom prop, `rulerDayWidth(width, total, 1)`): geometry against the span, edits against the real trip; a bar the span clips loses the clipped edge's handle and corner (`clipStart`/`clipEnd`). `StagesPanel` names the span in its header and starts a new leg inside it. **Both overview zoom pills are gone**; `StageZoomControl`/`useStageZoom` survive only for `MediaLightbox` (the ruler's pill came back on 2026-09-28, «The stage ruler zooms again»). The loupe is not stored: where you are looking is not part of the trip.

**A right-click on a day TELLS it first, then edits its stage (2026-09-13).** The day menu (`DayMenuItem.group`) opens on "Tell this day · Reel · Carousel · Single photo" — a piece created there lands on the day CLICKED, not the day open below, and opens straight away — then a rule, then "Stage" with the leg edits. Pieces first because telling days is what the grid is for; a separator because the two groups act on different things. Same menu on the heatmap and the short strip. **Cells have no border** (the maintainer's mock, his call on elegance): the rung colour is the cell, the open day is a 2px ink OUTLINE (no layout, the lattice never shifts) and today an inset muted ring — and both now read tokens, where the old hard-coded ink hex vanished in the dark theme.

**Brought back to the mock (2026-09-13, the maintainer's diff):** day cells carry NO leg stripe any more — the lane under the grid (and the ruler) is where a leg is read, and the tint on 365 feet was noise; the hover card still names the leg. The legend heads the section ("The journey · nothing ▢▢▢ often · click / right-click / loupe hints") instead of trailing the grid. The loupe FRAME encloses the month labels (transparent, a 2px line), its grip is a small tab on the top edge and its edges are two black pills at mid-height — a solid bar over the labels was the first cut. The ruler carries a RUNG STRIP (`rungAt`, the grid's own levels) under its head, and a bar says its days ("42 d"). The stages header reads "Loupe · 21 Jul → 7 Sep · 49 days" on a long trip. **The loupe lives in WEEKS (rev. same day, his second screenshot):** its frame is drawn on the whole columns holding its first and last day, 5px off them, and every drag and arrow moves it by a week, edges snapping to Monday / Sunday (`defaultLoupe` opens on a Monday). Placed at the start day's fraction of a column, the edge and its handle sat mid-cell and a trip's lone first Sunday could not be clicked. The handles take the pointer only in a 40px band around their pill, and a pointer never focuses them (a focused handle drew its ring as a second outline). **Trap:** the grid's box is `overflow-x-auto`, which clips VERTICALLY too — the grip above the month labels was cut off; anything an overlay draws past the grid needs padding inside that box (`pt-4 pb-6` when an overlay is passed). The frame closes 8px under the last lane (18 was judged too much).

**The loupe is moved from anywhere, top and bottom (2026-09-14, the maintainer's ask: the 12px grip took aiming, on a phone above all).** (1) Its BODY slides it: `LoupeBrush` watches presses from the grid box natively (the frame itself still takes no pointer, so a tap opens the day under it), and a press becomes a slide by `pressIntent` (`shared/ui/press-intent.ts`, tested) — a mouse past 6px of travel, a finger only after a 350ms still hold, because a finger that travels at once is scrolling the page; the click it ends in is swallowed, Android's long-press context menu too. The grip and edge handles stay. (2) The RULER scrolls the loupe: a sideways wheel (trackpad, shift + wheel) anywhere over it, and a swipe. Content follows the finger; pixels become whole weeks through `panWeeks` (carry kept, loupe.ts), and a track wider than its box scrolls in its box first. Panning never moves the open day. **Trap fixed on the way:** `TripOverview`'s follow-the-open-day effect was keyed on the whole `trip`, so ANY edit (a leg dragged in a window scrolled away) yanked the loupe back to the open day — key it on the dates and the day only.

**The ruler is ONE gesture surface, and a throw keeps going (2026-09-16, reported from the maintainer's phone: a swipe on the stage/legs band "only works on the first instant").** Two faults, both measured in headless Chromium with real touch events (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent` at 390×844). (1) The swipe was confined to the head and the scale, two bands 16 and 26px tall on a phone, and they held the gesture with `touch-pan-y` alone — which the browser takes back on the first diagonal move (`frontend.md`, the rule this produced). (2) **Everything between them answered a swipe by EDITING**: the lanes are the track's whole middle, a bar claimed its drag at pointerdown, so a finger meaning to scroll dragged a leg a month back, and one starting on an edge handle collapsed a 21-day leg to one day. Now: the whole track is the surface (`useFlingPan` on the track element, `touch-pan-y` as the fallback only), a TAP still opens what it landed on — a day anywhere, a leg on its bar — a SWIPE travels from any point of it and glides on after the lift, stopping dead at the trip's edge (`panBy` answers false, `onPan` returns the weeks it really moved), and a leg is picked up by a MOUSE at once but by a FINGER only after the same 350ms still hold the loupe's body uses (`pressIntent`; the hook stands down while a leg is held, and gives up a pending hold the moment the finger travels). The two band `<div>`s are gone; the scale keeps its `pointer-coarse` rail as pure decoration. Verified end to end: a diagonal swipe across the lanes moves the window six weeks and leaves both legs untouched, a flick glides and lands on 1 Jan, a hold-then-drag still moves a leg, taps still open a day and a leg, a vertical swipe still scrolls the page, and `prefers-reduced-motion` gets the travel without the throw.

**Mobile border fix on a long trip (2026-09-14).** Reported: on a phone, a long trip's heatmap + loupe read as too-round cells, a too-thick black loupe frame, and the open day's own square looking like it broke the frame's border. Two separate causes. (1) The cell's `rounded-[3px]` was fixed regardless of `cellPx` — at the 6px floor a 300+ day trip hits on a phone, 3px reads as a near-circle, not a square (fixed: `cellRadius = clamp(1, floor(cellPx/4), 3)` in `DayHeatmap.tsx`, applied via inline `borderRadius` since a class can't take a computed value); the frame kept a flat `border-2 rounded-[7px]` meant for a roomier grid, disproportionate at that same floor (thinned to `border` + `rounded-[5px]` in `LoupeBrush.tsx`). (2) **The real cause of the "cut" look was geometric, not paint order**: the OPEN day always carries a 2px outline 1px off its edges (a 3px halo), and opening/dragging the loupe routinely lands the open day in the loupe's own EDGE column (`loupeContaining` centres or ends the window on it) — at `OUTSET`'s old 5px, that halo reached to within 2px of the frame's own border, and at a phone's pixel density the two visually fused into one lumpy line. A `z-10` on `LoupeBrush`'s root was tried first (the instinct: "raise the z-index") and made no visible difference — confirmed with `getBoundingClientRect` in a live headless Chromium session that the two shapes' boxes really do sit that close, regardless of stacking order; a 1px frame border is simply too thin to visually cover a several-px-wide halo beside it. Fixed by widening the gap instead: `OUTSET` 5 → 8, the open day's `outlineOffset` 1 → 0 (2px halo instead of 3px), and the scroll box's side padding `pl-2 pr-2` → `pl-3 pr-3` to keep the wider frame from clipping at the trip's first/last week. **How to apply**: two elements that visually abut (a selection ring, an overlay frame) need real pixel clearance between their outermost edges, not just a z-index — z-index only decides who wins on the pixels they actually share, and does nothing when the problem is that both are drawn, just close enough to blur together.

**The overview is not usable on a phone, and the numbers say why (2026-09-22, his report: «la heatmap, certes, est élégante vue de haut sur les 345 jours… mais pas du tout exploitable, impossible de cliquer sur le jour sans se tromper»).** MEASURED at 390px, where the shell's `px-4` leaves 358 and the grid's own `pl-3 pr-3` leaves 334: `fittedColumn(334, 50)` gives a **6 × 6px** cell on a 345-day trip — a seventh of the 44px a touch target needs, 1/53 of its area — and a ONE-MONTH trip is no better, `ShortDayStrip`'s `flex-1` cells coming to **7,7px** wide, too narrow to write the `Mo 12` above them. The ruler's day is `max(6, 358/56)` = 6,4px, so a leg's 10px handle is 1,5 days wide. Three surfaces scroll on three axes at two scales (4,4px per WEEK on the grid, 6,4px per DAY on the ruler, the loupe travelling over the first to move the second), and the whole column still comes to ~560px of the 844 available: **the room is there, the calendar refuses it because it insists on showing the year at once.** **The finding that shapes any fix**: on a phone the weekday grid and the day ruler are INVERSELY coupled and cannot both be usable on one span — a 28px cell puts ~73 days on screen, which is 4,9px a day on the ruler (under its own `MIN_DAY`, so it scrolls again and the two disagree), and an 8px ruler day would ask for a 47px cell, 329px of calendar height. So a phone gets ONE day surface; the ruler survives on the wide screen. Three directions, the boards, and what is his to decide: `docs/roadtrip-overview-mobile.md` (A «Le ruban» — the same grid, 28px cells, scrolled, legs in the same scroller; B «Les mois» — the axis turned, a month per block, 46px cells falling out of `width/7`, and leg dates dragged at 46px a day instead of 6; C «Les escales» — L3 on a phone, legs as the spine, a day as a 48px line that can SAY what came out). **Two decisions they share, and they are the proposal**: the whole-trip heatmap stops being a TARGET and becomes a MAP (read, and jumped from — twelve targets, not 345; his 2026-09-13 refusal to drop it is respected, it is kept and re-purposed), and **the loupe becomes the scroll position** rather than a dragged frame, which retires `LoupeBrush`, its 350ms hold, `useFlingPan` and `panWeeks` from the phone and answers «scrollable de manière native» literally. Nothing is built; above 820px nothing changes.

**He chose B the same day, and B in full is designed (2026-09-22, `docs/roadtrip-overview-mobile.md` §8).** Facts it rests on, all checked in the code rather than assumed: **the shell's bottom bar is ALREADY drawn on the overview** — `showRail = compact && !!tool` (`App.tsx:175`) has no condition on the screen and `SectionRail`'s own comment says so, so its 57px are paid whatever we do and the design SPENDS them (Trips publishes **Étapes** and **Voyage** beside `Library`, and the ⚙ leaves the top bar — a control removed, not added); and the browser's own chrome is already out of the budget, because `--app-h` is MEASURED (`app-height.ts`). What is left on an iPhone in Safari is **624px** (730 − 48 masthead − 57 bar), which is the number every height below is designed against: trip bar 44 (against ~110 for a serif `<h1>` + subtitle + three figures — on a phone they are one row), year map 58, day strip 68, **455 for the calendar** where a month block asks 414. A home-screen launch gives ~95px back, already paid for. **The day**: a 68px STRIP always above the bar (date, leg, the day's pieces as hook thumbnails at their OWN frame — solid accent border published, dashed draft — and a count), pulling up into a ~470px sheet that is `DayPanel`'s existing rows plus the leg in one row with *Modifier* and, last, what the instance holds for that date; nothing new is stored, the `thumbs` store already holds every hook. **The legs**: the Étapes sheet lists the twelve with their own coverage barcode, the open one carries its fields, the ruler's gap `+` becomes a row — and **«Ajuster sur le calendrier» is what replaces the ruler on a phone**: the days outside the leg fade, its two ends carry a 28px round grip dragged along the cells at **one day = one cell = 46px** (against 6,4px on the ruler today), a long press offers *commencer/finir ici*, and a visible ± stepper is the keyboard twin nothing in this suite goes without. **A tap on a day SELECTS it and never opens the sheet** — the calendar is what you sweep, and a sheet per cell would cover it and cost a dismissal per day; the STRIP is what opens it, which is only affordable because the strip never scrolls (three thumbnails then `+2`, the list being the sheet's job) and because a day nothing came out of carries `+ Raconter` instead, the tool's commonest gesture. A second tap on the already-open cell was rejected on the deck band's own September precedent (undiscoverable). A **pellicule** VIEW swaps the rung ramp for the hook thumbnails at month scale — same cells, same targets, only the fill changes — and its toggle is ONE control in the trip bar, a `localStorage` preference like the gallery's Cards / Bands, never repeated per month header: a view belongs to the trip, not to a block of it. The ramp stays the default (the tool's question is about holes), and whether it should is the one thing only his own trip can settle. **Built so far (his «développe le tout», 2026-09-22): C1** — `shared/roadtrip/month-grid.ts`, pure and tested: `monthBlocks` draws the WHOLE calendar month (a week starting mid-month reads as a mistake) with the trip's own days listed apart, says the year on the first block and every January, `weekRuns` is the generic run-length a leg ribbon is drawn from (broken by a null cell or a null answer), `monthCell(width)` is a seventh of what the gutters leave clamped 28..56 (47 at 358px), and `visibleBlock` reads the block under the viewport's upper third. **C2** — `MonthCalendar.tsx` + `YearMap.tsx` on the compact shell: the calendar is the column's ONE scroller (`overscroll-y-contain`, the page never scrolls sideways — 0px measured), the map frames `visibleBlock` and jumps by MONTH (twelve targets), the cell is `monthCell(width)` — 50 × 45px measured at 390 because the shell's `px-2` leaves 374 —, a ribbon per week run (`weekRuns` keyed by stage id) named where the leg begins and where it enters a new month, an unnamed stage saying so like the ruler. Traps: the route-following effect jumps `'auto'` on MOUNT (the phone opens on the open day's month) and afterwards only when the selected cell is OFF SCREEN, or every tap would scroll; the wide screen is untouched, and until C3/C4 the day panel and the stages panel ride in the scroller's `tail` so nothing is lost on a phone in between. The compact trip bar is one pill-high row (back as a chevron, the name at `text-xl`, told/total as a chip) plus ONE mono line for the figures where the silence keeps its verb. **C3** — `DayStrip.tsx` (68px, `flex-none` above the rail, never scrolling: three hooks at their own frame then `+N`, the verb `+ Tell it` on an empty day) and the day SHEET (`BottomSheet` at 0.62/0.92 over `DayPanel`'s new `variant: 'sheet'` — no frame, no heading, the leg as a row with `Edit ›`, a row's four controls folded behind `⋯` into the calendar's own `DayMenu`, the verbs at 44px); `use-day-thumbs.ts` is the one read both draw from (`DayPanel` takes `thumbs` and reads only when not given). **Trap, measured**: a `DayMenu` rendered INSIDE a row whose `<li>` opens the piece bubbles every item's click up to `onOpen` — every menu item opened the editor; it is wrapped in a `stopPropagation` span. Verified in headless Chromium: a tap on a cell opens no dialog and the strip re-reads, the strip opens the sheet, a piece made from it opens straight away and the strip counts it with its thumbnail on return, `⋯` marks published, Escape closes. **C4** — the overview PUBLISHES two cells to the shell's bar on compact (`usePublishSectionBar`: `Stages` · `Trip`, marked only while their sheet is up, the piece editor's rule) and the cog leaves the compact bar; `LegsSheet.tsx` is the ruler's job as a list (a row per leg with its own coverage barcode sampled to 26 bars, the open row unfolding the SAME `StageCard` — now exported from `StagesPanel` — and the ruler's gap `+` as rows with `+ cover`, sorted into lived order with the legs); a ribbon tap and the day sheet's `Edit ›` open the sheet on that leg WITHOUT moving the open day (`openStage` navigates to the leg's first day, which on a phone would yank the calendar); the stages panel and its ruler leave the compact screen entirely. `Trip` opens `TripDetailsModal`. Verified: cells `Library · Stages · Trip`, no cog, no ruler, marking follows the sheet, `+ Stage` opens a card whose fields write the row, gaps become rows. **C5** — «Adjust on the calendar» (`StageCard.onAdjust`, offered by the legs sheet and never by the wide screen, which has the ruler): a DRAFT of the leg's dates held in `TripOverview` (`adjusting`), written on Done and dropped on Cancel/Escape — the garage's rule for a modal edit; `MonthCalendar.adjust` draws ONLY that leg at its draft dates (`legOf` answers it alone), fades every other day to 0.34, and puts a 28px GRIP on each end that alone takes the pointer (`touch-none`) and reads the day under the finger from `elementFromPoint(...).closest('[data-date]')` — so the drag crosses weeks and one cell is one day; a tap on a day moves the NEARER edge (`nearerEdge`, `stage-edit.ts`, tested: outside the leg the side it lies on, inside the nearer, a tie to the end); a visible `‹ date ›` stepper per edge is the keyboard twin (Shift = a week), in the strip's place; entering the mode JUMPS the calendar to the leg's start month, keyed on the leg's id alone so a moved start does not scroll. **Two traps, both measured**: a block's `offsetTop` is measured from the nearest positioned ancestor, so without `relative` on the scroller every jump landed ~250px past the block's top (the July capture in C2 was already hiding its first three weeks and nobody saw it); and writing the trip from INSIDE a `setState` updater is a setState on the tool during this component's render — React warns, and the write must read the closure instead. Verified: the sheet closes into the mode, the leg is brought on screen, grips 28px, days outside fade, a tap, both steppers and a drag over three cells each move the right edge by the right days, Cancel keeps the stored dates, Done writes them, the strip comes back. **C6** — the PICTURES view: `atelier.roadtrip.calendar.view` in `localStorage` (the gallery's pattern, try/catch for private mode), ONE `Segmented` in the compact bar (icon-only, `sr-only` labels, never per month), and `MonthCalendar.pictures` — a told day draws its hook as `background: url() cover` with the number on a `bg-frame/55` foot strip, a count badge past one piece, a solid accent border where any went out and a dashed one for drafts; a told day whose hook is not read (or never painted) keeps its RUNG, the honest fallback. **The hooks are read for the month on screen and its two neighbours only** (`onVisible` → `windowPosts` → the same `useDayThumbs`), never the trip's: a year of pieces is a year of JPEGs in memory for cells off screen, the reason `use-cover-thumbs` reads candidates alone. Since 2026-09-28 that read is INCREMENTAL (`thumb-window.ts`, tested): a month boundary reads only the month that came in and revokes only the one that left — the hook used to re-read all three months and mint new URLs, and every cell flashed. A re-baked hook keeps the same id, and is read fresh anyway because the overview unmounts while its piece is edited. Verified: rungs by default, the hook drawn on a told day and not on an untold one, `2` on a day with two pieces, dashed on a draft, the choice survives a reload, one toggle on the screen. **C7** — the WIDE screen is the B6 board: the same blocks side by side (`MonthCalendar.columns` is a MAXIMUM — two above 820px, three above 1180 — and the real count is what the width allows at a 34px cell, `FIT_CELL`, so 1280 beside the library's rail gives two blocks of 52px cells rather than three of 32), the year map above them, the `StagesPanel` BETWEEN map and blocks with its ruler on the month on screen and its two neighbours (`spanOnScreen`, ~9px a day — better than the dragged loupe's 6.4, which is why the brush could go), and above 1180px an `aside` of 22rem holding the day panel and the open leg's `StageCard` (`StagesPanel.hideCard`); below 1180 both ride under the calendar. `visibleBlock` answers the FIRST block of a row (a row is read from its left), or a three-block row framed its last month. Retired: `LoupeBrush.tsx`, `ShortDayStrip.tsx`, the grid half of `DayHeatmap.tsx` (its day card, menu, `levelOf` and `cellTitle` stay, shared by the calendar), the loupe state and `panLoupe`; `loupe.ts` stayed for the ruler's `panWeeks` (and became the ruler's zoomable window on 2026-09-28), `day-grid.ts` for a zoom-anchor test. **This supersedes two September rulings by his approval of the boards**: the dragged loupe (L1) and «the open day stays under the calendar; a right column was offered, not chosen». The heatmap itself is NOT dropped — it is the map. Verified at 1280 and 1000: 345 cells of 52 and 56px, blocks side by side, the ruler naming the months on screen and following a jump, the day and the leg in the aside at 1280 and under the calendar at 1000, 0px overflow. *Adjust* is a phone gesture only: the wide screen keeps the ruler, which does the same job at a pointer's precision. Four smaller questions stay open (§9). **Retouches from his first hands-on (2026-09-22, the seven points of <https://claude.ai/artifact/JCTtxVCRdLUruJNhuwiHDs>): R1** — the compact overview leaves the shell's `px-2` (`-mx-2`) and `MonthCalendar.gutter` pays the room INSIDE the scroller and under the map, because a selected Monday's outline was clipped against the shell's gutter (the `frontend.md` rule, met again); the `DayStrip` is the SHEET FOLDED — edge to edge, the sheet's own `rounded-t-paper-lg`, border and shadow, the grip centred — since a strip stopping 8px short of the screen read as cut next to the full-bleed sheet it opens into. **R2** — a leg ribbon has NO cap (the `inset 3px` of full tint that marked a start drew a head on every leg — his "radical" cut) and one rule for its shape: 6px at the leg's two real ends, 0 where the week cuts it, so a continuation reads as one and a whole leg is a pill; a name is written only on a run of three cells or more, and a leg starting on a weekend is named on the row after (`carried`), never as three letters and an ellipsis. **R3** (`frontend.md` has the sheet rule) — `BottomSheet` LEAVES with an animation: every way out asks for the close, the panel plays `sheet-fall` and the scrim fades, and `onClose` (which unmounts it) fires on `animationend` with a 300 ms guard; a host that closes the sheet itself — the day sheet's `Edit ›` swapping into the legs sheet — still cuts, on purpose. The whole HEAD (grip + title row, 45px) is the drag and tap surface, the ✕ excluded by `stopPropagation` on its pointerdown; the grip is a 12px strip with a 28 × 3 bar. **R4** — the map's frame is READ AT THE PIXEL and DRAGGED, which reverses the brief's «nothing here is dragged» (§3.2) by his ask: one map column IS one calendar week row (`weekIndexOf`, the same Monday origin as `heatmapWeeks`), so `visibleWeekSpan` turns the scroller's rows (`data-week`, measured on every scroll — 60 `offsetTop` reads, one layout) into a fractional span and `scrollForWeek` is its inverse; a press on the band becomes a drag past 4px (captured only then, so the month buttons keep their tap), grabbed inside the frame it keeps its offset, outside it is carried by its middle; the scroll it asks for is `behavior: 'auto'`, never smooth, or it lags the finger. Traps: the first block's padding week (before the trip) has a NEGATIVE index and the map no column for it, so scrolling through it moves nothing — correct, and what made the first browser check look wrong; a straddling week is two rows with one index, and both functions take the first. **R5** — a SHORT trip (`SHORT_TRIP_DAYS = 31`, the old strip's threshold, his pick over two months) is ONE block of its weeks with a week either side (`weekBlock` → key `weeks`, `tripBlocks` choosing), no year map, the header naming the month it starts in and a `MonthMark` on the row holding another month's 1st — because a one-column map and a whole month drawn for four days told him nothing (Normandie); the shape is the same on the wide screen (one block, the ruler on the whole trip). A consumer reading `block.key` as `YYYY-MM` must treat `weeks` first: the pictures window is then the whole trip, and `spanOnScreen` is undefined. **R6** — a sticky header over the page's ground is TRANSLUCENT paper with a `backdrop-blur`, never `bg-paper` flat: the ground is paper PLUS two radial gradients (`index.css`, the paper glow), so a flat of the token reads lighter than its surroundings wherever the gradient darkens — a rectangle in his screenshot. And one block to a row is centred (`mx-auto`): the cell caps at 56px, so a compact window wider than 440px otherwise leaves it stuck left. (R7 was the plural of «day of silence» — nothing to remember.) **R8 (2026-09-22, from a gesture profile, not a report)** — the calendar re-renders on EVERY scroll frame (the map's frame is its `span` state, fractional, so equality never holds) and on every hovered cell (the card's state lives in it), and `MonthBlockView` was not memoised: twelve blocks, 420 day buttons and their ribbons, each cell asking its leg, redrawn per scroll pixel. **Measured on a 345-day trip with 200 pieces, script time per gesture: 30 scroll steps 874 → 141 ms, 30 wheel ticks 592 → 98 ms, a 40-cell hover sweep 1 059 → 159 ms, at 1280 and 390 alike.** What did it: `memo` on the block with every prop the calendar hands down made stable (`NO_HOVER` constant, `onMenu` a `useCallback`, one ref callback per block memoised on the block list — a fresh closure per render re-attaches every ref), the scroll read coalesced to one `requestAnimationFrame`, the year map's 350 cells built once per trip and size so only its frame moves, and the overview's `stageOf` / `onOpenLeg` as `useCallback`s (an inline arrow there re-rendered every block each time `onVisible` fired, once per month scrolled past). **How to apply**: a component the scroll or the pointer re-renders per frame must hand its heavy children stable props and `memo` them; the profile that finds this is script time per gesture (CDP `Performance.getMetrics`), never long tasks — 28 ms a step is many tasks under 50 ms. **The piece EDITOR was measured the same day and left alone**: its clock is `useDeckTransport`'s `still` state (and `setPlayhead` from the stage on a clip), so the whole `PostEditor` renders every frame of playback — ~150–200 ms of script per second on the DEV server, a CPU profile showing it is almost all React's own dev-mode validation (`validateProperty`, `jsxDEV`) and reconciliation, the suite's own code under 60 ms in 3 s. Moving the clock into a store the stage and the deck strip subscribe to is the fix if a phone ever shows it; it touches three files and the badge's timing, and the measured payoff did not justify it blind. What WAS made cheap in that render: `useTripGrade` (and Develop's `useRollGrade`) stringified the stored grade on every render to detect a change — memoised on the grade's identity now, because a legacy upload's whole `.cube` sits inlined in `customText` and once a frame was the drag. **Four more rules from the same audit (2026-09-22)**: assigning a canvas's `width`/`height` CLEARS it and reallocates its store even to the same value, so `BadgeStage` compares first (it assigned on every paint, sixty a second while playing); a paint that AWAITS (the fonts, in `renderBadge`) takes a sequence number and a `live()` check after the await, or a paint the next one overtook lands its complete frame over the newer one and hands a stale canvas to the thumbnail — `RenderBadgeOptions.live`; the closing card's `qr` is memoised, since it is a paint dependency and a fresh object per render repainted the card on every unrelated render; the `MediaActions` offer reads `startPiece` through a REF and is keyed on the day alone, because keyed on the callback it was rebuilt on every trip edit and each rebuild published null then the record — two provider updates re-rendering the Library and the lightbox per keystroke; and the rail thumbnail's signature carries `post.badge.hook` and `trip.car`, because a folded hook serialises to four scalars (its paint is a closure) and an Itinerary stop moved or a Virée colour changed left the cell as it was. The gallery cards memoise `tripCoverage(trip)` (it walked every day of every trip on every render), and the hook clip download goes through `shared/media/save.ts`'s `downloadBlob` — the local copy revoked the URL synchronously, which cancels a multi-megabyte download in Firefox and Safari.
