**Agreed (maintainer, ruling on the UI audit).** A short trip (proposed threshold: up to 31 days, derived from the two dates, no setting) gets a plain day strip — each day a real width, heatmap and legs merged, no zoom pill. The whole overview loses its clutter at any length: counts move into the heading beside the name (the "longest stretch" sentence becomes a clickable silence figure), one frame level (the open leg is a row, never a grey card inside a card), a one-line legend, a shared `DateRangeField` instead of native date inputs.

**Refused: dropping the heatmap for long trips.** His words: on a year-long trip (his Australia trip) the heatmap "was a good idea", it is the only thing that shows the whole journey at once. The leg ruler's drag-and-drop is also kept ("more or less OK"). **How to apply**: never propose a long-trip overview without the weekday heatmap, and never replace the ruler's drag gestures.

**The long-trip defect measured (2026-09-13)**: the heatmap and the ruler do not share an axis — a week is ~17px on the grid and ~42px on the ruler (6px `MIN_DAY` floor × 7), each scrolls on its own, a year's ruler is ~2190px so it shows about five months, and the grid shows legs only as 3px foot stripes. **Chosen by the maintainer (2026-09-13): L1** ("C'est parti avec L1"). The three, for the record: L1 the heatmap fitted to the full width with a leg LANE drawn on the same week axis beneath it, and a draggable LOUPE window on it that the existing day ruler details below — the window replaces both zoom pills and gives the ruler a comfortable day width; L2 one row per month (31 cells), legs as foot stripes, drag cut at month ends; L3 legs first, a thin whole-trip barcode over one row per leg with its own mini heatmap, best past ~30 legs and a candidate second view later (like the gallery's Cards / Bands). **Defaults taken with the go-ahead, not ruled on**: the short/long threshold is 31 days, derived from the dates; the open day stays under the calendar (a right column above 1180px was offered, not chosen); the legacy pages' group is called "Instruments".

**Défilé's sweep is authored (2026-09-13): easing, a hold, and the days by name.** Rules that came out of building it: (1) **every easing offered must have a closed-form inverse** — stops are placed on the inverse and the head glides on the curve, so a numeric inverse would land a stop NEAR its time and the numeral, the flash and the tick would each read a different frame; `EASINGS` in `scrub-plan.ts` is the whole table and a round-trip test guards it (it caught a wrong `ease-in-out` inverse first time). (2) The opener's life is `plan.endSeconds` (hold + sweep), never `sweepSeconds` — every reader of "is it still sweeping / where is rest" moved to it when the hold arrived. (3) The author's picture choice is DATA the variant hands the shell (`wantsPictures`, refs under keys since 2026-09-14 — see «Défilé flashes SOURCE pictures»), never a fetch. **Trap, measured**: a tile that switches its wrapper element (`<span>` reporting vs `<button>` toggling) REMOUNTS its canvas, and a paint effect keyed on the unchanged picture never repaints the new node — every tile came back transparent on a pixel readback. A canvas painted from React uses a **callback ref**, which runs on every attach.

**Défilé's ticks are tuned in the score (2026-09-13): kit, pitch, drift.** Same rule as the volume — every tuning is an event property (`voice`, `rate`) written by `scrubScore`, never a node downstream, so live playback, a still's bed, a silent clip's track and a mix follow one set of numbers. `SCRUB_KITS` keeps two invariants across every kit: a leg's landing is the DIFFERENT one (a distinct voice on the ratchet, the same voice lower and a little louder elsewhere), and the seat is the seat everywhere — it ends the phrase rather than being a step in it, which is also why a drift moves the landings and not the seat. Pitch is read out in semitones (`12·log2`), the unit an ear thinks in. **How to measure a pitch change without ears**: zero-crossing rate over a tick's first 30ms; coarse on band-passed noise (an octave reads as ≈×1.76), but direction and order are unambiguous.

**Défilé's tape look is authored (2026-09-13).** Width, edge offset, two colours, opacity, height, spacing, track, band, edge fade, head shape, glow — all `ScrubOptions`, all validated on read (a colour that is not `#rrggbb` falls back, never throws). Two rules to keep: **the geometry and the fade are pure and tested** (`tapeGeometry`, `edgeFadeAt`, `hexToRgba` in `scrub-plan.ts`) and `paintTape` only reads them, so a variant of the tape is a set of numbers and not a second painter; and **the fade is a per-tick alpha plus a gradient fill** for the band and the track, never a mask or a `destination-*` composite — the head's glow is a shadow, and a shadow is dropped under those (`studio.md`). The spacing slider IS `tapeTicks`' `minGapPx`, so the same rule thins a long trip and spaces a short one. The band is for a bright picture; a scrim still dims the tape because the seam is under the shades (`docs/hook-engine.md` §12).

**Virée — the car on the map (2026-09-14).** The engine's third real variant, built on the maintainer's brief (a map with a route, stops chosen or derived from the picked photos, a cartoon black Toyota Prado in 3D driving from stop to stop with halts to show pictures — "surprends-moi"): `shared/roadtrip/hooks/drive*.ts`, `mesh3d.ts`, `car-model.ts`; the design and the file map are `docs/hook-engine.md` §13. What the code does not say: (1) **The car is a hand-rolled software renderer, never a 3D library** — the maintainer's "optimisée, fluide" is met by ~180 convex-part faces through a painter's algorithm in the same 2D context every opener paints in; Three.js was weighed and declined (a dependency the size of the tool, a WebGL context per stage that is never reclaimed, a copy per frame). A future 3D miniature (a plane, a boat) is a second `*-model.ts` over `mesh3d.ts`, and its parts must stay CONVEX or the ordering breaks — `render-order.test.ts` now says so out loud. (2) **The default car is black because the brief said so, and a black car needs the highlight**: `DEFAULT_LIGHT.gloss` is what keeps it a shape; if the palette ever changes, check the black car first. (3) **A stop is what the document can justify**: a located place or a geotagged picture; an unlocated picture rides with the stop before it or the end of its day's leg, and what fits nowhere is COUNTED in the panel — never a guessed spot (the route trace's rule, kept). (4) **The map is drawn, not fetched** — the paper, the graticule and the scale bar come from the route's own projection; OSM tiles were not added, and adding them would be a third network exception to put to the maintainer, not a paint option. **He asked for it on 2026-09-28**: a third ground, `tiles` — see «Tiles INSIDE the openers» below; paper stays the default and the fallback. (5) **The prints are a PILE beside the stop, and the car is drawn over them**: the first render spread the cards across the road and buried the car under them, which reads as the map eating its own protagonist. (6) **The reveal is a buffer canvas**, not per-stroke alphas — the one canvas beyond the frame's, kept in the closure's scratch and remade on a size change. Verified in headless Chromium through `prepare` → `paint` on a fixture (five option sets, eight frames each) and with the panel mounted standalone; a real export and the chooser's GPS reading on real files were not exercised here.

**Virée drives YOUR places too, with the Itinerary's own editor (2026-09-28).** His ask: *«le même picker [que l'Itinéraire] pour la vue carte voiture … peut-être un composant commun»* — places that are not in the legs. `DriveOptions.stopsOn` gained `custom` (labels **Legs · Your map · Photos**: three longer words overlapped in the 200px control column, measured) and `stops: MapStop[]`; the model and its edits moved to `hooks/stops.ts` (map-plan re-exports the old names — the `easing.ts` rule: what two variants want lives beside the contract), the picker to `hooks/stops-editor.tsx`, drawn by both panels, each saying only what a stop's picture does in its drawing. **Rules**: a custom stop is the author's assertion (point (3) above extends to it, as for the Itinerary): named as typed, `leg: null`, its own picture shown there whatever day it was shot; a PICKED picture with a position joins the nearest stop, one without is counted (`unlocated`) — never guessed; the caption may follow the car on `custom` as on `places` (a day name on `pictures` is not a place). **One list followed across openers**: `HookVariant.sharedStops` names the key; `switchHookVariant(…, from)` hands the LEFT opener's non-empty list to the chosen one (the newest edit wins over what the chosen one was left with; an empty list hands nothing, so a Virée that never used its own places cannot wipe an Itinerary's), and `fresh` (Virée: `stopsOn: 'custom'`) applies only the first time the chosen opener is used. Verified headless: three stops clicked on an Itinerary were driven by Virée on `Your map`, a fourth added there came back to the Itinerary, the car drawn on the paper between them at 1.3 s. The editor's picking field still takes the Itinerary's bow; Virée passes a small one for `curved` since its Catmull-Rom has no single bow.

**The car is the TRIP's, dressed in a garage (2026-09-15).** `TripDoc.car` (**v19**, portable — the backup carries it) holds the model, the colour, the finish and the gear every Virée of the trip drives; the maintainer chose the trip over the piece (a journey has one car), and asked for a whole modal with the car in 360°. What the code does not say: (1) the migration reads through `readVehicleSpec` so a document with junk or nothing lands on HIS car — the Prado in Raptor black, fully geared — which is what the opener drew before the field existed. (2) **`tsc -b` type-checks the specs**: five test files build `TripDoc` literals (`deck`, `trip-cover`, `day-badge`, `slide-render`, `trip-coverage`), so a required field on the document fails the BUILD until each fixture carries it — budget that into any future `TripDoc` field. (3) A matte black car is a silhouette under the renderer's pure multiplier; `Light.sheen` (an additive term from the key, default 0) is what keeps it a shape, and `vehicleLight(finish)` is the one place a finish becomes a light — check the black car first after any change to the palette or the light. (4) The gear geometry is CONVEX by construction — a hoop, a rail, a handle are several boxes — and the load sits where the photographs have it (solar left, box front right, three cans across the rear: water · petrol · water), which a test pins. (5) **The garage is ONE controlled panel with TWO homes** (`GaragePanel`, the `CoverPanel` rule): the trip settings sheet's Car section writes on every switch, the `GarageModal` the Virée panel opens from the piece holds a DRAFT and writes on Done — a stage that redraws behind the sheet on every flag is a distraction, and Escape must leave the trip as it was. The panel reaches the modal through one host verb, `HookPanelHost.configureVehicle`, set by the picker from a prop that comes down `PostEditor → LookTab → HookPicker`: the picker's contract stays the piece's opener and never receives the trip. **Side by side, not stacked (2026-09-16)** — the maintainer's report: flipping a switch meant scrolling down to it and back up to the car. The panel now lays ITSELF out on its own width (a container query at 44rem, because its two homes give it different room): the turntable fills one column and the choices scroll in the other, the panel taking its host's definite height; narrower it stacks and the host scrolls as before. Both sheets grew for it — the trip settings sheet is 80rem × 54rem at most at EVERY section (his call: bigger all the time is fine), the garage modal 68rem — so a desktop reads rail · car · choices. A fitting that needs another (spot lights → bar, roof load → basket) is a row listed only while that one is on; the flag stays stored and `effectiveGear` is what draws. (6) **The turntable is the drive's renderer, not a second drawing**, and its angle is a LOOK: never stored, never the piece's Camera row. Its loop runs only while it turns — a gesture pauses it, it resumes 1.5 s later, and `prefersReducedMotion()` (now `shared/ui/reduced-motion.ts`) keeps it still, repainting only when the car changes; the view is written through refs and repainted directly, never a state per pointer move; `touch-pan-y` so a finger turns the car and the sheet still scrolls, and only a mouse or pen tilts. The paper is read off the element's CSS tokens so the dark theme paints its own. (7) Verified in headless Chromium, not on the maintainer's phone: the frames with and without `ctx.car`, the panel standalone (toggle repaints, drag and keys turn, reduced motion stops the loop), the sheet at 1280 and the drill-down at 390, and the modal opened from a real piece.

**The hull is cut into panels, and nothing shares a volume (2026-09-17).** The maintainer reported the bonnet, the glass and the wheel flares going see-through as the car turned, and suspected the sort had been lost — it had not. Two causes, both geometry: `body` was ONE box 4.6 m long whose top was a SINGLE polygon, so with the nose toward the camera it took its place in the order from the vertex at the TAILGATE and the bonnet was laid down as if it sat 4.6 m away; and every wheel was modelled INSIDE a body with no arches. What the code does not say: (1) **No polygon may span the model.** The hull is a stack of convex slices (`hullSlice`, `hullBlock`) cut at the shut lines a car really has — the base of the windscreen, the top of the tailgate — and at a `shoulder` above the wheels. A slice is keyed by its own extent, which is the whole point; cutting at a place a car has no line would buy the same correctness and show a seam, because `paintMesh` inks once per FACE. (2) **An interior face is worse than useless.** Stacking blocks creates horizontal faces buried under the block resting on them: invisible in reality, but still faces the order has to place, and it placed them over the bonnet. `extrude` takes `null` for a cap to not build it at all. The same rule retired the arch's inner wings entirely — the wheel standing in the gap hides them from every angle the camera can take. (3) **The arch is an ABSENCE.** Across each axle the skirt simply is not there; a body narrowed to clear a wheel would need end walls that tie with their neighbours' and sort on top of them. (4) **Everything that sits ON the hull reads `HULL`.** The corner lights, the grille and the badge used to hard-code the body's own numbers in a second place, so a change to the body silently detached them. A lamp crossing the shoulder is CUT at it (`bands`), because a decal is ordered exactly only while it lies inside the one face it is pushed off — and a decal carries no ink, so the cut is invisible. (5) Measured over the sweep: 6.6% → **3.0%** of a view showing a surface that is not the nearest, worst pose 13.5% → 7.3%, worst part 20% → 36% of itself. What remains is a long panel keyed by its far corner, not geometry sharing a volume. (6) **A BSP tree is the textbook cure and is ruled out**: it splits polygons where they cross, and one stroke per face means every fragment would draw its own line and scribble seams across the panels. The cartoon style does not cause the fault, but it does forbid that fix.

**The paint order sorts FACES, never parts (2026-09-16).** The maintainer reported pieces of the car going missing as he turned it. The cause was `renderOrder` ordering PARTS by the depth of `part.centre`: comparing two convex solids by one scalar is only sound when neither one's span along the view contains the other's, and this model breaks that everywhere — a decal lies ON the face carrying it, the cabin sits ON a body whose top face is one polygon spanning the whole car. Measured over a sweep, the old rule buried the solar panel and its frame to **0%** of themselves, the cabin to 2% and the flares to 5%; the wrap-around corner lights vanished for a ~20° arc of headings at **every** elevation, the cabin for up to 95° of them below 46°. What the code does not say: (1) **The key is the face's FARTHEST vertex, and the reasons are specific.** A face that spans the car then sorts by the end genuinely behind everything, so it is laid down first and cannot cover what stands in front of it; and it is exact for a decal, because depth is linear over a plane, so the maximum over a polygon contained in another cannot exceed the container's — which is why a decal must stay INSIDE the face it is pushed off. The average key and the nearest-vertex key were both measured and are both worse (nearest catastrophically); a `0.85·max + 0.15·mean` blend trades a better whole-car average for a worse floor on the flares, so it was declined — the floor is what the complaint was about. Do not "simplify" any of this back. (2) **Convexity is load-bearing**, not a style note: it is what lets one global sort be right, because after culling no two visible faces of a convex part overlap. (3) **A painter's algorithm cannot order interpenetrating solids at all**, only least-badly; never widen the test's epsilon to hide one, separate the surfaces in the model instead — which is what the entry above then did. (4) **One key cannot order every pair, and the last few are SETTLED, not re-sorted (2026-09-17).** A long panel is keyed by its far corner, so the bonnet sorts behind a wheel standing under its near half — two faces not separable by any single number. After the sort, `settleOverlaps` asks the real question of the pairs whose screen boxes overlap: clip one projected outline against the other and read each face's own plane depth inside the shared region, then move the one that is really behind. Bounded on purpose (a fixed look-ahead, a fixed number of moves per face) so it cannot cycle and cannot grow with the model, and it costs about 1 ms a frame. It NEVER splits a polygon: that is the textbook cure and the one thing forbidden here, because the ink is stroked once per face and a fragment would draw its own line across the panel it came from. It took a view showing the wrong surface from 3.0% to **0.68%**, the worst pose to 1.9%, the worst part to 54% of itself. The sequence is deliberately no longer monotonic in depth — a test asserts the moves stay a small minority, which is what says this is a repair and not a second sort.


**A second car: the Kadjar (2026-09-28).** His ask: *« une nouvelle voiture : Kadjar 2022 bleu marine avec 2 barres de toit horizontales — le modèle 3D, les réglages et tout »* (`hooks/kadjar-model.ts`, a `VehicleLine` in `vehicle-spec.ts`). What the code does not say: (1) **"barres de toit horizontales" was read as two bars ACROSS the roof** — the accessory one buys to carry a box — and the factory longitudinal rails are a separate switch, off by default, on which the bars then stand. NOT confirmed with him: if he meant the rails, swap `VEHICLE_LINES['kadjar-ph2'].fitted`, no geometry changes. (2) **The gear is ONE vocabulary and each model offers its part** (`VehicleLine.gear`); a flag outside it is carried and ignored, and every reader — `sameVehicleSpec`, `gearWords`, `colourName`, the garage — asks the model which flags it means. Picking a model brings it AS IT COMES (a Kadjar in Raptor black with a bull-bar flag is nobody's car), and the car left behind is remembered for the length of the visit. No migration: nothing reads the two new flags on a Prado, and `readVehicleSpec` fills a Kadjar from the Kadjar's own defaults. (3) **The id is `kadjar-ph2`, not `kadjar-2022`**: the facelift ran 2018–2022 and an id outlives a word. Colours are Renault's range as remembered, the French name as the note, no paint code claimed; "Navy blue" is his word, brightened to `#1d2f5e` after the first render read black on the flanks. (4) **The hull is cut at shut lines chosen by the oracle, not only by the drawing.** Uncut, a long panel over each wheel (the bonnet, a 2.9 m mid block) put the worst view at 2.9% against the 3% gate; cut along the bonnet's edges, at the wings' ends by the headlamps, at the door gap and at the rear door's back edge, it is 0.76% with the worst part keeping 67% (the Prado: 2.31% / 54%). Also measured, not taste: 14 wheel facets rather than 16, the tyre a hair proud of the flank (either the other way lost the rear door to its wheel at a low camera), and the arch eyebrow in THREE pieces (in one it kept 68% of itself against its own tyre, in three 92%), the end pieces sloping so the seams read as an arch's facets. A decal crossing a cut is cut with it. (5) **A thin part is all outline**: the ink is a few pixels whatever the part, so bars drawn to scale read black; they are drawn wider than a real aero bar. Look at a new thin fitting at map size (~118 px long) before trusting it. (6) Verified: `render-order.test.ts` now judges every registry line (a third car is gated the day it is listed); renders in headless Chromium at garage and map size; the garage driven — switch, rails on, back to the Prado and back, the stored document read from IndexedDB; a Virée frame painted with the Kadjar. Not on his phone, not against a photograph of his car.

**A third car: the Trafic (2026-10-07).** His ask, with two photographs of the van: *« 2 voitures 3d à ajouter dans trips : Renault Trafic blanc avec panneau solaire sur le toit … et Renault Zoé blanche classique »* (`hooks/trafic-model.ts`, a `VehicleLine` in `vehicle-spec.ts`, id `trafic-ph2` — the third generation's 2019 facelift the photographs show, the C-shaped daytime lights and the chrome-barred grille). **The van's owner answered the next day (2026-10-08): *« j'ai un panneau de 430 watt et c'est un L2 »*** — so it is the LONG body (5.40 m, a 3.50 m wheelbase; the L1 was first drawn) and the panel a residential 430 W module, ~1.72 × 1.13 m, not the 1.5 × 1.0 guessed. Everything about the cab is placed from the NOSE, so the length went into the wheelbase and the sliding door alone; the model id stays `trafic-ph2` (the id names the generation, not the body length). What the code does not say: (1) **The Kadjar's private helpers became `car-parts.ts`** the day a third car asked for them — `makeBody(plan)` returns the hull slice, the wall/side/across decals and the diamond that know that plan; `roadWheel`, `spokedRim` (the Kadjar's alloy) and `cappedRim` (a van's steel wheel under a cap) are free functions. The Kadjar was serialised before and after and is byte-identical; a fourth car copies none of it. (2) **A van's flanks LEAN above the belt, and `prism` cannot draw a lean**: its walls are vertical and only its caps slope. A wall whose top is scaled in x stays FLAT only where its two ends share an x or a y, so the three upper slices (the cab with its glass tiles, the sliding door's slice, the tail's) are hand-built solids on a RECTANGULAR plan with a vertical panel to the belt and a leaning one above — which is why the Trafic's tail corners are square and only its nose is rounded, and why the belt reads as the swage a real Trafic has. The skirts under them build no top and the slices no floor: both would be buried faces. (3) **The solar panel is a new gear flag, `roofSolar`**, not the Prado's `solar`: that one rides in the basket and `effectiveGear` ties it to `rack`; a van's is a frame standing on the roof itself, a prism whose bottom is not built, the panel a decal inside its top. On by default — it is why the van is here. (4) **The roof is cut at the cab's back and the sliding door's rear edge**, so no roof polygon is 4 m long; the seams read as the pressed ribs a Trafic's roof really has. **And what lies ON the roof is cut where the roof is**: one 1.6 m frame over the cab's roof and the sliding door's was a solid keyed by its far end exactly like a bonnet — nose toward the camera, the cab's roof was laid over it and the panel kept 79% of itself, the worst view 2.5% against the 3% gate. Cut at the cab's back (two prisms with no wall at the cut, two decals meeting there under the frame's ink) the worst view is 0.13% and no part loses a sample. The Kadjar's roof bars are short enough to lie within one roof face; the rule only shows on something long. (5) Measured against the oracle as the Kadjar's rules predicted; the Prado's worst is 1.9% / 54%. Colours: Renault's range as remembered, the French name as the note, white as photographed (`#f0f0ec`). (6) Verified: the gate, every spec, renders at twelve poses through the real `renderOrder` + lighting written out as SVG and screenshotted in headless Chromium (recipe: a `vite-node` script in the session's scratchpad — the turntable's own maths with polygons instead of a canvas). Not on his phone, not in the garage's React panel here, not against the photographs beyond the eye.

**A fourth car: the Zoé (2026-10-07).** The second half of the same ask: *« Renault Zoé blanche classique »* (`hooks/zoe-model.ts`, id `zoe-ph2` — the 2019–2024 phase, white, mirrors its one switch). What the code does not say: (1) **"classique" was read as the car AS IT COMES** — Glacier white, no roof gear, the five-spoke alloy a Zen wears — not a trim name; no photograph was given, so the proportions are Renault's figures (4.09 × 1.79 × 1.56, a 2.59 wheelbase) and the marks are the phase 2's as remembered (slim lamps with the C of the daytime lights, a chrome bar and the big diamond over a grille-less nose, the rear handle hidden in a black C pillar). (2) **The Kadjar's cabin moved into `car-parts.ts` as `greenhouse`** (a `SideLean`, a profile, the right flank's tiles; roof and screens across; the floor kept, as the Kadjar had it) the day a second hatchback wanted it; the Kadjar is byte-identical over it, checked the same way as for `makeBody`. (3) **A steep hatch meets the window line AHEAD of the C pillar's foot**: the Zoé's rear glass climbs more than it reaches back, so `cTop` (where the screen meets `WINDOW_TOP`) lies at y ≈ −1.35 while the pillar's foot is at −1.52; a tile with a top corner at −1.44 on the window line stood 6 cm outside the screen's plane and the convexity test refused the cabin. The pillar's top corner IS `cTop`, and the body behind it a triangle down to the belt — a rule for any car whose hatch is steeper than its C pillar is wide. (4) A decal in the BODY's own role draws nothing (no ink, same colour): the Zoé's door crease was tried that way and removed; a crease needs a hull cut, which the shoulder already gives. (5) Measured: worst view 1.33% (the wings over the bonnet straight down at 80°), worst part the front door at 82% — within the gate with no cut added. Verified as the Trafic: the gate, the spec, twelve poses in headless Chromium. Not on his phone, no photograph to compare against.

**Boats, and a vehicle per piece (2026-09-29).** His ask: *« rajoute 2 modèles 3D de bateau : bateau classique pour aller aux Whitsundays, le Viper pour aller voir l'outer reef, et bateau à Bremer Bay pour voir les killer whales »* — three listed, three built. What the code does not say: (1) **"classique" was read as the ORDINARY day boat** in contrast to the Viper, i.e. the motor catamaran most Whitehaven day trips use — not a sailing boat, and not a named vessel (`whitsunday-cruiser`); the Viper is Airlie Beach's jet boat (offshore monohull, Rolls-Royce jets, 36 belted seats, Bait Reef), the Bremer Bay boat Naturaliste Charters' *Alison Maree* (20–23 m catamaran, three viewing decks, a 360° wheelhouse) — facts from web searches, the operators' own sites being blocked here, so **every livery is a guess**, said in the colour note and repaintable. (2) **The vehicle is per PIECE when borrowed**: a boat is a day's excursion inside a Prado trip, so the trip keeps its car and a Virée picks `vehicle` + `vehicleColor` (`vehicleFor`); both are `contentKeys`. The trip garage lists the boats too (a sailing trip may drive one) — the section is still called Car. (3) **Boats taught the paint order three rules the cars had not**, each measured with the oracle and a trace of `settleOverlaps`: a wall STANDING ON a deck leaves deck behind its inner face, and the repair, rightly moving the deck behind the wall there, drags it behind everything else — so a deck stops where its balustrade starts and the balustrade stands beside it (the Alison's aft deck: 18% kept → 96%); a RAIL, a CANOPY or a HULL's deck as long as the boat is keyed by its far end exactly as a bonnet is — cut them at the deck's own breaks (`wallRun`, the two canopy slices, `demihullParts` cut where the bridge deck ends, whose part under the bridge builds no deck); and a seat and its backrest as two blocks sharing a face lost to each other — ONE convex bench from cushion to back (Viper 2.8% → 0.7%). Final worst: cruiser 0.09% / 97%, Viper 0.88% / 90%, Alison 0.82% / 96%. (4) The catamarans are heightened a size, as the cars' wheels are: at true proportions they read as barges from above. (5) Verified: the gate over all five vehicles, renders at garage and map size, a Virée frame per boat with its wake, the panel driven (select, paint saved after the debounce, back to the trip's car), the garage on a boat. Not on his phone, not against photographs of the boats.

**The Solar Whisper (2026-09-29).** His ask: *« un bateau électrique qu'on a pris dans la Daintree pour voir des crocodiles, il était électrique et silencieux »* — identified as the **Solar Whisper** (the Daintree River's only zero-emission boat: a 9 m fibreglass Goodwin longboat, electric outboards, a solar roof charging its batteries, seats along both edges, 24 guests at most, a "croc cam"; operator pages and listings via web search). The beam (2.6 m), the TWO outboards and the white/green livery are guesses, said in the colour note. What the code does not say: (1) **A roof sits FLUSH on its corner posts** — an overhang covers the post's top, `settleOverlaps` rightly puts the post behind the roof there, and doing so lays it under the bench beside it (the aft post kept 44%); flush, the worst part keeps 94% and the worst view is 1.64%. (2) **The foremost post stands aft of where the bow narrows**, or it stands off the deck's edge; a bench is one convex piece FLUSH on the gunwale (no deck behind it), a hair short of the posts. (3) **`VehicleModel.wake` is per boat** (`paintWake`'s `reach` scales arm length AND strength): 1.5 for the Viper's jets, 0.35 for a boat whose whole point is that it glides silently — a test pins it as the least. (4) Verified: the gate over all six vehicles, renders at garage and map size. Not on his phone, not against a photograph.

**The word is VEHICLE (2026-10-09).** His ask: *« un peu partout dans le projet, on a une notion de voiture … j'aimerais qu'on la corrige pour que ça soit plutôt un véhicule »*. Decision: the concept is called a vehicle in the code and on screen — `vehicle-spec.ts` (`VehicleSpec`, `VehicleModelId`, `VehicleLine`, `VehicleGear`, `VEHICLE_LINES`, `defaultVehicleSpec`…), `vehicle-registry.ts` (`VehicleModel`, `VEHICLE_MODELS`, `vehicleModel()`), `GaragePanel` / `GarageModal` / `VehicleTurntable`, `HookContext.vehicle`, `HookPanelHost.configureVehicle`, the trip settings' *Vehicle* section and every generic «the car» in a panel's words. What deliberately still says car: `car-model.ts` (it IS the Prado) and `car-parts.ts` (a car's parts), the Vitrine's internals (it stages cars only), the boarding rider (a car drives aboard), the ids, and two STORED keys — `DriveOptions.carSize` (renaming a hook option needs a migration for nothing) and `TripDoc.car` until the fleet replaces it in one migration. **How to apply**: a new mover is a `VehicleModel` with a `kind`; write «vehicle» unless the sentence is about wheels or a road.

**The trip's FLEET, with dated changes (2026-10-09, v31).** His two asks: *« assigner un véhicule sur une première étape du voyage et un autre sur d'autres étapes »* and *« le Prado était vert avant, et c'est en arrivant à Melbourne qu'on l'a repeint en Raptor Black Matte »* (`vehicle-fleet.ts`, `FleetPanel.tsx`). What the code does not say: (1) **`TripDoc.car` became `TripDoc.vehicles`** in ONE migration (v31) with the rename's stored half, so the word changed once: the car is the main vehicle, no change, and `car` is DELETED. The house style keeps its file key `car` for the main vehicle AS IT SET OFF — a second vehicle or a repaint belongs to one journey and is left out (tested). (2) **A change is a whole LOOK dated by a DAY**, never a patch and never a model: reading one needs nothing before it, the trip is tracked by calendar day like every reader (a piece's date, the recap's clock), and another model is another vehicle. The place is the author's words beside it; naming a place the trip holds with a known day writes that day (`placeDay`: the place's `arrived`, else its stage's start). (3) **Every reader is on the piece's day**: `HookContext.vehicle` is the main vehicle as it was on `post.date`, so a Vitrine or a Virée dated before Melbourne draws the green Prado with no option; the garage opened from a piece dresses the state IN EFFECT that day (`withLookOn`) and says which. (4) `TripStage.vehicle` and `TripPlace.arriveBy` (a `VehicleRef`: one of the fleet, or a model borrowed in a paint) and `TripDoc.crossings` (the water rule) are stored from v31 — OFF on a migrated trip, on for a new one — and read by the vehicle plan (`vehicle-plan.ts`). (5) `mainVehicle` tolerates a missing fleet: tests and hand-built contexts cast a partial `TripDoc`. (6) Verified: the panel mounted headless — a change added, «Melbourne» typed set 2025-04-14 from the place, Raptor black matte landed on the change only, a Trafic added; not in the real settings sheet, not on his phone.

**The road CHANGES vehicle, by itself on water (2026-10-09).** His ask: *« quand je survole l'eau, automatiquement, passer en mode bateau … sinon une interface pour assigner un véhicule sur certains tronçons »* (`map/terrain.ts`, `map/load-terrain.ts`, `hooks/vehicle-plan.ts`). What the code does not say: (1) **The chain is piece › place › water › stage › trip** (`driveVehicles`), the look read on the MOMENT's day (`momentDay`: the recap's clock, else the stop — on the road the day BEFORE the next arrival, or a change dated the day it reached Melbourne starts as it leaves Geelong: measured, fixed). A piece's own `vehicle` option still forces its whole road; `'trip'` now means FOLLOW the trip. (2) **The water rule is «does the hop change land?»**, never «is there water under the line»: the drawn curve cuts bays (1 276 km of the Bight under Esperance → Adelaide) — with ONE refinement found by measuring: **Natural Earth draws Africa and Eurasia as ONE polygon**, so Algeciras → Tanger Med read as a bay; on one land, water is a STRAIT when the coast between its shores is over `STRAIT_RATIO` (5) times its width. Measured on the lab's whole Australian road: the Bight, Port Phillip, Hervey Bay → Airlie stay roads; Bass Strait, K'gari, Whitehaven, Bremer Canyon sail; Port Lincoln → Adelaide sails the gulfs (a ferry exists, and so does the road: set Adelaide's *arrived by* to undo it). The Daintree is land at 1:50m — manual. (3) **Cost**: a land test reads a latitude BAND of each ring's edges (Eurasia's ring has thousands); a hop's terrain is cached per coastline by every stop's position (the projection fits them all), because `prepare` runs per slide; Paris → … → Sète measured 49 ms cold, 0.2 ms warm, the index 50–70 ms once. (4) **The coastline is loaded like the towns**: `landIfLoaded()` in `hookContextFor`, the editor asking for it only when a drive runs under the rule (`PostEditor`), the road driven whole until it lands — an export from a surface that never loaded it drives whole too. (5) **Changes are found ONCE on the timeline** (`driveRoad`, 1/30 s steps) — an export frame is painted alone, so the paint never compares with a previous frame; a swap throws rings, a repaint a disc of the new paint (`paintChange`), and a boat taken mid-run gets its wake from the swap. A ferry at an automatic crossing plays the boarding since the same day — «The car DOCKS at a ferry crossing» below. (6) Verified: terrain on the real coastline (tests), the plan on two synthetic islands (tests), Virée frames headless over Geelong → Melbourne → Devonport → Hobart with the Prado repainted at Melbourne and the Spirit across; not on his phone, not in the real editor.

**Where a vehicle is CHOSEN (2026-10-09).** Three controls, one per rung of the chain, each where its subject already lives — never a fleet screen that edits stages: (1) the STAGE's card holds a vehicle select beside its place style (`StageCard`), its empty option naming what it inherits (*Main vehicle · Prado*); (2) a PLACE's fields hold **Reached by** (`PlaceFields`), the hop that LEADS to it, its empty option saying *The road decides · a boat on water* or *The stage's vehicle* by the trip's rule; (3) the WATER RULE is the head of the trip settings' Vehicle section (`CrossingsPanel`): the switch and the boat on top, the bridge and shore distances folded under *Fine settings* because they correct the 1:50m map, not the trip, and every why behind an ⓘ (`FoldHints`). One select serves both refs (`VehicleRefSelect`): the fleet first (the main one marked), then every model *Borrowed for the day*; a fleet id the trip no longer holds stays listed as gone rather than silently showing another vehicle. A borrowed model carries its paint (`{borrow, color}`, 2026-10-09, his «ajoute le contrôle de peinture sur les étapes et les lieux»): the model's named colours and a colour of its own are drawn beside the select (`VehicleRefSelect`), the model's own colour stored as NO colour, another model dropping the paint; a fleet vehicle shows none, being dressed in the garage. Verified: the three controls driven headless in a harness (the refs written, the fine settings), the settings sheet rendered at 1280 and 390; not in the real editor, not on his phone.

**The car DOCKS at a ferry crossing (2026-10-09).** His ask: *« fais le passage en ferry automatique avec l'embarquement »* (`ferryDocks` and `RoadCrossing` in `vehicle-plan.ts`, `buildSchedule`'s rests, `crossingAt` / `dockReach` / `riderBlend` in `boarding.ts`, `dockedS` in `drive-paint.ts`). What the code does not say: (1) **A ferry stretch remembers the car it carries** (`VehicleSegment.rider`: what the hop would drive without it, only when that is a CAR and the boat `carries`), and **the car docks at both ends of it** — except at a seam with another riding ferry, so it never drives off one ship and onto the next at one quay; touching riding ferries are ONE crossing. (2) **The docks are in the SCHEDULE, never only in the paint**: the plan takes them through a callback (`drivePlan`'s `docksOf`, called on the plan's own path — the shores are found on the path it builds) and `buildSchedule` cuts the road at RESTS (a stop it halts/stays/docks at, a shore it docks at), each run between two rests eased on its own. A shore dock is a `dock` phase (stop −1, `moment.at` null), a dock at a stop goes off-the-ferry BEFORE the halt and aboard AFTER the stay, and the road's two ends are the hold's and the arrival's beats exactly as on a borrowed ferry. The road time is untouched; each beat adds `BOARD_SECONDS` / `ALIGHT_SECONDS`. Every reader builds the plan through ONE `planOf` (panel, `wantsBasemap`, `prepare`) or the camera track and the stage would time two drives. (3) **Which vehicle a moment draws is read on the phase's side of a boundary** (`lookupS`): a run takes its own stretch, a boarding beat the ferry ahead, an alighting one the ferry it came by, a halt after an alighting the car — `segmentAt`'s «the stretch arriving» alone drew the car through the boarding. (4) **The ship docks OFF the shore** in the middle of the road: its centre `dockReach` ahead (stern ramp's foot where the car stopped) and behind at the far side, slid between the two over the crossing (`dockedS`) — in plan units per FRAME, since the ship's drawn length is pixels and the camera zooms; where the crossing is shorter than the ship it holds still at the middle. (5) **The car changes size on the quay, never in a jump**: the first 35 % of the boarding beat blends the road's full-size car into the rider (place, heading, size), the last quarter of the alighting blends it back, so the road drives on with the very car it left with (`riderBlend`); at the road's own ends it stays a rider, as on a borrowed ferry. (6) A ferry waiting at the quay leaves no wake. (7) Verified: the schedule, the plan's docks and the boarding helpers by tests; Virée frames headless over Geelong → Melbourne → Devonport → Hobart (the water rule's Spirit, follow and whole cameras) and with Devonport reached by the Spirit (docks on stops, the Prado repainted at Melbourne boarding black); not in the real editor, on his phone, nor the clip export encoded.

**Ferries, and the car ABOARD (2026-10-09).** His ask, answering the vehicle lab (https://claude.ai/artifact/2vPp5snYLh1q8JHSGmP5D9): *« fais un Spirit of Tasmania et aussi un mode où notre véhicule monte dedans le temps du passage … il monte dedans et disparaît … ensuite on voit le ferry comme un bateau régulier … rajoute aussi un ferry type France–Maroc ou France–Espagne »* (`ferry-parts.ts`, `spirit-of-tasmania-model.ts`, `med-ferry-model.ts`, `boarding.ts`). What the code does not say: (1) **The Spirit is I/II as they sailed his trip** — 194.3 × 25 m, the operator's figures (the IV, 212 × 31, enters service Oct 2026); its livery is the operator's one word, red, drawn as a band and a funnel, the "red curves" not drawn. The Mediterranean ferry is NO ship: painted like the one of his Tanger Med photograph (navy hull, white superstructure), two funnels side by side so the two never read alike — a guess, said in the colour note. (2) **Both are drawn a size wide** (30 and 32 m): at true slenderness a ship is a stick at map size and the car aboard is as wide as its hull — the catamarans' rule. (3) **A ship is drawn bigger than a car** (`VehicleModel.mapScale` 2.2; every other vehicle 1) and the boarding car at `RIDER_SCALE` 0.42 of a car — a cartoon ratio, the true one (forty Prados) leaves the car a speck; the stern door is 70 % of the beam so the car visibly fits. (4) **`VehicleLine.carries` is the document-side ferry flag** (`carriesVehicles`, pure — no 3D import), the first piece of a vehicle's kind living in `vehicle-spec.ts`; `kind` itself is still the registry's (the rename the lab proposes moves it). (5) **The car is a RIDER, never in the ship's place**: placed through the ship's own pose along its centre line (`riderTrack`, in ship metres), so it follows the turn and the tilt exactly; painted BEFORE the ship, which covers it as it goes in, except out past an end of the hull and nearer the camera than that end. Never sorted with the ship's faces: a car inside a hull is two interpenetrating solids, which no painter's order can settle. (6) **Two beats of their own and nothing else moves**: `BOARD_SECONDS` at the END of the hold (after a badge's lead), `ALIGHT_SECONDS` at the start of the arrival; the runs are the same length, shifted. The panel, `prepare` and `wantsBasemap` all read ONE `boardsOf` (a borrowed ferry, a trip car that is a car, the toggle) — a plan timed without it would disagree with the camera track the shell baked. (7) **A ship's superstructure is as wide as its hull**, so its walls continue the hull's and no strip of deck lies behind them (the boats' wall rule); the hull is cut into slices (`hullSlices`) with no deck under the superstructure, a tier's roof is built only where nothing stands, the funnels stand on a short roof slab of their own, and the bridge is split where the wings press against it. Oracle: Spirit 0.00 % / worst part 100 %, Mediterranean 0.04 % / 100 %. The face-count floor (> 30) failed from dead astern and ahead until real details went in — winches, a foremast, a stern rail, aft-facing windows. (8) Verified: every gate, Virée frames painted headless (waiting at the quay, entering, crossing, driving off) from a Vite page in the session's scratchpad, the panel mounted standalone; NOT in the real editor, on his phone, against a photograph, nor the clip export encoded.

**Undo and redo run over the TRIP, from the tool's one funnel (2026-09-15).** `RoadTripTool` passes its open trip to `useHistory` (`shared/history/`, the engine and its rules are in `architecture.md`), so every edit that reaches `handleChange` — a badge dragged, a leg resized or renamed, a place reordered, a piece added or deleted, a grade, the cover picked from the gallery — is a step, and nothing in the panels had to learn about it. The buttons sit in the `headerExtra` slot beside the sync pill, on the overview and in the piece editor both. What the code does not say: (1) **the label is the screen you are on** (`post:<id>` on a piece, `trip` elsewhere), which is what stops a slider on one piece from merging with the trip-wide change made a moment later; (2) the sync machine's `onReplace` **resets** the history — the instance's copy is not an edit, and undoing back onto what it replaced would push the losing version straight up again; (3) a restored trip goes back out through `handleChange`, so it saves locally and is pushed on idle exactly like any edit — an undo is an edit, not a rollback; (4) **what a step cannot carry is what the document does not hold**: deleting a piece prunes its baked thumbnail (`TripOverview`), so undoing brings the piece back without one until it is opened, and the develop sheet's own draft (written on Done) has its own reset, not this. Verified in headless Chromium against the IndexedDB document, not the screen: a leg and a piece added then stepped back one at a time and forward again, the same by ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z after navigating from the piece back to the overview, and a press inside a text field taking the field's own letters back while the trip stood still.

## The hook's calendar is kept while its own piece changes (2026-09-28)

`hookCalendar` — what every opener reads of the trip — was rebuilt through `tripCoverage` (~1.5 ms on a 300-piece trip, measured) about three times per pointer move of a badge drag. It reads only the OTHER pieces, the legs and the trip's two dates, and the piece being edited is exactly the one it leaves out, so it is now kept (16 entries, by the piece left out) and served while those are the same OBJECTS. That rests on the rule the whole tool already follows: **a trip is updated immutably** — an untouched piece keeps its identity, a touched one never does. Mutating a piece in place (only a test ever did) would serve a stale calendar. `prepare()` itself was measured at 2–13 µs per opener and left alone.

## The piece's export wears Develop's grammar (2026-09-29, his pick from the Trips lab)

- **The piece is taken AT THE CLICK.** Every export verb freezes the looks
  before its first `await` (`shared/roadtrip/frozen-looks.ts`, tested): the
  documents were already the click's render, but `lutFor` / `filmFor` answer
  from the LIVE grade stack, so a look nudged while a reel encoded reached the
  slides not yet rendered. Each slide and each collage cell (with its own
  develop, the way the renderers ask) is answered once; an unforeseen
  question falls back to live rather than to no look. Without this the lock
  below would lie.
- **Slides leave in DECK ORDER, one at a time** — a still through
  `renderDeck` with `include` on its one position, a clip through
  `renderSlideVideo` — where it used to render every still, then encode the
  clips. A still costs one decode either way; what it buys is a run that can
  be followed left to right on the deck strip.
- **The verbs are PINNED** (`DeliverBar`, `placement` `panel` docked, `sheet`
  or `drawer` on a phone): the piece is the primary; the menu holds every
  slide as a still (the old "As images" SWITCH, which the header's Export
  never read — a verb cannot be missed), the open slide as a PNG
  (`exportDeck(position)`) and the hook as a video. The Export tab keeps what
  is SET (the plan, Delivers, the transcode offer, the Studio link); the plan
  is computed by `PostEditor` once, for the tab and the bar alike. The header's
  `Export` stays as a shortcut to the same primary.
- **The run is seen slide by slide** (V1 + V4, the shared model in
  `shared/tasks/run-progress.ts`, `tasks.md`): one unit per slide keyed by its
  deck POSITION (the id `DeckStrip`'s `runAt` looks up), phases Render → Write
  for a still, Encode → Write for a clip (the encode carries its measured
  ratio). The bar is drawn on EVERY tab while `exports.run` is set; on a phone
  it rides the open sheet or drawer, and a closed panel leaves the header's
  fill, the deck marks and the masthead pill. The header's fill is
  `runFraction` — stages, never time. Each file is written by `writeOne` as
  it is made (no reel held in memory, "done" means on disk), and the closing
  note counts written / failed-to-write / could-not-render.
- **L2 — the Export tab and the deck ORDER are the run's; retouching is
  free.** The tab sits under `LockSections` + `RunLockNotice` and shows the
  plan held at the run's start (the live one would already list a still
  turned into a clip); `addSlide` / `removeSlide` / `moveSlideTo` / the
  closing-card switch refuse while `exports.run` is set, and `DeckStrip`
  greys them (`orderLocked`) — a unit is a slide by POSITION, so a move
  mid-run would rename every file after it. What changed under the run is
  named first in the closing note (`shared/roadtrip/run-edits.ts`, tested):
  a slide by its `deckSlides` entry, the hook also by its whole badge, and
  "the look" when what dresses every slide moved (both grades, the theme,
  the badge words, the camera names, the closing card).

## The gazetteer is read in a worker (2026-10-06, P5b of `docs/develop-performance.md`)

Audit PERF-06: the city index (135 233 rows, 6.5 MB) was fetched, parsed
and its towns sorted on the main thread the first time a map opened —
364 ms of frozen thread measured headless, a visible freeze on a phone.
Rules:

- **`gazetteer-worker.ts` fetches, parses and ORDERS; `load-gazetteer.ts`
  starts it once per session and ends it the moment the answer lands**, so
  the JSON text and its rows never touch the main heap. The list crosses
  back by structured clone (the ~130 ms that remain on the main thread) and
  the towns' order as a transferred `Uint32Array` (`townOrder`, pure,
  specced: indices into the list, suburbs left out; `townsFromOrder` reads
  it back as the cities themselves — a `GazetteerCity` IS a `Town`). The
  sort never runs on the main thread again (156 → 0 ms).
- **`loadGazetteer()` keeps its signature** (the deduction, the locate verb,
  the export's place name read it unchanged); **`loadTowns()` is the maps'
  shared read** — `TripMapView` and `StopsMapSheet` each had a private
  promise and their own sort. No `Worker` (a test, an old engine, a worker
  refused to start) runs the same work in this thread.
- The page's ABSOLUTE url crosses to the worker: a relative `BASE_URL` would
  resolve under the worker's script, not the page.
- Next, on a measurement on his phone only: a COLUMNAR index (names as one
  array, numbers as typed arrays, transferred) takes the clone to ~0 and
  needs `nearestCity` and the maps to read columns.

## A MAP view of the overview — BUILT 2026-09-29

**His ask**: *«in trips i would like a map view, have a way to toggle between calendar and map view»*. Answered with a lab and no code: https://claude.ai/artifact/7PbFSMbgWxAEZRXnhpVx5e (a working copy of the overview at wide and phone sizes, on an invented 345-day Australia trip). **The proposal, so a later session builds this design and does not redraw it:** (1) ONE `Calendar · Map` segmented control, first in the trip bar, beside `Rungs · Pictures`. The fill switch keeps its meaning in both views: rungs become a dial per stage, pictures become the stage's latest hook as a tile. The view is a `localStorage` preference (`atelier.roadtrip.overview.view`), never stored on `TripDoc` (the Cards/Bands rule). On a phone the bar is already full, so the switch replaces the told/total chip, and that figure moves into the mono summary line. (2) **The switch swaps the MIDDLE only.** The trip bar, year map, day panel or strip, stage card and sheets stay, so the open day and stage carry over. In map view the year map frames the open stage, and dragging along it walks the days while the map follows. (3) **The map's unit is the STAGE, never the day**, because a place carries no dates («A stage is a LEG» above). Each stage wears a DIAL of its days on the rung ramp, one tick a day clockwise from 12, placed on its place or halfway along its path. In the lab, a tap on a stage opens it and moves the open day to its first day (his Q1). (4) The route runs in lived order. A stage's places are joined in its tint (drawn a deeper twin of `STAGE_TINTS` for contrast), and stages are joined by a road that is solid up to the open stage and pale after it (the Itinerary's «whole path, filled in» rule). A road is DOTTED where days are unaccounted for: days in no stage, or a stage whose place has no coords. (5) What cannot be placed is COUNTED in a corner tray with its verb (Locate… / Cover…), never guessed (Virée's rule). Two stages at one place are pushed apart and tied to it by a hairline. (6) The map is offline by default: MapLibre as in `StopsMapSheet`, with the gazetteer towns as the basemap. The lab proposes shipping a coastline (Natural Earth 1:50m land, public domain, ~150 kB, generated by a `gen-*.mjs` script), because towns alone read as a scatter of dots. OSM tiles stay the existing opt-in. An optional «Where the pictures were» layer reads `/api/assets/geo?by=day` (Deduce's route) once and stores nothing; it gives the only honest «you are here» point. **Five questions are his** (listed in the lab): the day after a stage tap, the coastline, where the instance layer lives, preference or route, and whether the replay stays. **Plan in commits**: pure `trip-map.ts` + tests → `TripMapView.tsx` on the wide screen → the switch + year-map framing → the phone (stage bar with its days, layers in a sheet) → coastline (optional) → instance layer (optional).

**His answer, the same day: *«go ahead and build it, with the coastline»*** — so Q2 is answered (a coastline ships, `deployment.md`) and the other four are taken as the lab built them. **The arithmetic is `shared/roadtrip/trip-map.ts`** (pure): `tripMap` lists the stages in lived order with their LOCATED places, an anchor (`pathMidpoint`, measured on a flat map scaled by the path's own latitude so it lands on the line), their days, the roads between located stages with `dotted` set exactly where an unplaced stage or an uncovered run lies between, and `offMap` (unplaced stages, `rulerGaps`); a place typed by hand is kept as a place and simply not drawn; `progressIndex` resolves a travel day to the LATER stage like `stageAt`; `spreadAnchors` splits two stages at one place sideways, the later to the right; `placeLabels` tries right, left, above, below and drops a name with no room unless it is forced. **Trap met writing it**: excluding «the mark's own box» by comparing box centres to the mark also excluded ANY box centred there — a wall covering the whole view let a label through. Spots start past the mark's radius, so no exclusion is needed at all.

**Built the same day** (`TripMapView.tsx`, `StageDays.tsx`, `MapStageBar.tsx`, the switch in `TripOverview.tsx`). What the code does not say: (1) **The switch swaps the middle and nothing else** — on a wide screen the map takes the calendar's place and the ruler's (`StagesPanel` is not drawn in map view: its `+ Stage` and `Deduce` wait on the calendar side), and the aside (day panel, then the open stage's days as a Monday-first grid, then its `StageCard`) is drawn at EVERY wide width in map view, because a map has no minimum width to defend the way a row of month blocks does. On a phone the told/total chip leaves the bar for the switch and joins the mono line. (2) **The year map frames the open day's WEEK, not the stage** (`veil={false}` so the rest of the year is not washed out; the stage is its accent `LoupeBar`): a frame as wide as the stage changed width under the finger whenever the drag crossed into another stage, and the grab offset the band keeps went wrong. Dragging walks the day a week at a time keeping its weekday, and the route is REPLACED within 600 ms of the last step — ONE history step per drag (`navigate(…, { replace })`, threaded as `onSelectDate(date, options)` → `go(date, null, options)`). (3) **Everything that opens a stage moves the day into it** — a tap on a dial, its name or its path, *Locate…*, *Cover…*, the arrows: opened without moving the day, the aside drew one stage's days above another stage's card. (4) **The map is a picture with fixed colours** (the picking map's rule): its paper, ink and the heatmap's LIGHT ramp are constants, so a dial reads the same at night; the chrome over it (legend, tray, controls) follows the theme. The land is a MapLibre fill + line, towns a circle layer fed per `moveend`; roads, stage paths, dials, leaders and every name are ONE SVG redrawn per frame inside the canvas container (`frontend.md`); a tile in the pictures view is an HTML button in the same layer; stages are parted by what they draw (a tile is wider than a dial). The dial's ticks take a day's click only when a tick is ≥ 5 px and the dial ≥ 14 px — below that a tap is the stage's. Adjusting a leg's dates is the calendar's own gesture, so *Adjust on the calendar* switches the view back. **Not built, deliberately**: «Where the pictures were» (Q3 unanswered — it would be a new read of the instance) and the replay (Q5). **Verified** in headless Chromium at 1400, 1000 and 390 (touch), light and dark, on a seeded 345-day trip: a dial tap opens Uluru on its first day, the stage's grid and the arrows move the day, a hover card per dial, a year-map drag of eleven weeks adds ONE history entry, the switch round-trips keeping the day and is remembered, 17 hook tiles in the pictures view, *Locate…* opens the Gibb River Road's card, *Cover…* makes stage 33 over the four days and opens it; 0 px overflow, no console error. Not on his phone.

**«Not on the map» PLACES what it can (2026-10-07, his «à quoi sert cette section… Newcastle est déjà visible… Locate ne montre pas la même étape», then «A + B»).** The confusion had two roots: the grey «Newcastle» on the map is the GAZETTEER's town label, not his place (a timeline import writes a place's NAME with no coords), and *Locate…* only opened the stage card. Now each row says what it lacks (`no place yet` · `no position` · `found: Newcastle, NSW`) on its own wrapping line, and its verb fixes it: **Place it** writes the town the shipped index answers for (`place-locate.ts`, pure: `lookupTrip` → `indexAnswer` → `applyIndexPicks`), **Place all N** writes every sure answer in ONE `setStages` (one ⌘Z), **Fix…** opens the stage card on that place's Fix panel («Same name» tab when it has a name), **Add a place…** opens it on a new place. **Sure means**: towns of the place's country else the trip's (never across a border — Exmouth/Devon), of the state it names (by name or official code), a suburb (`section`) set aside for the town, then ONE left, or the nearest within 300 km of the stages AROUND it (its own located places, else the last located place before and the first after in lived order, else the trip's centroid) with the next ≥ 3× as far; else Fix. The place keeps its id, dates and writing (`replacePlace`, `source: 'search'`). The card is asked to open a place through a module store (`place-request.ts`: `requestPlace` → `usePlaceRequest(stageId)` / `takePlaceRequest` in `PlacesEditor`), because the card mounts AFTER the stage is opened — a prop would have to be cleared by hand. The index is read only while a named, unlocated place is listed (`gazetteerOrEmpty`, the map's own cache). A single Place it opens the stage the way a dial tap does (day moved in, the phone's sheet closed). **Test trap**: driving a trip opened at `#/roadtrip/<raw id>` makes the first day navigation RELOAD the stored copy (the route's ref becomes `tripRef`), wiping an edit still in the 800 ms save debounce — open a seeded trip at `tripRef(trip)`; the same reload follows a rename (pre-existing, not fixed here). Driven headless at 1440 and 390 (touch): Place all + ⌘Z + Place it, Fix… on Richmond (three states), Add a place… on an unnamed stage.

## 2026-10-09 — A trip is CLONED from its card: name and destination in one small sheet (his pick, variant A of the lab)

**Decision (maintainer, from a four-variant lab — modal · one tap per destination · on the card · destination list — he took A, the mini-modal).** `Clone…` in the card's ⋯ (`TripActions`, so Cards, Bands and the resume band all carry it) opens `CloneDocumentModal` (`shared/ui/`, engine-level like `ImportDocumentModal`; a Studio project is the same gesture with other nouns): a NAME prefilled with the first free one in the chosen source and editable, and a *Keep on* choice that is drawn only when `documentSources` has more than one entry. The sheet opens on the original's own source, the case where a suffix is needed. **Naming rule** (`shared/sources/doc-name.ts`, pure, the files' rule from `unique-name.ts` written for names): a name nothing in the destination carries is KEPT (cloning `Australia` onto an instance with no `Australia` yields `Australia`); a taken one drops a clone suffix it wears and takes the first free `(2)`, `(3)`… (a gap is reused); case and edge spaces are ignored. The number is in PARENTHESES on purpose — the maintainer's pick over `Maroc 2` / `Maroc copy` / `Maroc-1`: it is the only style that needs no guessing (`Route 66 2` leaves a reader unsure whether 66 is a clone marker). **A typed name is never rewritten while typing**; the line under the field says what will be saved, and a name already used in the chosen source is numbered at the click by the same rule. A change of destination re-suggests only while the author has not typed.

**How it is built, and the traps.** `cloneTrip` (`clone-trip.ts`, pure) goes through the backup file — `toTripFile` → `tripDocFromFile` — instead of a second serialiser, so a clone is exactly what an import would give (everything but the id, source, timestamps and each piece's Studio project; pictures are found again by hash). Two things the file does not do are done there: **every piece gets a NEW id** (hook thumbnails are keyed by post id and `deleteTrip` prunes the thumbnails of ITS posts — a clone sharing ids would lose its covers the day the original is deleted, and the reverse), with the cover's pins remapped and the thumbnails COPIED under the new ids once the trip exists (`copyThumbs`, local to the device even for a clone kept on an instance, as for any trip); and `cameraNames`, a `TripDoc` field the file does not carry. The spec asserts that every other field equals the original's, so a field forgotten fails there. Writing goes through `createOn` like a creation (where the trip is KEPT first; a refusal leaves nothing and says so). The gallery then draws the clone with the accent and a `new` tag, scrolls it into view (`revealInScroller`, never `scrollIntoView` — lint), and pins a status line (`Cloned as “X” · where` — Open · Undo, gone after 12 s); Undo is the gallery's own delete, remote included. A trip listed only on an instance has its Clone greyed with the reason (open it here first): a clone is made from the copy this device holds. **Shared with the Studio** (2026-10-09, `studio.md`, «A project is CLONED»): the sheet, the naming rule, `ClonedNotice` and `useFreshClone` are `shared/ui/` and `shared/sources/`, a gallery card carries `data-doc-id` so the clone can be scrolled to, and only the document's own copy (`cloneTrip` / `cloneProject`) is per kind. **Not decided, left as built**: the clone starts from the STORED trip, so an edit still inside the 800 ms save debounce of an open trip is not in it; a lab-only idea, the one-click path when a single source exists (variant B's shortcut), was NOT built — the sheet then asks for the name alone.
